Score 9/10 The chapter where Anubis stops being simple.

1.15 You’re My Problem

2,432 words. The schoolyard, a few weeks later, a Friday.

What happens. Kage has gone quiet. Thanatos hasn’t replied. Huoyan orders Orion to talk to him. Orion finds Kage eating cheddar for lipids, and the conversation turns into shoving and an accusation that Orion “cock-blocked” his gear. Orion says it’s Kage’s fault he’s weaker. Kage snaps, and for the first time his telekinesis works: six cement pillars rise and circle him while a seventh lifts him into the air. He seals the exits and calls Orion every epithet of Hermes. He lays glue mines, fakes tears, and wins with a telekinetic barrage of glue balls. He kicks Orion unconscious, cleans the glue off him with telekinesis, carries him to the nurse, and collapses at the door.

Details I caught.

  • “Kage had gone quiet, the same kind of quiet he’d worn all last year. The difference was that he hadn’t stopped training.” Huoyan and Anubis read the silence as a relapse. The reader knows it’s a secret.
  • “Only telekinesis still wouldn’t come, no matter how many glue balls he stared at.” That sets up the payoff in this chapter cleanly.
  • “Vance. Approach.”
  • Huoyan on Abby: “Not sure if it’s a crush or whatever, but she will just affirm him. I need someone who can actually crack him open a bit.”
  • “That boy is gonna die at this rate.” Huoyan caught himself and cleared his throat. That’s a teacher saying the true thing and then hiding it, which is Huoyan’s whole personality.
  • “Play a video game with him or something.” Huoyan brings this up again in S2 Ch2.
  • Orion: “Abby, Rhatella, hell, even Ailani asked me about him once. That freaked me out.” This plants Orion’s feelings for Ailani.
  • Anubis got Orion out of “not one, but two” punishments.
  • Orion breaks the hallway speed rule.
  • The schoolyard has no roof, a lone gazebo, and doors on both sides.
  • Sharp cheddar for lipids. “I really didn’t feel like drinking straight up oil. I’ve done it before. That shit is nasty.”
  • “Go make out with Rhatty-whatever her name is.” Orion: “It’s not even like that between us.”
  • “You’re not even top of the class.”
  • Orion: “It’s not my fault you’re weaker than me; it’s yours. You’re the one who did nothing last year!”
  • “His hair started to flick up.”
  • “Kage was a bit surprised by his own display. This was the first time he’d actually been able to do telekinesis right. He couldn’t let his own shock show, though.” The power works the first time he’s angry and certain. S2 Ch12 explains why that matters.
  • The Hermes epithets: Diaktoros (guide), Khrysorrhapis (golden wand), Angelos (messenger), Polygios, Kyllenios (of Mount Kyllene), and finally Hermes. Kage really knows his mythology. That fits the Prologue’s “sharp enough to ace anything.”
  • Orion: “Ah, so that’s why you called me Mercury before. I am Greek, Kage, not Roman.” It’s ambiguous, in a good way. It could mean Orion has Greek heritage, or that he means he’d be the Greek god, not the Roman one.
  • “This isn’t a movie, asshole! You don’t get to monologue! If I was a villain, you’d already be dead!” He says this two chapters after shaking a villain’s hand.
  • “Turns out we’re falling, Angelos!”
  • Glue mines: compressed balls that explode on contact. “Well, I guess it wouldn’t be ‘pyro,’ but still!”
  • “Awww, Polygios is all gunked up!”
  • Orion’s counter: “Perception and timing… you had let your guard down to brag about your little trick. Thought we weren’t allowed to monologue?” Orion throws Kage’s own rule back at him in the same fight. The book does this callback-inside-a-chapter move more and more as it goes.
  • The tears: “This isn’t fair! I deserve to win!… D-Don’t you think I’m strong…?” Then one laugh. Were the tears fake? The book doesn’t say, and I’d keep it that way. My read is that they were real and he weaponized them anyway.
  • He steps on Orion’s chest, like Severine did to him. “You let your guard down, Kyllenios. Have a good rest.”
  • He kicks Orion in the temple, then “Kage’s grin left his face,” and he gently cleans the glue off him.
  • “It’s always fun going up against you, Hermes…”
  • “His skin burned hotter with every step.” The fever cost from the S1 Ch2 power card, honored at the finish line.

What it’s doing. It’s Kage’s first win, his first telekinesis, and the book’s turn toward darker methods.

What works. This is a great fight. The callbacks inside it (stepping on the chest, “you let your guard down”) show that Kage learns by copying the people who beat him. The ending, where he cleans Orion up and carries him, complicates “we’re not friends” more than any speech could.

What I’d push on. Last time I asked for one line of Kage’s reaction to his own fake-cry trick and the temple kick. On the reread, the book answers it in the right place, which turns out to be the next chapter. In S2 Ch2, “Kage’s eyes went to Orion’s temple, right where his foot had landed. He looked away,” and he mutters, “It doesn’t matter how I won. I still beat you.” In S2 Ch3 he looks at Ailani’s temple “and for a second, all he could see was Orion’s.” Putting the guilt a chapter later is smarter than putting it here, because here he’s still winning. My only remaining note is the grin leaving his face as he cleans Orion up. It’s a lovely beat. One more word of what replaces the grin would make it land.

Score 9.5/10 The best fight in Section 1, and a perfect section ending.

Section 1 overall

Score 9.0/10 (up from 8.8). Section 1 builds the world, the cast, the rivalry, and the temptation, and it plants more than the rest of the book has paid off yet. This draft fixed most of the notes I gave it: the Prologue is tighter, Severine’s voice is consistent, the telekinesis rule is clear, the collapse has warnings, and Thanatos has a better name and a better pause. Its weakness is still a slow middle (Ch7 to Ch9), where the same “we’re not friends” beat plays several times. Its strength is that every major thread in the book starts here.

Chapter by Chapter: Section 2, “Abby Neosame”

2.1 Patient Confidentiality

1,538 words. Flashback: “A year earlier, in only the second week of her first year.”

What happens. Abby, thrilled to be at hero school, hunts down Lucifer because her power shows her “something is zapping your energy from inside.” He assumes it’s blackmail and holds a flame to her face. She explains “patient confidentiality.” He says it’s “something with my enhancement” and she can’t heal it. She promises, three times over, not to tell anyone. Then she asks him point-blank why he’s so mean, and learns he cranks his worst traits “up to eleven” so nobody gets close enough to see. Since he says being alone is what helps, she agrees to leave him alone. When Reina and Elias arrive, he leaves without saying goodbye. Abby: “I’m happy to not be your friend, Lucifer.”

What changed in this draft. The frame I asked for is there, in the first sentence: “A year earlier, in only the second week of her first year.” That’s all it needed. The jump from Kage collapsing at the nurse’s door now reads as a deliberate cut. Lucifer’s defense line is also cleaner: “I…crank my worst traits up to eleven so people don’t want to talk to me.”

Details I caught.

  • “She already had her first patient picked out.” She approaches helping like a mission.
  • The prayer-hands gesture.
  • Lucifer: “Of course there’d be some freak in my class who can see right through me.” Then “I will burn you alive!” That’s the same threat he uses on Kage.
  • “I know nothing is free. What’s your angle?” Lucifer assumes everyone is transactional. Anubis, who later strikes a deal with him, proves him right.
  • “Nurses and doctors have patient confidentiality!”
  • “There is a girl who can see stuff when she wraps her hair around you!” That’s Zulfiya, mentioned a section and a half before her diagnostic power becomes a plot point.
  • “Enhancements shouldn’t feed on their users like that.” It’s a world rule, and Abaddon breaks it.
  • “She’d been told to wait before.” This is the brother, revealed in S2 Ch5.
  • “For the third time, say you won’t tell anyone.”
  • “Why are you so mean?” is a gloriously blunt question.
  • “I…crank my worst traits up to eleven so people don’t want to talk to me. If I make more connections, then more people have a chance to see what’s going on with me. That’s a liability I can’t have.” That’s Lucifer’s whole defense system in one line. Notice the overlap with Thanatos’s warning in S2 Ch7, that the biomass will “amplify what’s already there.” Lucifer turns up his worst traits on purpose. Kage has something inside him doing it for him.
  • “Me and Elias have history. And Reina, she…uhm…” He blushes, then: “DON’T WORRY ABOUT WHY I TALK TO REINA!”
  • Abby: “God chose me to be the one who makes sure everybody’s okay! I don’t know why you were gifted with fire, but I’m sure there’s some divine purpose.”
  • “Is helping others not a desire?”
  • “Since I got here last week.” So Lucifer arrived a week before Abby found him.
  • “I’m happy to not be your friend, Lucifer.” That’s the gentlest version of the book’s “not friends” motif, and the most loving.

What it’s doing. It opens the section named for Abby by showing who she is at full strength, and it plants Lucifer’s secret.

What works. This is one of my favorite chapters. Abby is goodness without being a doormat. She understands Lucifer well enough to give him what he asked for, which is distance. Lucifer’s cruelty is redefined as protection in a single scene.

What I’d push on. The frame is fixed. One thing I’d still look at: Abby’s dialogue is almost all exclamation points (“Lucifer, I have to talk to you! It’s super important!”). That’s her voice, and it should stay bright. But the one quiet line she has, “Is helping others not a desire?”, is the one that stops Lucifer cold. Give her one or two more lines at that volume and her brightness will read as a choice rather than a setting.

Score 9/10 Abby at her best, which makes what’s coming hurt more.

2.2 No More Fighting

2,034 words. Nurse’s office, the same Friday.

What happens. Kage wakes up with Zulfiya’s hair locking the IV into his other arm. Abby is healing Orion. Huoyan arrives smiling, slams their heads into the wall, explains how much he’d hoped they could push each other, and bans them from fighting each other on pain of suspension and expulsion. He also assigns them night patrols together, starting Monday. Zulfiya reports that Kage hit 110°F, and Abby’s halo flickers. As Kage leaves, Abby gives him a gold whistle her late brother made, which only she can hear.

What changed in this draft. The whistle handoff got longer, and every added word earns its place. “It was the same gold he’d seen sparkle at the bottom of her backpack in the training room. Kage reached for it, and his hand stopped halfway. He was scared about taking away a family heirloom, but Abby just kept smiling and pressed it into his palm. Her fingers stayed on it for a moment, and then let go for Kage’s fist to close around it.” Then the chapter’s new last line: “The whistle weighed basically nothing, but her backpack still felt lighter without it.” The grip motif gets its tenderest version. For once, someone else’s hand lets go so his can close, and the person who let go is the one who feels the loss. It’s the best new sentence in Section 2.

Details I caught.

  • “Morning, King.”
  • The IV is in his right arm this time, wrapped in hair. Zulfiya learned.
  • Abby: bruises, strained muscles, joints “in rough shape,” and “a concussion right at the temple.”
  • “We’re really stupid people.” Then: “Kage’s eyes went to Orion’s temple, right where his foot had landed. He looked away and whispered under his breath. ‘It doesn’t matter how I won. I still beat you.’” That’s the guilt for S1 Ch15, delivered as a glance. He’s defending the win to himself.
  • Zulfiya: “Oh, my sovereign! You’re a riot!” Severine said “God, you are a riot!” in S1 Ch8. That’s probably a coincidence, but it’s a nice rhyme between two women who unexpectedly like him.
  • Huoyan “with something very terrifying. A smile.”
  • “Sir, respectfully, I told you something like this would happen.” Orion’s deadpan is great.
  • Kage laughs, so Huoyan shoots a fireball at his chest. Remember that this burns him. In S3 Ch15, Huoyan notices Lucifer’s fire didn’t.
  • Huoyan’s hope: “maybe they could get to Tulivalo and Minkowski’s level… those two could go so far together!” This confession is the heart of the chapter.
  • “Ozul just wouldn’t do it, and Vance’s hands move too fast.”
  • The ban and the night patrols. The ban returns in S4 Ch6 as Kage’s whole reason for wanting Orion in the final.
  • “I never want to agree with Kage about anything, but if I do, you can guarantee that what he’s saying is correct.”
  • Kage rips out a tuft of his own hair. “Abby winced from her corner like it was her own scalp.” She feels other people’s injuries.
  • “Mr. Perception-And-Timing.”
  • Abby: “This is the first time we’ve talked together since you guys’ first fight!”
  • Kage slips: “go back to healing Hermes.” Abby: “Hermes..?” Then, on the way out: “See ya, Hermes!” “This time, it wasn’t a slip.” One line turns a slip into a nickname he’s chosen.
  • 110°F. “Out of the corner of his eye, he saw Abby’s halo flicker. She’d stopped healing Orion and was staring right at him.” Abby sees through people. What did she see in Kage at 110°F? It’s a small beat that could be nothing, or the first time her scan caught something odd about his body before the biomass. See Theories.
  • Kage: “My body is probably built for it, anyway! My power heats me up when I use it, so maybe my DNA is made to handle that.” That’s funny, and it might also be a clue.
  • “Please don’t end up here again, King? I think Huoyan might kill you if you do.”
  • The whistle: “My brother made this whistle before he died. I’m not exactly sure how it works, but I know only I can hear it!” It’s the gold that sparkled in her backpack in S1 Ch5, and the narration now says so directly.
  • “Thanks. I’ll be sure to use it a ton.” He never uses it while she’s alive.
  • Orion: “At this rate, I’m gonna be the one to kill him.” He remembers this line with horror in S2 Ch8.
  • “Then her eyes drifted back to the door.” The chapter ends on Abby looking at the door Kage left through. She’ll walk out into the city after him three days later.

What it’s doing. It closes Section 1’s arc with consequences, gives Kage the book’s central object, and sets up the patrol.

What works. Huoyan’s hidden hope reframes him completely. The whistle handoff is still understated, but now it has hands in it, which is exactly right for an object this important.

What I’d push on. Still the same note. The head-slamming is played for comedy, but it’s a teacher physically assaulting students. The book could let someone (Orion, dryly) note it, or let Huoyan’s guilt about it surface later. In a book this interested in adults misusing kids, it’s worth acknowledging.

Score 9/10 (up from 8.5). The whistle chapter, now with the right last line.

2.3 Arts And Crafts

1,843 words. Training room two’s mirror room, same day.

What happens. Kage wanders into training room two and finds a hidden mirror room where Ailani is dancing. They argue loudly and have a standoff (sigil versus dripping glue) before standing down together. They talk. She admits people talk about him. He apologizes, she agrees to talk again, “but…we aren’t back together yet,” and he loses his mind over “yet.” She shows him a telekinetic crystal butterfly. He tries a glue butterfly, and it splats. She invites him to train with her. He refuses because he doesn’t want to risk hurting her.

Details I caught.

  • Thanatos hasn’t replied. Kage’s texts don’t even show as read. “Did I not do enough?”
  • The mirror room is “clearly made for yoga and ballet.” There’s a small pink speaker. She’s dancing. This is the first clue to her real dream.
  • They’ve both been avoiding room one since the treadmill, and both ended up displaced.
  • “Dull tools only get duller without someone like me to sharpen them!” “Maybe I found someone else to sharpen me!” That’s Thanatos, hidden in an argument.
  • “And you sound just as bitchy!”
  • “Don’t make me slap you again!” So she’s slapped him before.
  • “Crystal girl is gonna shoot her dinky little crystals at me?” (“Dinky” is a better insult than the last draft’s “demure.” It’s the word Kage would actually use.)
  • The sigil: “Kage’s stomach clenched the same way it had in training room one. He knew that sigil. She wasn’t bluffing.” The body memory from S1 Ch3, called back.
  • The mirrors. “Neither of them moved. Glue pattered onto the floor. In every mirror around them, another Kage and another Ailani were squared up. All of them were itching to do something.” That’s a great image. The room multiplies the standoff, and every reflection is one more version of the fight they could have.
  • And then: “Kage’s eyes landed on her temple, and for a second, all he could see was Orion’s.” This is why he stands down. It’s not just that he loves her. He just kicked someone in the temple, and he can’t stop seeing it. It’s the strongest single line in the chapter, and it changes what the standoff means.
  • The standoff: “I see that sigil disappear, and you’ll see my nails stop spewing…” It’s a lovely mutual disarmament.
  • “You match me pretty well, Ms. Shoma.”
  • People who talk about Kage: Huoyan, Orion, Rhatella’s sister (“the big girl in our class,” Ailani’s friend), Zulfiya (“king”), and Abby.
  • “Ailani hesitated, then sat down next to him. Not close, but next to him.” Distance, measured exactly.
  • “I am not mean! You made me act that way.”
  • “Ailani searched Kage’s face for any hint of insincerity and found none.” She reads him like Elias reads liars. File that for S4 Ch6, where she tells him he has “a lot of answering to do.”
  • “You said ‘yet’!” eight times in a row.
  • “You only started training, and that’s it. Other than that, you’re the exact same idiot.”
  • The butterfly: she squeezes a crystal into being, pokes it, the sigil rotates ninety degrees, and the butterfly lifts and flaps. Every enhancement in this book has a tell: Kage’s hair stands up, Ailani’s sigil turns, Rebento’s eyes swirl.
  • Kage’s glue butterfly deforms and falls. “He didn’t understand how he was struggling to do telekinesis after he was doing it fine against Orion.” The book explains why in S2 Ch12: his confidence broke “somewhere around the mirror room.” Read the two lines together and the cause is right here on the page: he stopped being sure the moment he saw Orion’s temple on Ailani.
  • “I’ll find time, or I’ll make time.”
  • “To be honest, I don’t want to fight you.” “I’d be pretty mad at myself if I did end up hurting you.” Ailani: “I’m not a porcelain doll, Kage… if we fought, I’d kick your ass.” After S4 Ch7, Ailani is one win (over Orion) away from facing Kage in the grand final. This is the setup.

What it’s doing. It’s the reconciliation, and it’s built as a fight that turns into a dance.

What works. The argument escalates the way real exes argue: it’s petty, specific, and gets funny right when it’s about to get ugly. The standoff is a great choice. Two people who could hurt each other, deciding at the same moment not to.

What I’d push on. “Arts And Crafts” is a sly title (the butterflies, and later Ailani’s “while you play with your arts and crafts”). But the chapter’s emotional peak, the word “yet,” is still buried in the middle. Ending the chapter closer to “yet” would make the title’s callback land harder later.

Score 9/10 (up from 8.5). The mirrors and the temple turn a good reconciliation scene into a scene about guilt.

2.4 That Didn’t Last Long

1,440 words. Ailani’s dorm, then Lucifer’s, the following Monday.

What happens. After a warm Saturday and a dry Sunday, Kage goes to Ailani’s dorm Monday after class. Lucifer has told her about Kage flirting with Reina. She quotes Kage’s own S1 Ch3 promise back at him, he admits the flirt came after it, and she tells him to lose her number. He sits against her door typing and deleting “sorry.” Then he decides it’s Lucifer’s fault and storms to Lucifer’s dorm. Lucifer and Elias loom. Orion arrives just in time for patrol and pulls him away.

What changed in this draft. The best addition in the chapter is the hinge between the two scenes. Kage slides down her door and “pulled out his phone to text her and typed out ‘sorry,’ then deleted it. He typed it out again. He deleted it again.” Then: “The longer he sat there, though, the less it felt like his fault and the more it felt like Lucifer’s.” That’s an exact picture of how blame moves, and it’s the biomass’s future in miniature. Before anything is inside him, Kage already turns guilt into a target. Elias’s farewell also got sharper: “Kage couldn’t tell if it was a threat or if Elias actually meant it. Somehow, that was worse.”

Details I caught.

  • Still no word from Thanatos or Frostbite.
  • “That looks like the face I make when doing Huoyan’s homework!”
  • “Silly me for having hope.”
  • Lucifer telling her was “one of the few times he ever spoke to me.” So how did Lucifer know they were talking again? They reconciled in a hidden room on Friday. This is never answered. See Continuity.
  • Ailani quotes S1 Ch3 verbatim: “…none of those other ladies could even come close to you. You’re the one I wanna be with, and you always have been.” That’s excellent continuity.
  • “Ailani chuckled, but her jaw was tight and her eyes had gone glassy.” She laughs to keep from crying, which is the same trick Kage uses.
  • “For a second, he thought about lying, but he didn’t. ‘No, Ailani. It was after I had said that.’” He tells the truth. It costs him, and he does it anyway. That counts for something, and the book now shows the lie he didn’t tell.
  • “And, Kage, lose my number.” Paid off with “Keep my number” in S2 Ch14.
  • Lucifer’s tail “began swinging happily.” It’s an honest body part on a dishonest person. The tail is the best tell in the book.
  • “Hey, Min! The ant decided to show up today! Maybe Anubis sent him on a mission to find us!” Elias is in Lucifer’s dorm. Keep that in mind.
  • Lucifer’s insults: “period hair,” “daddy Orion,” “worm,” “pebble,” “little Ozul,” “Brown-nosing Anubis.”
  • “Actions have consequences.”
  • Elias: “Is there an issue, Luci? Do you need some help with this?” He adjusts his glasses.
  • Orion whistles from the end of the hall.
  • When Orion arrives, Lucifer’s “tail stopped swinging and went stiff, but his tone of voice didn’t change.” The tail tells the truth again: Orion’s arrival bothers him far more than his voice lets on.
  • Orion is “only an inch taller” than Lucifer, “but that inch pissed Lucifer off to no end.”
  • Kage whispers that together they could take them. Orion: “It’s idiotic to the highest degree.”
  • “Affirmative,” says Elias, with death glares. Kage has “glue already beading at his fingertips” before Orion covers his mouth.
  • Elias: “I pray you guys come back okay. I would love to continue this conversation together.” “Kage couldn’t tell if it was a threat or if Elias actually meant it. Somehow, that was worse.” Kage remembers it in S2 Ch11: “It was like he knew something bad was going to happen.”
  • “Tell Anubis I say hi… his little scouts.”

What it’s doing. It takes away the romance right before the disaster, so Kage goes into the worst night of his life having just lost Ailani again.

What works. That’s cruel and correct structuring. It also sets up Ailani’s “The last thing I ever said to him was to lose my number…” in S2 Ch8.

What I’d push on. Still open: Lucifer’s motive for telling Ailani, and how he knew they were talking again (“I got wind that you and her were talking again”). His in-story answer (“she deserves transparency”) is obviously a lie. A later beat showing why (Anubis’s attention, jealousy, or something about Ailani specifically) would close the loop. Orion’s guess on patrol (“he just knows Anubis has eyes”) is a good start, but it’s a guess.

Score 8.5/10 (up from 8). The floor drops out before the fall, and the typed-and-deleted “sorry” is the best small moment in the chapter.

2.5 Go Help Someone Else

922 words. Lucifer’s doorway, the same Monday.

What happens. Abby catches Lucifer at his door and begs to help: her power shows the drain is getting worse. He explodes, calls her “a nothing support hero,” tells her “the best doesn’t need support,” and kicks her foot out of the doorway. She cries and remembers her brother: she was told to wait, she waited, and he died. She decides to go help Kage on patrol instead. Inside, Lucifer tells Elias he’s cruel to her because he doesn’t want her hurt. “Angels can’t befriend demons.”

What changed in this draft. Three changes, and together they make the best chapter in the section better.

  1. The brother has an image now. I asked for one concrete detail. You gave me this: “She sat by his bed every night with her hands folded to pray, when she could have laid them on him instead.” That sentence is the whole character. A healer whose hands were folded when they should have been open. It also explains the prayer-hands gesture she makes at Lucifer in S2 Ch1. She’s been making the wrong gesture with those hands her whole life, and she knows it.
  2. The decision has a rule now. “She had waited for her brother. She wasn’t going to wait for a whistle.” That’s the cleanest piece of motivation in the book. It also turns the whistle into the instrument of her death in a way that’s nobody’s fault and everybody’s: Kage didn’t blow it, so she came anyway.
  3. The title line moved to Abby. It used to be “Fine. I’ll help you later, Lucifer. I’ll just go help Kage today.” Now it’s “Fine. I’ll go help someone else today,” Abby said to the door. “But I’ll help you later, Lucifer.” She’s quoting his “go help someone else” back at a closed door, as a promise. It’s a promise she can’t keep, and the chapter title now belongs to her.

Lucifer’s last line also changed. “Our enhancements work perfectly together” and “At the end of the day, angels can’t befriend demons” became: “Reina’s different and you know that. She can take my heat. Abby would just get burned, and she does not work with me or for me. Angels can’t befriend demons.” It’s less quotable and more honest. “She can take my heat” says the same thing about Reina’s power as the old line, and it adds the reason he’s cruel to Abby: he thinks closeness to him burns people. Given what’s inside him, he’s right.

Details I caught.

  • Elias: “Don’t kill anyone while I sleep.” It’s a deadpan joke with a dark second meaning, given what we learn about Abaddon.
  • “My power scans on its own! Every day I have to see you hurting…” Abby can’t turn off seeing. She has to watch.
  • Her “tiny leg trembled before the tall devil.”
  • “We had this exact same conversation last year.”
  • “Just cuz you think your life’s purpose is to help people does not mean we, ugh! It does not mean I require it!” The slip. “We.” Lucifer almost includes Elias.
  • “But, what about Elias..?” “STOP ASKING ABOUT HIM!” Abby’s scan has seen something in Elias too. That could be trauma, or something else.
  • “What happened to you and that bug Kage?! BOTHER HIM!” Lucifer’s insult sends her to her death. He doesn’t know that, and the book is careful not to say it. But it’s there.
  • The brother. “Her brother was incredibly sick, but Abby was too young for doctors to trust her… But she never tried, and that had been killing her ever since.”
  • “A part of her thought that everything with them might be fine since Kage hadn’t blown the whistle all weekend, but another part worried Kage would never blow it, no matter how bad things got.” She reads Kage correctly. Orion says the same thing over her body in S2 Ch6.
  • Lucifer: “I don’t want her hanging around and ending up hurt!”
  • Elias: “You make an exception for Reina.” Lucifer: “She can take my heat.” This is paid off when Reina absorbs his fire in S3 Ch14.
  • “Angels can’t befriend demons.”

What it’s doing. It’s the shortest chapter in the section, and it does the most damage. It gives Abby a reason to walk into the patrol, and it gives Lucifer a guilt he doesn’t know he has yet.

What works. Everything. The brother reveal recolors every earlier Abby scene. “She’d been told to wait before” from S2 Ch1 now means something terrible. And Lucifer’s last line is the best line he has in the book.

What I’d push on. The brother note is answered. The image is about Abby, though, not him. He still has no name, no age, no enhancement, and he built a whistle “only I can hear,” which means he made something for her specifically. One more detail about him (even just his name, said once by Abby) would make the whistle heavier every time Kage touches it.

Score 10/10 (up from 9.5). Short, devastating, perfectly placed, and now the decision to walk into the patrol is airtight.

2.6 Divine Intervention

4,051 words. City streets, Monday night.

What happens. On patrol, Kage complains about not being allowed to fight Orion and about Lucifer. Orion lets slip that Ailani asked about Kage, and blushes. A scream: an official has been murdered outside a government building, and a masked figure on the roof is destroying a camera. The villain, Nerezza, recognizes Kage from Thanatos’s alley. Kage and Orion literally crash into each other trying to flank her. She chokes Kage with shadow tentacles and whispers, “I see why Thanatos likes you.” Orion rallies Kage (“You improvise, I execute!”), and Orion knees her through a window. She comes back and starts shredding Orion. Kage, afraid of exposure, won’t call the teachers or blow the whistle, and texts Thanatos instead. He tells himself he has no other choice. Abby arrives. Nerezza lets Orion break his speed limit to save Abby from one tentacle, then stabs Abby through the chest from her own shadow. Abby tells Kage everything will be alright, says she’ll always be his number one supporter, and detonates in light (“Divine Judgment”). Only her singed backpack remains. Nerezza survives. Thanatos arrives and catches the tentacle meant for Kage.

What changed in this draft. You rewrote the two most important paragraphs in the book, and both are better.

The decision. The old version gave Kage three options and a reason to refuse each. The new one makes him hold two of them in his hands at once. “He stared at his phone still clutched in his hand. Huoyan’s contact was still pulled up, right where he’d left it.” He’d scrolled to it a minute earlier to report the “win.” Help is one tap away, already on the screen. “Orion’s screams grew even louder. His other hand brushed the whistle in his pocket. Abby could help, but no. Not with Nerezza here, and not with what Nerezza knew.” That last clause is the big change. In the old draft he didn’t blow the whistle to protect Abby (“He didn’t want that risk”). Now the reason is the same as the reason he won’t call Huoyan: Nerezza could tell Abby what he is. Every refusal is now about his secret. Then: “He told himself he didn’t have another choice.” “Told himself” is the most damning phrase in the chapter, because the reader just watched him have three.

Abby’s death. “Her enhancement did what it always did and scanned. For once, every number was her own, and Abby knew exactly what they added up to.” Her power has been counting other people’s cuts and bruises since S1 Ch5 (“exactly twenty-seven cuts and fourteen bruises”). Now it counts hers. It’s the best sentence in the book, and I don’t say that lightly.

Two smaller changes. Orion’s limit-break now tops out at 250 mph instead of 300 (“One-fifty, one-seventy-five, two hundred. Then he broke through and went even faster”), and the chapter keeps the number: “Two hundred fifty miles per hour, and it hadn’t mattered. He was now the frozen one.” The old “too scared to move anymore” was a description. “The frozen one” is a reversal: he’s become Abby a minute after saving her. And as Divine Judgment goes off, “Abby was still smiling at him. Then the light swallowed her whole.” She’s smiling at Kage as she dies, which is the last thing she can do for him.

Details I caught.

  • Kage stretches his shirt “on the verge of ripping.” He literally can’t contain himself.
  • “That sneak attack shit is for villains!… show up with a smile… Attacking from behind is so pussy…” That’s ironic coming from a boy who’s spying for villains.
  • Orion: “If you’re doing poorly consistently, then Anubis would eventually stop having high hopes for you.”
  • “You’re not exactly…muscular, Kage.” “I’m just lean.”
  • Orion: “I was pretty excited when she asked to speak to me during off hours, but then, she asked about you. Not fun.” The faintest blush. That’s Orion’s crush on Ailani, and Kage doesn’t notice.
  • “Some sixth sense? Does your broken power do that too?!”
  • Kage’s goal is “number one hero,” but “He hadn’t really set any goalposts.” He has the destination and no route to it.
  • The murder: a man in a suit outside a government building. In Chapter NULL we learn he was pushing the Security Accords, which would put cameras all over the hero city.
  • The camera the masked figure destroys. Anubis later says his “main city drone” was “destroyed beyond repair” that night. Those are almost certainly the same thing.
  • Kage: “I’m just gonna call you Hermes!” Orion: “Hermes…works.” Orion’s real hero name is revealed in S3 Ch6, and it’s Hermes. Kage named him. That’s lovely.
  • “The Z team?”
  • The skull crushed “like a grape.” The book doesn’t flinch.
  • Nerezza “recognized the boy. She had seen him with Thanatos and Frostbite in that alleyway.” That pays off “other presences.”
  • Kage throws off his shoes and socks again.
  • The collision: “WHAT HAPPENED TO ALL THAT ‘perception and timing’ BULLSHIT…” “We haven’t exactly practiced working together.” It’s funny and also true.
  • Nerezza’s card (in case-file style): only twenty miles from where she has walked. Overuse blinds her, locks her hands, fills her mind with pain. “She is the darkness that has taken on a human form.”
  • “I see why Thanatos likes you.” That’s the moment Kage stops caring about the choking.
  • “We’re going to die here…”
  • Orion “took a page out of Kage’s book”: “This isn’t the Sticky Situation I’ve met! Wake up!… You improvise, I execute!”
  • Nerezza: “‘Sticky Situation! No longer bringing smiles!’” She mocks the exact philosophy Kage told Rhatella.
  • Vanity: “How dare you ruin my beautiful face… The only reason you’re still alive is because-!” She’s cut off. Because someone protects him. The boss? See Theories.
  • Kage kisses Orion’s cheek “over and over.” Orion: “No kissing. I do not swing that way, Kage.” “Bold of you to assume I do.”
  • “I’m not sure if I can kill you yet.” So she has orders.
  • Toothed suction cups.
  • The decision. Huoyan or Anubis means exposure. The whistle means Abby, in front of a woman who knows his secret. Thanatos “had said he wouldn’t help Kage again.” He texts Thanatos anyway and “prayed he would arrive.” Every option is a betrayal of something, and he picks the one that protects the secret.
  • Abby “had been walking through the streets of the city since sundown,” calling their names.
  • She freezes because she can see every injury. Her gift overwhelms her.
  • “Run, boy.” Nerezza lets Orion go on purpose.
  • 150, 175, 200, 250 mph. Orion breaks his stated 200 limit by fifty. This is the book’s title idea (“Limitless”) made literal.
  • “Whoops! My finger slipped!” The tentacle comes out of Abby’s own shadow.
  • She can’t heal herself, and she can see her own heart “pushing blood towards organs that were already giving out.” Her power makes her death worse.
  • Her wings, which Kage had never seen, flap instinctively.
  • Orion freezes now: “He was now the frozen one.”
  • Nerezza: “Would a hero let something like this happen?! No… They would’ve traded their life.”
  • “It’s okay, Kage…everything is going to be alright. I came here to support you.” The narration notes they’re the same words from the day they met.
  • “I will fulfill that promise. I will always be your number one supporter, Kage.”
  • “A huge technique Abby called Divine Judgment. One where she would strike down enemies as well as herself.” Kage, who can barely breathe, still “ran to tackle Orion to the ground. He braced the two of them.” His first instinct in the blast is to cover Orion.
  • The backpack: “One of the straps was torn off… singed and scorched.”
  • “We won, Kage. Get up…”
  • “Why would she give that to me if she was gonna show up anyway?!” Orion: “Maybe it’s because she knows you barely accept help from anybody?” That’s the saddest correct answer in the book.
  • He wears the backpack by its one strap.
  • “I can’t believe you’re making me do this, child. I told you I was done saving you.”

What it’s doing. It’s the book’s midpoint, and everything after is a consequence.

What works. Almost everything. The decision Kage makes (Thanatos over the whistle) is the hinge of the whole story, and it’s earned by fifteen chapters of setup. Abby’s death uses every rule you gave her: she sees everything, can’t heal herself, and freezes at the sight of pain. The callback of her first words is devastating. Orion breaking 250 mph to save her, and saving her, and it not mattering, is cruel in the best way.

What I’d push on. Two things. First, the kiss gag between Kage and Orion sits right between “We’re going to die here” and the real horror. It’s a deliberate breather, but it’s the one tonal choice in the chapter I’d reconsider; a smaller beat (Kage hugging him, then catching himself) would keep the relief without a punchline. Second (still open), “Divine Judgment” gets one sentence of explanation as it happens. A single earlier mention, somewhere in Section 1, that Abby has a technique she’s afraid of would make the detonation feel like a choice she always knew she might make.

Score 10/10 Still the best chapter in the book, and the rewrite of the decision makes it more clearly Kage’s fault.

2.7 Fractured Brought Whole Again

4,108 words. The road out of the city, the same night.

What happens. Orion recognizes Shifting Horror. Kage slaps him to keep him from thinking, glues himself to Orion’s back, and they flee at 100 mph while Thanatos and Nerezza flirt. Twenty miles out, a shadow wall. Kage rockets them upward, the wall closes into a dome, and Kage twists in the fall to land under Orion and protect his legs. Nerezza drags Kage out and presents him to Thanatos. Inside the dome, Orion hears Kage and Thanatos talking like partners. Nerezza tells Orion that Kage is a traitor and makes him choose: his life or Kage’s, by the count of five. At “three,” Orion tells her to go after Kage. She sets a game: Kage must dodge two tentacles for 45 seconds, every touched limb gets broken, and any limb touched twice also gets broken on Orion. Kage rockets toward the full moon, whose light weakens her shadows, and falls. Thanatos rules the last touch came after time, so Orion is spared. Nerezza mutilates all four of Kage’s limbs while Orion watches. Thanatos lets Orion run. Then Thanatos rebuilds Kage from his own body, piece by piece, and warns him that the biomass will “amplify what’s already there.” Kage wakes up reaching for Abby’s backpack and the whistle.

What changed in this draft. The most important revision in the whole manuscript is in this chapter, and it’s four sentences long.

Orion’s count. In the last draft, Orion answered at three with a speech that framed his choice as logic: “If you aren’t lying and you will give Kage a chance since I answered so quickly, then this choice is the most logical.” I called that “choosing his own life and telling himself it’s strategy.” The new draft takes the strategy away. At “Two,” “The more Orion listened in, the more it sounded like Kage wanted to help instead of hurt. But a small, ugly part of Orion didn’t care, and it already knew what it wanted to say.” Then at three: “That’s enough counting! I made my choice. Kage, not me.” The logic speech survives in a shorter form, but the narration now tells you it’s a cover: he says it “trying to sound confident.” Orion hears Kage begging for him and chooses himself anyway, and a part of him wanted to. That’s harder to forgive and much better to read. It also makes S2 Ch8’s “A part of him prayed for this” a confession rather than a revelation.

Orion’s exit. “His heart yelled at him to turn around and help Kage, but Kage had already gone quiet, and Orion’s legs never slowed down. He had made his choice at three. Now he was making it again.” “Now he was making it again” is a great line. It means the dome wasn’t one bad second. Running is the same choice, made a second time with no count to blame.

Thanatos’s warning. The old version said the biomass would “amplify your traits, especially the negative ones.” The new version: “This mass inside you will amplify what’s already there. The things you try to suppress and hide will be difficult to hold back now.” That’s a big change, and I think it’s the right one. The old line told the reader the biomass makes Kage worse. The new line says it makes him more. That puts the moral weight back on Kage. When he’s cruel to Rebento in S4 Ch6, the biomass isn’t the excuse anymore. The cruelty was already there. It just got louder. (It also makes Lucifer’s “crank my worst traits up to eleven” from S2 Ch1 into a deliberate echo: Lucifer does by choice what the biomass does to Kage.)

Smaller things I noticed on the reread, all good:

  • The slap gets a motive: “If Orion started asking why Thanatos had saved him, everything would come out, and Kage couldn’t take one more thing breaking tonight. Not with Abby’s backpack still hanging off his arm.”
  • Before the 45-second game, “Kage slid Abby’s dolphin backpack off his arm and set it down on the curb, gently, like it could still get hurt.”
  • The moon strategy is earned on the page: “Abby’s death hadn’t stopped replaying in his head since it happened, and every time, more of it became clear. Nerezza had flinched and covered her eyes before the blast ever hit. Light hurt her.”
  • The last line: “The first thing he reached for was Abby’s backpack with one hand and the whistle in his pocket with the other.” Rebuilt hands, and both of them reach for her.

Details I caught.

  • Orion: “One of the most notorious villains currently on the underworld scene.” He knows exactly who this is.
  • The slap. Kage keeps Orion from analyzing, which proves Kage knows Orion is smart enough to figure it out.
  • “C’mon, Hermes! Mush!!!”
  • 100 mph is Orion’s cap with a passenger.
  • “What an elegant use of an enhancement.” “It may have been attraction.”
  • “I pride myself on killing with grace.”
  • “You are not chaos like that boy, Nerezza. You are artistry.”
  • Twenty miles. She uses her own limit as a wall. “It is causing a bit of a migraine.”
  • “A boy who’s gone through what he has and still smiles is someone very good at being fake. Either that, or he’s just truly that ‘inspired.’” How much does Thanatos know about what Kage has been through? See Theories.
  • “You gorgeous sadist… Damage, though, can be repaired.” It’s a promise and a threat.
  • Orion notices “something different about the way Kage moved… He seemed clearheaded, like he’d decided nobody else was getting hurt tonight.”
  • Kage takes the fall for Orion’s legs. Cracked ribs and a fractured shoulder blade. “If Orion’s legs became damaged, he knew they couldn’t get away.” He’s protecting Orion’s ability to escape, which means protecting Orion over himself.
  • “Sticky. Hey, Sticky. You okay?”
  • “I came bearing gifts… It’s in near perfect condition.” “It.”
  • “Most predators tear their meal to shreds the moment they catch it… You? You deliver it untouched.”
  • Thanatos’s hand on Kage’s shoulder is “the way someone touches something that belongs to them.”
  • The darkness “pulled away from his touch like it knew what he was.”
  • “Monsters don’t fear other monsters. They recognize each other.”
  • “I can’t let someone else die because of me!” He punches the dome with concrete gauntlets.
  • “That boy is neither of our problems.”
  • Inside: “They’re…talking like they know each other.” “They’re arguing like a worker argues with their boss.”
  • “Haven’t you wondered why your teachers haven’t shown up yet? It’s because that backstabber didn’t want me to reveal his villainous activities.” She’s lying about why, but she’s right that Kage didn’t call them.
  • “Let’s cut the cancer off now.”
  • The count. One. Two. Orion hears Kage begging to save him, and “a small, ugly part of Orion didn’t care.” Three: “Kage, not me. Let me out of here and go after him. You said you’d give him a chance if I answered fast, so this is the most logical choice.” He hoped “Nerezza would enjoy his answer coming out at three instead of just before the five count ended.” Even the timing is a calculation to please her.
  • “No! That wasn’t part of the deal!”
  • Thanatos: “I want Kage to have more of an audience than just myself.” “You both can go home and talk about your emotional trauma together.” He pats Orion’s head too.
  • The moon. Kage figured out from replaying Abby’s death that light hurts Nerezza. He used his grief as data.
  • Thin air, cold, lipids gone. An eight-second fall.
  • Thanatos “timed every second.” “That last touch is null and void.” The villain is the referee.
  • “This brat actually protected his little friend.”
  • “Hermes is gonna be okay…”
  • The mutilation is graphic, and the book commits to it.
  • Orion runs: “His heart yelled at him to turn around and help Kage, but Kage had already gone quiet, and Orion’s legs never slowed down.”
  • “He is a fun plaything.”
  • The rebuild. Forearm to ribs. Shoulder to left arm. The migraine brings “bits of the things he’d absorbed over the years… their instincts trying to claw their way out.” Chest to right arm, which Kage’s body rejects twice. Both thighs to the legs, and Thanatos’s cells start trying to take over Kage’s: “No, don’t you dare.”
  • “One hand twitched. Then the other.” Even the rebirth comes back to the hands.
  • Thanatos ends up smaller, thinner, starving.
  • “This mass inside you will amplify what’s already there. The things you try to suppress and hide will be difficult to hold back now.” Also: “You’ll regenerate like never before.”
  • “You’ll work for me from now on.”

What it’s doing. It’s the second half of the midpoint. It breaks Kage, breaks Orion’s self-image, and remakes Kage’s body.

What works. The count to five is the most morally interesting scene in the book. Orion isn’t a coward. He’s a teenager who has just watched someone die, and he takes the logical way out and then has to live with it. The rebuild is written with real physical texture. The cost to Thanatos makes the act ambiguous: he sacrifices for Kage, but the way you’d sacrifice for an investment.

What I’d push on. Still the same small note. The game’s rules (touched once, touched twice) are a lot to track during an action sequence, and I had to reread the ending to confirm which limbs counted. Thanatos’s ruling now reads cleanly (“you grabbed Kage’s right arm after time was up. That last touch is null and void”), but one more clause, “so nothing touched twice,” would settle it for the reader.

Score 10/10 (up from 9.5). The rewrite of the count makes this the moral center of the book, not just of Orion’s arc.

2.8 About Kage…

2,171 words. Ailani’s dorm, Wednesday.

What happens. Orion has spent two days deciding whom to tell. Everyone asks him where Kage is, and he says he doesn’t know. He tells only Ailani: Abby and Kage were “fatally wounded.” He holds back Thanatos, his own choice in the dome, and the fact that Kage was working with villains. He tells her he’ll report it after the tournament. She sobs, shoves him, and then laughs when he does an impression of Kage. They promise to make Kage proud.

What changed in this draft. This chapter gained about 110 words, and the new and sharpened lines are some of the best in the book. They all do the same job: they show Orion lying to himself in real time.

  • “Then he ran, and his legs never slowed down. They had been bouncing under his desk ever since.” The fastest boy in school can’t keep his legs still. That’s guilt as a body tic, and it uses the power.
  • “It was just a few streaks of worry, but they dissipated at the reminder: Kage would’ve wanted this. He told himself it was true, so there was no more need for rumination.” That’s the same “told himself” that damned Kage in S2 Ch6. Both boys now have the phrase.
  • The photo. “Kage’s hand was a blur at the edge of the frame, caught halfway to blocking the lens. Orion approached it and picked it up. He examined it a bit more. In the picture, Kage’s arm was a smear, but whole. Orion’s thumb tightened on the frame.” Two days ago he watched that arm split “straight down the middle.” He’s looking at a photo of a whole arm and can’t put it down. That’s an extraordinary sentence. “But whole” carries the entire previous chapter.
  • The silence: “His enhancement stretched every millisecond of her silence into an eternity, and for once, he couldn’t outrun a single one.” The old version (“Each millisecond began feeling like an eternity”) was good. This one adds the reason it hurts: speed is how he escapes, and here it traps him.
  • “Her eyes went to the photo on the dresser. Orion couldn’t look at it. He was reminded of the last thing he’d said about Kage.”
  • “He loved you. His whole face lit up whenever he talked about you.” Orion meant every word. That didn’t make them any easier to say. He’s telling the girl he likes that she was loved by the boy he let die.
  • “But telling her how they got separated meant telling her who had done the separating.” That’s the trap, in one sentence.
  • “And Abby would’ve wanted…” Orion trailed off. He didn’t know exactly what Abby would’ve wanted. He had never taken the time to ask. The first half of his guilt, from the opening paragraph, comes back as a sentence he can’t finish.

Details I caught.

  • Who asks about Kage: Rhatella “casually,” Zulfiya “in wonder,” Lucifer “in some deranged form of curiosity, and then, almost under his breath, whether Abby was with them. His tail stayed stiff the whole time.” Lucifer cares about Abby. The tail tells on him.
  • Orion’s guilt has two halves. He didn’t get to know Abby, and “A part of him prayed for this. A part of him desperately wanted Kage out of his life.”
  • “He told Nerezza to attack Kage. He could’ve chosen himself, and he chose Kage.”
  • “If anyone deserved to hear it, it was the girl Kage had spent his last night trying to win back.”
  • He “walked, not ran.”
  • Ailani’s room: purple, fluorescent, string lights. The photo: Kage with glue dripping down his face and his hand blurring toward the lens, Ailani laughing beside him. “I should probably toss it.” Orion puts it back “exactly where he found it.”
  • “Abby burst completely into light, and Kage…Kage was attacked beyond recognition.”
  • “The last thing I ever said to him was to lose my number…”
  • “What he said was true, but it wasn’t the whole reason.”
  • “WELL, I DON’T KNOW WHAT TO DO!… I don’t know what to do…”
  • The Kage voice: “say fuck ’em, go to that tournament and kick some fucking ass!”
  • “The last person he spoke about was you… He loved you.” Orion gives Ailani the comfort he can, while having a crush on her.
  • “Keep smiling. Both of them would’ve wanted that.” Smiling is Kage’s defense, and Orion prescribes it.
  • “I don’t…want to relive that right now…”
  • He holds back tears, “especially not in front of Ailani.”

What it’s doing. It’s grief from the outside, and the start of Orion’s cover-up.

What works. Orion becomes the most morally complicated character in the book here. He’s protecting himself, protecting Ailani, protecting Kage’s memory, and moving closer to the girl he likes, all at once. None of those motives cancel the others.

What I’d push on. Still the same note. Orion’s reasoning for delaying the report until after the tournament is thin, and the book knows it (“it wasn’t the whole reason”). But two students are believed dead and a murderer is loose. Someone should push back harder than Ailani does. If she doesn’t, the reader will, so the book should get there first. Ailani almost does (“The villains just get to walk the streets freely?!”), and Orion cuts her off by yelling. Let her hold that position one beat longer.

Score 9.5/10 (up from 9). “A smear, but whole” is one of the best images in the book.

2.9 You’re Supposed To Know

1,287 words. Anubis’s office, midweek.

What happens. Huoyan storms in asking where Kage and Abby are. Anubis corrects his grammar, says his city drone broke Monday night, and that he was at a sponsor dinner. Huoyan accuses him of seeing students as marketing. Anubis tells him to leave. Huoyan’s handprints are left scorched into the desk. Alone, Anubis takes off his sunglasses and breathes out.

What changed in this draft. The chapter now goes one sentence past the scorched handprints: “Anubis took his sunglasses off and let out a breath that had been held way too long.” That’s the beat I asked for last time, a moment where Anubis is clearly worried and hides it, and you put it in the best possible place: after the door closes, with no one watching. The sunglasses come off exactly twice in the book so far: once after he aims the finger gun at Kage’s back in S1 Ch2, and once here. Both times nobody is looking at him, and both times he’s just done something to or about Kage. (Huoyan: “I knew you before those stupid sunglasses.” The glasses are the brand. Taking them off is the man.)

Details I caught.

  • “Oh, cut the shit, Khaled.” Anubis: “Please, Mr. Niao, use our last names. It’s a formal respect.” “You’ll always be just Khaled.”
  • Anubis literally says the word “Sigh.” Huoyan: “What person actually says the word ‘sigh’?” That’s a great little joke.
  • “We are simply coworkers. Nothing more.” “Oh, I know. You’ve made that very clear. I am certainly not trying to rehash anything right now.” Past romance, almost certainly.
  • Anubis still calls him “Hon” throughout. A man who insists on “simply coworkers” while using “Hon” is telling on himself.
  • “It’s ‘neither has.’… You have to keep working on your English.” Huoyan is a second-language English speaker. It’s a nice detail, and a petty move from Anubis.
  • “My main city drone broke… destroyed beyond repair.” The masked figure’s camera.
  • “Approved by the United Nations every single time.” The UN controls the program’s purchases.
  • “You’re supposed to be the watcher!” “Mr. Know-it-all!”
  • “Maybe both of them just needed mental health days.” That’s an appalling line in context, and deliberately so.
  • Huoyan’s rant quotes Anubis’s own lines: “‘I don’t decorate my office because that would be a waste of money.’ ‘Students should be given licenses earlier.’ ‘Let’s put the Elite Tournament on T.V. and sell tickets to watch it in the arena!’”
  • “My students are good children. They may be dysfunctional, but all of them are truly heroic.”
  • “And if they don’t come back? Then what, Number One?”
  • “Huoyan slammed his hands on the desk. The few papers on it curled and browned at the edges.” Then the scorched handprints. Huoyan’s anger leaves a literal mark.
  • Anubis “spoke politely, but the air in the room grew heavier with every word.” That could be tension, or it could be his enhancement. In S2 Ch15, Anubis’s stare feels “so gravitational that Kage felt he might fall through the floor.” The chapter in Section 1 that introduced him is called “Anubis-pheric Pressure.” See Theories.
  • “You never have time for anything unless it benefits you.”

What it’s doing. It lays out the adult conflict about what the program is for.

What works. It’s tight and angry, and it leaves Anubis looking both callous and possibly hiding something. The drone “breaking” on exactly the night it mattered is either very bad luck or very convenient. The scorched handprints are a perfect closing image.

What I’d push on. The note is answered. One small thing: Huoyan says the two have been “absent since Tuesday… it’s been three days now.” That only works if this scene is Thursday. The chapter after Wednesday’s (S2 Ch8) doesn’t say, and the timeline reads as “midweek.” A one-word anchor (“Thursday morning”) would settle it.

Score 9/10 (up from 8.5). The adult world in one argument, and now Anubis has a private moment that makes him harder to read.

2.10 Another Task

2,887 words. The villains’ warehouse, a few days later.

What happens. Kage has spent days in a bathroom corner, hugging Abby’s backpack and blowing her silent whistle. When Frostbite reaches for the whistle, Kage flips him by instinct. Frostbite, surprisingly, pats his head and brings him clothes, grooming supplies, and makeup. Thanatos and Frostbite argue over who owns Kage’s future. Frostbite points out that Thanatos rebuilt Kage without consent. Kage reappears with a self-cut, cartoonish, “floating” hairstyle and eyeliner. Thanatos announces he’s going to Russia for a couple of months. Frostbite apologizes for threatening Kage, calls Thanatos weak, and assigns a new test: bring him Lucifer Tulivalo.

What changed in this draft. One change matters more than all the others: the disguise contacts are yellow now. In the last draft they were gray, which I flagged as a possible continuity slip against Ailani seeing his “blue eyes” in S2 Ch14. (It isn’t one: he only wears them for villain work.) But yellow is a far more interesting choice. Abby is introduced with “yellow eyes and twin-layered halo,” and her eyes “started to glow a deep yellow” right before Divine Judgment. Kage now does his villain work looking out through Abby’s eye color. I don’t know if that’s deliberate. If it is, it’s one of the best ideas in the book. If it isn’t, keep it anyway, and let someone notice it later. Rebento, maybe, or Ailani.

Other things that stood out on the reread (the consent line is the one other confirmed change):

  • “He patted Kage’s head. It was the first gentle touch Kage had felt since Abby.” That one sentence explains the bow that follows.
  • Frostbite’s consent line changed from “forced yourself onto him” to “forced your biomass onto him.” The old version was deliberately uncomfortable. The new one is more literal, and it keeps the consent point without the sexual-assault overtone. I think that’s the right call for this book, though it’s a little less sharp.
  • Kage’s loyalty starts to shift on the page: “Kage had been told never to listen to Frostbite unless Thanatos said otherwise, though he was starting to question why. At least with Frostbite, he always knew where he stood.”
  • During Frostbite’s pitch, “his hand had found the whistle around his neck, and his thumb kept running over it.” Frostbite “could feel Kage hearing his words and almost purposefully not listening.” The whistle is how he doesn’t listen.
  • After crying: “Kage wiped his face on his sleeve, and just like that, the old attitude was back.” The speed of the switch is the biomass, or the boy, or both.
  • “Abby’s scorched backpack still hung from his shoulder” as he shows off the new hair.

Details I caught.

  • “Pretty soon I’m gonna have to charge him rent.”
  • He blows the silent whistle for days. “If you ever need support or help from me, blow it! I’ll come as quickly as I can!” Kage finally uses it, too late. If Abby is somehow still out there, she has been hearing this.
  • Frostbite: “It’s gonna rust at this rate if you keep blowing it.”
  • The flip is instinct. That’s the first visible sign of the biomass.
  • “It’s okay, kid. Just don’t do it again. I’ll kill you if you do.” That’s the whole of Frostbite in two sentences.
  • “The other half, Frostbite would reveal to Kage later.” That’s a direct promise from the narrator.
  • “Sorry, I’m not really good with kids. And I kind of forgot your gender.”
  • Kage bows. “A form of respect he never really gave anyone… there was a reverential edge to it.” Thanatos notices and doesn’t like it.
  • “That is my project.”
  • “Did Kage ask you to do that for him?” Thanatos goes silent. “You just invaded the boy’s body without consent and forced your biomass onto him.” It’s still the sharpest moral line in the villain plot.
  • Thanatos’s motto: “Figure out what makes them tick, and try to give them what they desire.”
  • “I know for a fact you didn’t ask him how he was mentally. Only physical questions came from you.”
  • The new hair. “It was messy and layered, with long, wispy pieces framing his face. His bangs were swept up and to the side, and the top stood up so high it almost looked like it was floating.” The drooping “lazy” hair from S1 Ch1 is gone. “That lazy me is gone now… Just like an action figure!” He’s styling himself after his own hero-name philosophy, and he’s doing it in a villain’s bathroom.
  • Thanatos: “I’m training a degenerate.” Frostbite: “Lighten up, Thany. I think it looks good.”
  • “Rhatella’s gear was something only the old Kage was after.”
  • Yellow contacts to hide his blue eyes: “Your blue eyes are too much of a tell.” He’s never worn contacts before and puts them in “rather clumsily.” In S2 Ch14, Ailani stares into his “blue eyes,” so he took them out to come home as himself.
  • Thanatos is going to Russia. “The boss is sending me.” Later he’s in Russia, at Nerezza’s.
  • “You’re…not going to save me anymore?” “No, not that! What I said before that!” That’s Thanatos reminding him not to trust Frostbite.
  • The whistle now hangs on a string around his neck.
  • Frostbite’s bare hand: “The tips of his fingers were dark and blistered, like the cold was eating him from the inside out. Kage’s own hand curled around nothing again.” He decides he must have seen Frostbite on the news.
  • “Whether I’m your boss or your partner, I shouldn’t be threatening your life.” “At least, it was the half he was willing to say out loud.”
  • “Man plans, and God laughs.”
  • “Why am I not dead…?”
  • “I have to, for Abby…”
  • “Oh, God. You’re another one of those.” He’s tired of being told about his potential.
  • “Yeah, I know. You’ll kill me.”
  • The test: “Show me you’re above your nobody classmates.”
  • “I’ll happily kick Luci’s ass for you.”

What it’s doing. It’s the villains’ version of the nurse’s office. Kage is cared for, in a twisted way, and gets a new look and a new task.

What works. Frostbite turns into the book’s most interesting adult in one chapter. His care is clumsy, and every kindness comes attached to a threat. The consent argument is the sharpest moral writing in the villain plot. And the haircut is a great piece of visual storytelling.

What I’d push on. Same as before. The makeup and “forgot your gender” bit is a funny line. Just make sure it doesn’t become the only thing Frostbite’s tenderness is allowed to be. The narrator promises “the other half,” so the book owes the reader that payoff. It hasn’t come yet as of S4 Ch7.

Score 9/10 A grief chapter set in a villain’s lair.

2.11 Appreciate The Invite, Luci

1,536 words. The school at night, Thursday.

What happens. In a black hood, Kage sneaks into school with a glue key. He clings to the hallway ceiling and watches Orion and Ailani walk by smiling, fist-bump “for Kage,” and part. Alone, Orion punches the wall and curses Kage for dying. Kage almost leaves a strip of tape on Orion’s door as a sign, and decides not to. In Lucifer’s dorm (single bedroom, trophies everywhere) he finds an Elite Tournament poster and a black claymore engraved “Minkowski.” Lucifer walks out, and Kage knocks him out with the flat of the sword, tapes him up, and leaves through the window with him and the poster.

What changed in this draft. The chapter grew by about 110 words. The confirmed changes are Ailani’s line and the last two beats (below). But the part I want to talk about is Kage’s head on the ceiling, which reads more mixed than I remembered: less jealousy, more grief. “Seeing the two of them together made Kage’s stomach curl in a strange way. He was happy to see Orion okay now. If he were in front of Orion, he’d probably cry. But Orion didn’t look like he’d been crying at all.” That last sentence is the sting. Then: “Kage hadn’t even been gone a week, and Orion was already impressing Huoyan and getting back pats from Ailani.” After Orion punches the wall: “Nobody had ever punched a wall over him.” That’s an orphan’s sentence. It’s the first time Kage learns someone grieved him, and he learns it from a ceiling. And when he leaves Orion’s door: “His hand found Abby’s whistle under his collar, and he held it until his fingers stopped shaking. Then he slapped his own face a couple of times… Sure enough, a smile appeared on his face again. It came back faster than it should have.” That’s the biomass, flagged in one line, without anyone naming it.

Ailani’s line also changed. It was “But Kage cares way more about this than Abby…” Now: “But Kage would care way more about winning this thing than Abby would…Abby would just want to see us happy.” I liked the old present tense (“grief doesn’t do tenses”), but the new version is more generous to Abby and sets up “Keep smiling” from S2 Ch8.

Details I caught.

  • “This isn’t heroic in the slightest.”
  • On the ceiling, “He’d stopped following their words and was only watching their faces. Their smiles never wavered.” “Do they even care that I’m not around?”
  • “For Kage! Right?” Orion hesitates before bumping her fist. “Don’t forget about Abby.”
  • Ailani: “Of course for her too!” Orion had to remind her to include Abby. That’s a small, telling beat.
  • “We’re gonna kill it this weekend!”
  • “Why’d you have to go and die on me?! You piece of shit!”
  • The tape on the door would have been the perfect Kage gesture. He doesn’t do it because “I don’t want him thinking I have some crazy vendetta against him.” He’s already thinking like someone with secrets.
  • “I’ll see you soon, Hermes.”
  • Lucifer’s door is unlocked.
  • Only one bedroom. “Kage had always assumed Elias and Lucifer shared a dorm.” But in S2 Ch4 and Ch5, Elias was in Lucifer’s room, lying down on “the bed next to” Lucifer. So Elias sleeps over. That fits Elias as Lucifer’s night watchman. And on this one night, Elias isn’t there.
  • Medals, a trophy case, plaques. “God, his ego just knows no bounds.”
  • The poster: the tournament is this weekend, and top students enter the Elite Program (“a fast track to a license”).
  • The claymore: large, black, on holders, engraved “Minkowski.”
  • Kage remembers Elias’s “I pray you guys come back okay”: “It was like he knew something bad was going to happen.”
  • He plans to frame Elias to cause in-fighting. Then he puts the sword back.
  • The baseball-bat swing is quick and funny.
  • “Can’t exactly carry you and a giant sword, so the sword stays. But I’m taking this.” He hangs the sword back, slightly off, and Elias notices it’s a degree or two crooked.
  • “This’ll show Frostbite I deserve much more than I’m given at this good-for-nothing school.” (The last draft said “show him.” Naming Frostbite is clearer, and sadder: the person he wants to impress now is a villain.)
  • “Then he shoved the window open and swung out into the night.”

What it’s doing. It’s a heist, a haunting, and a kidnapping in under 1,500 words.

What works. The ceiling scene is fantastic. Kage watches his own funeral from above, which is literally the “soul up above looking down” from his King speech. Whether or not that echo is intentional, it lands.

What I’d push on. Still the same note. Lucifer, the top student in the world, goes down to a single swing. It’s plausible: he’s exhausted, it’s a surprise, and it’s the flat of a heavy sword. But one sentence acknowledging how tired he looked would sell it, and would tie it to his never-sleeping condition.

Score 9.5/10 (up from 9). The ghost chapter, and “Nobody had ever punched a wall over him” is the line that makes it.

2.12 What’s Hiding Underneath

3,004 words. Outside the warehouse, Thursday night. (Unchanged in this draft, word for word as far as I can tell.)

What happens. Kage delivers Lucifer to the base. Frostbite is gone. Lucifer starts seizing and transforms into Abaddon: ten feet tall, black-skinned, horns aflame, talking about himself in the third person. Abaddon explains that “the boy” never fully sleeps to keep him locked in, and that Kage’s knockout let him out. Abaddon pulps Kage into a wall. Kage coughs black blood and gets up. His broken leg regenerates in real time. Abaddon bites through Kage’s forearm. Kage drives the exposed bone into the back of Abaddon’s throat, and the biomass rebuilds his arm. Frostbite arrives and drops a block of ice on Abaddon’s head. Abaddon reverts to Lucifer. Frostbite lingers over Lucifer a little too long. Kage asks to compete in the tournament as his reward, and Frostbite agrees.

Details I caught.

  • “Maybe I should’ve listened to Thanatos. Clearly he’s unprofessional.”
  • The transformation: limbs and horns lengthen, the tail turns “thick and scaly,” and his skin goes from “normal pale” to “hollow black.”
  • “FREEDOM IS SWEETER THAN ABADDON COULD HAVE EVER IMAGINED!” The third-person, all-caps voice is perfect for a thing that has been a prisoner.
  • “The boy…he never quite sleeps… similar to a dog. He rests but never fully.” That explains Abby’s diagnosis, Lucifer’s yawning, and Elias sleeping over.
  • “Abaddon can see everything the boy does! Abaddon gets to choose that luxury for him.”
  • Kage: “If I was going to die, it would’ve happened a couple of days ago.”
  • “I’ve been training for months to beat someone that moves way faster than that.” Orion’s rivalry pays off against a demon.
  • “Crack. Crack! CRACK!”
  • Black blood.
  • Abaddon vaporizes the glue with heat. Kage knows “glue vapor.”
  • The key line: “Against Orion, Kage had made pillars like that float. He didn’t even think to try now. Back then, he’d been sure he was better than whoever stood in front of him. Somewhere around the mirror room, he’d stopped being sure.” Telekinesis runs on certainty. That explains the failed butterfly in S2 Ch3. It’s an excellent piece of retroactive explanation, and in this draft S2 Ch3 shows you the exact moment it broke: Kage looking at Ailani’s temple and seeing Orion’s.
  • The leg regrows. “Are you trembling? You’ve never been scared before, have you?… Fear looks really bad on you.”
  • “All that bug talk is making you sound a lot like Luci! No wonder you two are a team!” “ABADDON AND THE BOY ARE NOT A TEAM! I HATE HIM!”
  • The bite. Both forearm bones exposed. “Your blood is tasty, red one!”
  • The whistle, “speckled with his blood,” on the asphalt. He grips it before he stands.
  • “You can’t break…what’s already broken!”
  • The bone stab into the throat. On Saturday, Lucifer is still rubbing a throat that’s been “sore since he awoke,” and Kage sneers at its redness in the lobby.
  • “For a moment, Kage enjoyed hearing him in anguish. He wasn’t sure why, but he knew it was…different.” That’s the biomass, amplifying.
  • “Luci, you can’t keep secrets from me like that. I could’ve died!”
  • “I DID TEXT YOU!… YOU NEVER LOOKED AT MY MESSAGES!” Frostbite doesn’t read Kage’s texts either, just like Thanatos.
  • Frostbite and Lucifer: “Frostbite crouched beside him longer than required. He reached towards Lucifer’s face with his bare hand, then pulled it away. Even asleep, Lucifer was still hot enough to burn.” See Theories.
  • “I will return the boy tonight.”
  • “Show my friends I’m alive.” “Anubis was watching me!”
  • “I’ll be watching, so don’t embarrass yourself.” Frostbite watches from a bar in Chapter NULL.
  • “WHY IS THE WAREHOUSE SO BANGED UP?!”

What it’s doing. It reveals Lucifer’s secret and Kage’s new body in the same fight.

What works. The double reveal is a great structural decision. Two boys with monsters inside them meet in a parking lot, and each one learns what the other is carrying. The bone-stab is the most visceral image in the book. The mirror-room line is a superb bit of internal logic.

What I’d push on. Abaddon’s monologue explains a lot (how he got out, why the boy never sleeps, what he can see). It’s fun in that voice, but some of it could be held back for Lucifer’s mindscape in S3 Ch16, which repeats some of it.

Score 9/10 Two monsters meet.

2.13 Sophomores Are Tournament Bound

2,362 words. Saturday morning: Anubis’s office, Ailani’s dorm, Lucifer’s dorm, the bus. (Unchanged in this draft.)

What happens. Anubis says fifteen students are enrolled and one slot is saved for Kage. Huoyan reminds him Kage is missing. Anubis is sure he’ll show. Huoyan lets slip that he used to be Anubis’s sidekick. Anubis says he nearly kept Ailani out because she might distract Kage and because her hero ambition is “temporary,” and mentions his own brother is competing. Orion and Ailani warm up together, and Orion punches a bag “like a boxer aiming for someone’s vitals.” In Lucifer’s dorm, Lucifer has strange energy and a sore throat, and Elias insists the claymore is crooked. At the stadium lobby, the door creaks, and Zulfiya breaks the silence: “King! You’re back!”

Details I caught.

  • “The first year shows off the students when they’re too green.”
  • “I saved a slot for Kage.” Anubis saw Kage on camera Thursday night (confirmed in S2 Ch15), so he knew Kage was alive and didn’t tell Huoyan.
  • “Sticky Situation make his grand return!” “It’s creative and whimsical. There are days I wish our hero names were like that.”
  • Huoyan: “You’re the brand and merchandise guy.”
  • “One day, I’m your sidekick. The next day, I’m just a teacher and you’re my boss?” “Weren’t you-” “You’re airing out dirty laundry in my office. You save that for when you’re crying at home.” “I sleep here.” Huoyan lives at the school. That line is played for laughs and it’s quietly very sad.
  • No slot for Abby, “she is not a fighter,” and “she could also distract Kage.”
  • Ailani: “her desire to be a hero is only temporary. She has other dreams and goals.” Paid off in S4 Ch1: choreographer and dancer.
  • “My brother will do well in terms of school spirit.” “He has to carry that Nasser name well!” Huoyan: “your brother hasn’t had the greatest showing recently. He is heroic in spirit.” That’s Youseff.
  • Orion’s punches are “like a boxer aiming for someone’s vitals.” That’s the exact phrase from Kage in S1 Ch1. Orion is grieving the way Kage did.
  • “Kage would be cursing at us from up above.”
  • “We’ve been training together, so I know all your moves.”
  • “We were not and never were friends… our competition, that was…for lack of a better and more accurate term, special.”
  • “I was taught never to speak ill of the dead.”
  • Ailani: “My eyes have kind of run dry for Kage… He deserved to know I still cared about him.”
  • She takes his hand; he blushes.
  • Lucifer: “I feel like I got energy like crazy!” Abaddon fed and rested. “Between punches, he rubbed his throat.”
  • Elias: “You seem well rested.”
  • “The claymore. It’s crooked.” A degree or two. “If I said it’s crooked, then it’s crooked, Luci.” “There’s no need to lie.” “You really aren’t lying, are you?” Elias reads people well enough to tell.
  • “Those holders were specifically forged for the claymore… Someone was here.”
  • “Elias simply fidgeted with his collar.” That’s a tell. What’s he nervous about?
  • Reina’s “I’ll get you like BOOM!!!!” with “super thin and scrawny” arms. Her power makes that funny in retrospect.
  • “It’s a trap, you moron.”
  • The bus ride is seven minutes.
  • The lobby: thirty seconds of silence, then “King! You’re back!”

What it’s doing. It’s the “assembling the cast” chapter, and it sneaks in three major adult reveals.

What works. The tonal juggling is good: comedy in Lucifer’s dorm, mourning in Ailani’s, and a quietly sad confession in Anubis’s office. Ending on Zulfiya’s three words is a strong cliffhanger.

What I’d push on. It’s a lot of setup in one chapter. The Anubis–Huoyan scene alone has the sidekick reveal, the brother, the Ailani decision, and the “I sleep here” line. Any one of those could carry a scene. Consider giving the sidekick reveal more room somewhere else.

Score 8.5/10 A dense, rewarding setup chapter.

2.14 He’s Back

1,657 words. The stadium lobby.

What happens. Kage walks straight to Orion. Lucifer gets in his way, and Elias stops Lucifer. Orion is more afraid than he was of Nerezza, thinking Kage heard the dome. Kage hugs him and cries: “Hermes, you’re okay!” He apologizes to Ailani, who stares into his eyes, slaps him, curses him in Spanish, slaps Orion for not checking whether he was breathing, and tells Kage to keep her number. The three form a “band.” Orion quietly asks about Abby. Kage’s hand goes to the whistle, and that’s the answer. Anubis asks Kage to walk with him.

Details I caught.

  • Zulfiya hears “Hermes” and knows not to run up.
  • Kage sees Lucifer’s “slightly reddened throat and sneered.” He’s enjoying the evidence.
  • Elias: “Luci, don’t. Trust me.” What does Elias know?
  • Orion’s three options: run (looks guilty), stand (he might be exposed), strike first (he’d look crazy). “Act casual” is the most Orion thing possible.
  • “I kinda blacked out the other night.” “Blacked out” is how a teenager describes being dismembered.
  • “You’d be surprised how rare it is for a pretty girl like you to laugh at my dumb jokes.”
  • “Ailani’s face couldn’t settle on one feeling. Hurt, bewilderment, sorrow, and something akin to relief all fought for it at once.” Then “She stared deep into his blue eyes.” The yellow contacts are out. He came back as himself.
  • “HIJO DE PUTA!” “PENDEJO!” “IMBÉCIL!” Her Spanish comes out when she’s furious.
  • “You didn’t think to check and make sure this idiot wasn’t breathing before letting me cry?!”
  • “And Kage? Keep my number.”
  • “Three-man band.” Ailani: “three-person band,” and “I should’ve known you were sexist.”
  • Kage: “I’m not touching Elias. That man gives me the heebie-jeebies.” Ailani: “He is super creepy.”
  • “You stood next to us!… You’re committed, whether you like it or not!”
  • “Kage, if you’re okay, is…Abby..?” Kage’s hand goes to “the gold whistle he had around his neck.” Orion hadn’t noticed it until then.
  • “Do good for Abby’s sake.”
  • “The tension in the room drained from the hug, but once again, everyone was chill except Ailani.” I used the old version of this sentence as an example of telling after showing. The new one is better, because it turns the summary into a setup for her slap.
  • “I came here to make sure I beat you in front of everyone.”
  • “A different teacher” made the bracket.
  • “I am mildly contented by your return, Ozul.” That’s as warm as Huoyan gets.

What it’s doing. It’s the reunion, built on dramatic irony. Orion and the reader know more than the room does.

What works. The tension of Kage walking silently toward Orion is excellent. The reader shares Orion’s fear even knowing what Kage doesn’t. Ailani’s slap-and-curse sequence is the funniest beat in the section.

What I’d push on. Kage and Orion never talk about the dome or the villains. Orion knows Kage is working with Shifting Horror. Kage doesn’t know Orion chose him. It’s realistic that neither brings it up in a crowded lobby. But give the reader one private glance between them, a moment where each almost says something, so the silence reads as a choice.
