Score 9/10 Reunions are hard to write, and this one works.

2.15 The Talk And Walk

2,586 words. The walk to the stadium doors.

What happens. Anubis walks with Kage at the back and confronts him: cameras saw him on Thursday clinging to the ceiling and entering Lucifer’s room. Kage counters by calling out Anubis’s secret photos. Anubis, delighted to be challenged, says he wants Kage as his successor (“an orphaned failure turned number one hero just sells itself”) and mentions Lucifer’s “backstory.” Kage pretends to be surprised that Anubis knows about Frostbite. Class reps Lance and Rebento offer to throw Kage over the crowd so he’s first through the stadium doors. Kage reluctantly accepts help and lands in front of a cheering crowd.

Details I caught.

  • The walking groups: Lucifer’s group in front; Ailani, Orion, and Zulfiya; Severine alone; Volkan nervously alone; Cheyenne with “a gray-haired boy and a brown-haired boy.”
  • “Our security cameras in school are top of the line and everywhere.” “We aren’t legally allowed to have cameras in student dorms despite my asking.”
  • “Last I saw, you were heading into Lucifer’s room, and you never came back out.” And Anubis said nothing to anyone. Lucifer was missing for hours too.
  • “Wait, you want cameras in all of our rooms..?” That’s a deflection that also happens to be a great question.
  • “DAMMIT, KAGE!” Anubis’s temper shows for the first time.
  • “Like how you’re honest about taking pictures of students without their consent?” Kage throws the consent theme back at the institution.
  • “Are you challenging my authority as head of the hero program?” Then “That’s wonderful!”
  • “I almost called a physician!”
  • “I need someone to take my place someday soon.” Why soon? See Theories.
  • “You don’t put on a heroic act; you just act heroic.” This is the nicest thing anyone says about Kage, and Anubis means it as a sales pitch.
  • “An orphaned failure turned number one hero just sells itself!”
  • The succession order: his brother (“his heart’s just not in it”), then Lucifer (“an effort trying to cover up all that backstory”). Anubis knows about Abaddon.
  • “Huoyan is kind of scouting him himself.”
  • “Backstory?” Kage asked. “He thought of the ten-foot demon outside the warehouse and kept his face perfectly still.” Kage is a better liar than he was in S1 Ch14.
  • “You survived an attack from Frostbite!” “His hand curled around nothing at his side, but Anubis didn’t see.”
  • “The chilling air of the number one hero keeping him in place felt so gravitational that Kage felt he might fall through the floor down to the earth’s core.” Another weight-and-pressure hint about Anubis. See Theories.
  • “I was worried you might’ve been compromised and instead, you know when to retreat?! Marvelous!” Anubis says the word “compromised” out loud and then accepts the cover story. Is he that credulous, or choosing to be?
  • Lance Faisceau (gray, reflective hair, stern) and Rebento Azucre (brown hair). They argue over who’s the real class rep.
  • “You wish to beat Lucifer to the door?”
  • “‘Sticking’ it to someone! Eh! Eh!”
  • “Heroes help other heroes.” “In the future, I’m sure there will be a time where we need to work together.”
  • “I should probably get used to working with others. That’s never been my strong suit.” That’s real growth from the boy who said “you won’t even have a use for other heroes anymore.”
  • “Right, I forget that everyone here is stronger than me…”
  • “Why didn’t you ever try talking me into doing this during my first year?” “You weren’t Sticky Situation in your first year.”
  • “Little beetles.” The insect insults spread even to Kage’s allies.
  • Orion: “There goes Sticky Situation soaring overhead.” That’s fond.
  • “Let him have this pity win.”
  • The doors, the crowd: “He wanted this forever.”

What it’s doing. It confronts Kage with the hero establishment’s version of what Thanatos offered, and closes the section on the crowd.

What works. The Anubis conversation mirrors Thanatos’s handshake. Both mentors see Kage’s story as useful, both offer him the top, and both accept his lies when they’re convenient. The last line is ominous because the book has just shown us what the biomass does to wants.

What I’d push on. The Lance and Rebento plan is fun, but the “everyone here is stronger than me” beat goes by fast. It’s one of Kage’s most self-pitying thoughts, and it might deserve a moment longer.

Score 9/10 Two mentors, one boy, and a crowd.

Section 2 overall

Score 9.6/10 (up from 9.3). This is the strongest section of the book, and this draft is where most of your revision effort went. It shows. Abby’s brother has an image, Abby’s decision has a rule, Kage’s refusal to call for help now protects his secret every way he turns, Orion’s choice in the dome is uglier and more honest, and the biomass amplifies “what’s already there” instead of only the bad parts. The section has the midpoint, the death, the moral test, the transformation, and two monster reveals, and it never loses track of grief. If someone asked me to point to the chapters that prove this book is worth finishing, I’d still point to 2.5 through 2.8, and they’re better now than when I first said it.

Chapter by Chapter: Section 3, “Elite Tournament”

A note on the bracket before the chapters. Sixteen fighters, single elimination, split into two halves. Gene calls Round 2 the “semi-finals,” the half-winners’ matches the “finals,” and the last match the “grand finals.” It’s unusual naming, but it’s consistent. Here’s the bracket as it stands after S4 Ch7:

Round 1 Result Round 2 Half final
Lucifer vs. Severine Lucifer (ring-out, pushed barrier) Lucifer vs. Reina: Lucifer Kage over Lucifer
Reina vs. Geneki Reina (Geneki broke the floor)
Kage vs. Zulfiya Kage (forfeit) Kage vs. Elias: Kage
Elias vs. Cheyenne Elias (ring-out)
Orion vs. Inazuma Orion (low blow, kicked out) Orion vs. Rebento: Orion Orion vs. Ailani: next
Rebento vs. Alexander Rebento (gumball, ring-out)
Ailani vs. Youseff Ailani (“tripped”) Ailani vs. Lance: Ailani (S4 Ch7)
Lance vs. Volkan Lance (Volkan rolled out)

3.1 And…Begin!

2,242 words. The stadium lobby and the hero lounge, Saturday.

What happens. Lucifer threatens Kage in front of the screens. Orion privately tells Ailani that Kage shouldn’t be alive. The hero lounge introduces Kelly Iasacht and Gene Gardner, the tournament’s creator and Rhatella and Geneki’s mother. Anubis explains he’s rooting for Kage because Kage has no family in the stands, and Huoyan calls it self-interest. Gene announces the rules: lose in Round 1 and you’re out of Elite Program contention. Round 1 opens with Lucifer vs. Severine.

What changed in this draft. Orion’s warning to Ailani and Huoyan’s rebuttal to Anubis were both rewritten. On the reread I also caught three quiet threads that start in this chapter and feed later ones.

  • Orion’s private warning to Ailani now has a confession inside it that he doesn’t say: “Every word Orion said was true, but it wasn’t everything. Shifting Horror talking with Kage was still in Orion’s head. That’s where his shapeshifter idea came from, but he couldn’t tell Ailani. If he told her that, he’d need to tell her everything. That included what Orion had chosen on the count of three.” Orion’s lies now all lead back to the same secret, and the narration keeps a running count.
  • Huoyan gets a quiet motive: “His phone was out before he even finished sinking into the cushions. Anubis had been right about Kage, but Huoyan still needed to find out what’s going on with Abby.” So in S3 Ch2, when he “pocketed his phone,” he’s been searching for a dead student while everyone else watches the show. He’s the only adult still looking for her.
  • Ailani starts watching: “Her eyes lingered on Kage a tad longer than usual. A small-scale search to see if anything was off. For the time being, everything seemed okay, but that didn’t make her look away. If anything, it made her squint harder, looking for an imperfection.” That’s the seed for S4 Ch6’s “you’ve got a lot of answering to do” and arguably for S4 Ch7’s title, “Saw Right Through You.”

Details I caught.

  • Lucifer: “termite.” (It was “moth” in the last draft.) The insect insults keep coming: ant, worm, pebble, bug, termite, and later cockroach.
  • “I pray to any and all gods that I get to crush you in the first round.”
  • Orion: “Kage shouldn’t be breathing right now!” “I know it’s really him and not some shapeshifter. No one can act exactly like Kage. But something about him doesn’t add up.”
  • Ailani’s “Kage-like smirk.” “Kage taught me humor is a good way to handle feelings like that.” He’s rubbing off on her too.
  • Kelly Iasacht: blond, blue-eyed, a top hero who wants Anubis’s spot, and gets “less government funding since he chose not to teach at the hero school.” Government money flows through the school.
  • “My wife was already yelling at me.” He’s the only adult hero shown with a working family life.
  • Gene Gardner: rat-like, with large ears, a snout, and whiskers; a green suit and green glasses. She’s the creator of the tournament and the program. “I just adore announcing.”
  • Kelly: “My son didn’t end up showing up after all.” Who is his son? See Continuity.
  • Anubis: “It’d be wonderfully poetic if either of them win after Huoyan here tried to stop them from fighting.”
  • Huoyan: Tulivalo “has the highest average of anybody in class… He wants to be a hero more than most, so I want him to hero alongside me… if he’s in my agency.” Huoyan runs an agency, and he picks students by effort, which is the opposite of how Anubis picks.
  • Anubis: “Every kid in that lobby has their parents cheering for them… Kage doesn’t have that.” “He’ll perform better if he isn’t sad about his orphanism.” The last sentence ruins the rest, and Huoyan says so: “That last sentence ruined the whole speech for me.”
  • Huoyan: “If that were really your reason, you’d be rooting for every kid out there with no one in the stands or watching on T.V.” That’s a sharper rebuttal than the last draft’s. It catches Anubis in a logical hole, not just a mood.
  • Gene: “If you lose in the first round, you are disqualified from the Elite Program entirely.” “Less than half of you will be selected.”
  • “Thank you to the millions watching at home!”
  • Severine “didn’t respond to or acknowledge anyone.”
  • Elias: “I will remain exclusively supportive.”
  • The arena: a clear dome of thick glass and a white concrete square. Win by submission, forfeit, knockout, or ring-out. “Killing is also obviously banned.”

What it’s doing. It moves the story into a new mode, a sports tournament, and introduces the adults who run it.

What works. Gene and Kelly come in fully formed. The three adults on the couch (Anubis the salesman, Huoyan the skeptic, Kelly the mocking outsider) make a great commentary booth.

What I’d push on. Still a long runway. The first match starts at the very end, after about 2,200 words of setup. Some of the lounge material could be moved into the gaps between later fights, where the book already uses it well.

Score 8.5/10 (up from 8). The booth is built, and three quiet threads (Orion’s secret, Huoyan’s search, Ailani’s squint) start here.

3.2 Quick And Painful

3,096 words. Arena: Lucifer vs. Severine; then Lance and Kage.

What happens. Severine’s barrier holds off Lucifer’s flames, and she laughs. Lucifer notices her shield is a half-dome sitting on the ground, and pushes the whole thing, with her on it, out of bounds. She breaks down, screaming that she’s perfect, and calls for Kage by name as Kelly drags her out. Kage puts his hand on the glass, then slides out of sight. Rebento teases Kage about dating her. Lance tries to comfort him. Kage snaps (“DO NOT TELL ME TO CALM DOWN!”), Ailani calms him, and he apologizes to Lance. Then Reina vs. Geneki ends instantly, and Reina is declared the winner without knowing why.

What changed in this draft. You took my note about Severine. The quiet beat inside her tantrum is now a real beat: “She realized in her rage that she hadn’t really talked to a single one of her classmates, and for a moment that rage quieted enough for something somber to bleed through.” And after Kage disappears: “her body slumped in Kelly’s grasp, if only for a second.” The screaming is still long, but now there are two places where it stops, and both are about being alone.

Kage’s moment at the glass is the best thing in the chapter, and I undersold it last time: “The eyes felt like they were lingering on him forever, and Kage’s legs started trembling. He looked down at Severine. His hand reached out to press against the glass, and his other clung to the whistle. He didn’t see Severine down there. He saw the convenience store, and the world breaking around him. Then, it was Abby, the tentacle stabbing straight through her. His hand let go of the whistle. Then he pulled his other hand away.” That’s both hands, both losses, one after the other. It explains the whole spiral that follows with Lance.

Details I caught.

  • “I would not want to embarrass the current poster boy of the hero program with my absolute perfection!”
  • Lucifer’s card: Hellspawn. “Hell isn’t somewhere Lucifer came from; it’s something he carries with him!” The card mentions Abaddon outright: “Very few know of Abaddon’s existence, and even fewer have lived after seeing it!” Having the card spoil the secret the plot has already revealed is a fun choice. But note that the card says “even fewer have lived.” That supports the theory about Elias’s past.
  • “A low-grade thug at best!” “One of my many dislikes: foolishness!”
  • Reina: “He’s being tactical right now?! No way!”
  • Elias: “If he noticed the same thing I did, then I believe he’s going to win.”
  • The tactic: a semicircle, “not a dome,” means she’s standing on movable ground. It’s a great fight solution that comes from the rules.
  • “BONG!” Her feet are on the grass.
  • Severine: “BLASPHEMOUS… PROFANE… BORDERLINE SACRILEGIOUS!” She talks about her record like it’s religion.
  • Anubis sends Kelly because he’s not program staff: “That way, if she does something dumb and a fight ensues, we aren’t fully liable.” Liability over care.
  • “THAT’S FOR THE PEASANTS! THE COMMONERS! THE NOBODIES!”
  • “She had spoken to nobody; there was one exception to that, though.” Then: “OZUL! TELL THEM!”
  • Kage’s hand presses against the glass, then pulls away. That’s the grip motif again: reaching and not holding.
  • Rebento: “Were her lips cold during kissing too?” Lance: “REPULSIVE!”
  • Elias: “He’s telling the truth, idiot.” Elias can apparently tell when people lie. He also said, “You really aren’t lying, are you?” to Lucifer in S2 Ch13.
  • Kage: “A person was calling out for me to save them, and I just…left them. What kind of hero does that make me?” The store trauma has turned into a reflex: every call for help reads to him as a life-or-death rescue.
  • Lance: “All heroes freeze.” Kage: “No, they don’t! They’re not supposed to!”
  • “You can’t save people from themselves.”
  • “She’s pretty narcissistic.”
  • “DO NOT TELL ME TO CALM DOWN!… You’re just the perfect hero, aren’t you? The great Lance knows everything about everything.” He’s clutching the whistle.
  • Ailani’s touch calms him instantly. Orion “had this moment filed in his head for now, right next to every other unanswered question about Kage.”
  • Kage bows to Lance. Bowing keeps showing up for him.
  • “Oh, the straight man doesn’t get to be funny too? You’re putting me in slots, Kage.” That’s the best Lance line.
  • Geneki: about eight feet tall, muscular, a lazy eye, rat ears like Rhatella’s, and a tail that’s a cut-off stump. The class steps back from her, except Ailani and Kage.
  • Ailani: “Good luck out there, Gen!”
  • Kage: “I didn’t think she was a monster!” Ailani: “She’s not a monster! She’s a very nice girl.”
  • Anubis: “She’s incredibly strong, but lacks any uhm…personality.” That’s cruel and casual.
  • Huoyan hands Gene the mic “in a sort of genuflection.” It’s a sarcastic bow.
  • “Uhm…what just…happened..?”

What it’s doing. It has two fights that are anti-fights, and a real emotional fight between them.

What works. Lucifer’s tactic is the best “aha” of the first round. Kage’s guilt spiral over Severine is the chapter’s real heart, and Lance’s patience with him sets up their friendship.

What I’d push on. The quiet beat is there now. I’d still trim one of the all-caps blocks (the “PEASANTS… COMMONERS… NOBODIES” block and the “I AM NOT EVERYONE” block do the same job), so the two quiet moments have more room.

Score 9/10 (up from 8.5). It’s the chapter where Kage’s trauma becomes a public problem, and now the reader sees the trauma happen in his head at the glass.

3.3 Which Kage Is Best?

2,169 words. The lobby and the lounge, during an intermission.

What happens. Gene explains that Geneki stomped so hard she broke through the floor and was technically touching underneath it, which counts as out of bounds. Geneki asks her mother if she can go home, alone. Huoyan does math on why Kage can’t win. Anubis says the hero world is “boring” and people want someone relatable. Inazuma Sokudo, Kage’s middle-school acquaintance, crashes the lobby, reveals he sent the maid photo, and calls Orion slow and a “big black dolt.” Ailani calls it racist. Kage is called up to fight Zulfiya.

What changed in this draft. You took my note on the title. The question is now asked out loud: “Okay, but let’s be for real for a sec. Which Kage is best? I think maid Kage crushes desk-nap Kage!” Ailani: “Who wouldn’t pick the Kage that was so cute?” And Kage gets the interior beat I asked for: “They joked about ‘first-year him’ as if he didn’t hate that version of himself. A part of him wanted to say, ‘Have you guys forgotten I’m right here?’ but he didn’t wanna wipe the smiles off their faces. … But he did want to yell at Ailani for calling him cute. Abby called him that once, too. His hand drifted onto the whistle before he even noticed.” That’s exactly right. Nobody in the conversation picks the Kage standing in front of them. The one who would have (Abby, who called him cute in the maid photo) is gone, and his hand finds her.

Two smaller changes. Geneki now quotes the rule correctly (“anyone who loses in the first round is disqualified”), and then: “For once, Gene didn’t sound like she was talking to the cameras.” That one line gives Gene a real mother under the announcer. And Zulfiya’s entrance is now ominous: “the same hair that had wrapped around him in the nurse’s office. Zulfiya smiled at him, but the smile seemed off.”

Details I caught.

  • “You need to be more careful with your powers, hun. I thought I taught you better.” Gene corrects her daughter in front of millions.
  • Kage: “Concrete is like the strongest thing I can make!” He’s comparing himself again.
  • Ailani: “It’s hard to dance when you’re that bulky.” That’s the second dance clue.
  • Geneki: “Mother, you said that anyone who loses in the first round is disqualified from the Elite Program entirely… I wish to go home now… Alone, preferably.”
  • Reina: “That…could’ve been me…”
  • Merch: “hero program T-shirts, cups, hats, and souvenirs galore!” Huoyan “slid his phone back out of his pocket.” He’s still looking for Abby.
  • Anubis: “If Kage lost in the first round, I’d probably give him a similar look. What a failure that would be.” That’s foreshadowing for S4 Ch6.
  • Huoyan’s math: “If someone has half the potential as Ozul, and they’ve done a year more work, then they’re winning. They have triple the training time. One times one is less than zero point five times three.” Anubis: “by your logic, if Kage has three times their potential instead of twice as much, then he would surpass them all.” Huoyan: “If Ozul had three times, that would make him equal.” I checked it, and Huoyan’s math is right: 3 × 1 = 1 × 3. Anubis “was never big on math unless it came to money.” That’s a nice character joke built on correct arithmetic.
  • Huoyan: “That level of training might end up regressing him.” Anubis: “Sometimes you need to break to grow back stronger.” He doesn’t know Kage was literally broken and regrown.
  • “People aren’t into the whole ‘righteous perfect man’ anymore. They want someone they can relate to.” “A kid willing to do most anything to win a fight. That’s a good hero, no?” “Most anything” is exactly what Thanatos bought.
  • “WAZZZUPPPPPPPP!!!” “It’s the red to my blue!”
  • Inazuma knew Kage in middle school in Japan. “You should’ve seen the surprise on my face when this bum said he wanted to go to America.”
  • “I knew he would do nothing, but yeesh. I didn’t expect him to literally spend all last year with his head on his desk.” Inazuma knows about the desk from somewhere. Texts? Gossip? He’s been keeping tabs.
  • “I sent him that ‘Remember this?’ pic of him in a maid outfit a few months ago. Never got a reply, though…” That pays off S1 Ch5 word for word.
  • Ailani: “I always loved laughing at seeing his different-colored costumes.” So she visited the café.
  • Kage hates “first-year him” but laughs along to keep their smiles. That’s a small, kind moment.
  • “Big Vance.” “My last name is Sokudo, after all!” Sokudo means “speed” in Japanese. He’s the other speedster from S1 Ch2.
  • “This big black dolt.” Kage “didn’t even blink at the comment” and leaves. Ailani: “That’s a little racist, Inazuma.” “Oh, don’t be a snowflake!” “Orion’s jaw tightened, but he didn’t say anything. He just took a slow breath.” Notice who doesn’t say anything. Kage, who will scream at Zulfiya in five minutes for saying “support,” doesn’t defend Orion here. That’s worth a beat later. Orion is the one who has to absorb it.
  • Orion: “Kage might be rubbing off on him too much.” Orion is fighting the urge to laugh in Inazuma’s face.
  • “Your ‘aura’? Oh, quit the stupidity.”
  • “Hi, King.”

What it’s doing. It’s an intermission that introduces a disruptive new character and sets up Orion’s match.

What works. Inazuma pays off two separate seeds from Section 1. The math scene is a smart way to show Huoyan is right without making him unlikeable.

What I’d push on. The title note is fixed. The one thing I’d add is a consequence for Kage’s silence during the racist comment. Orion takes it alone, and Kage walks off to find Lance and Rebento. The next chapter shows Kage has a temper for exactly this kind of moment. If the book means for him to stay silent, let Orion notice it, even in one line.

Score 8.5/10 (up from 8). A good intermission that pays off two seeds and now earns its title.

3.4 Forfeit Versus Failure

3,124 words. Arena: Kage vs. Zulfiya; Elias vs. Cheyenne.

What happens. Orion and Ailani plan to watch whether Zulfiya’s hair can diagnose Kage. Zulfiya forfeits before the count ends, publicly, to support her “King.” Kage grabs her shirt and screams at her to take it back. Ailani and Orion debate whether this is grief or something worse. Lance and Cheyenne turn out to be dating. Nobody knows Elias’s power. Orion guesses strength and advises Cheyenne to strike first. Elias freezes her mid-dash, gives her a speech, banks a flurry of blows, and releases them all at once, sending her into the wall.

What changed in this draft. You took my note on Orion’s non-plan: “‘It’s the only plan I have that’s foolproof,’ Orion said with certainty. Ailani’s eyes narrowed at him. For a guy with no plan, he sounded weirdly relieved about it.” That’s the line. Ailani now suspects Orion as well as Kage. The book has two investigators, and one of them is hiding the answer.

The other thing I’d call important (I missed it on my first read) is Zulfiya’s hair, and it opens a new mystery. Before the fight she’s “tucking her blue hair behind her ear.” After the forfeit, Ailani complains: “No hair wrapped around him even a little. Not even a strand.” And on the walk back: “Her hair was a different story. It stayed tucked behind her ear the whole walk, and not a single strand drifted his way.” Zulfiya’s healing hair is also a diagnostic tool, and she’s keeping it away from him on purpose. Combine that with “the smile seemed off” at the end of S3 Ch3 and “King, I didn’t think this would end up this way. I do apologize,” and her forfeit reads differently. She may not be forfeiting to support him. She may be forfeiting so she never has to touch him in front of millions of people. See Theories.

Two smaller lines worth noting: “He’d turned one baseball into a whole game plan, and Cheyenne was the one who paid for it.” That’s Orion’s guilt in one sentence, and it’s the second time in two sections that his plan got a girl hurt. And after “Kage doesn’t know anything about anyone!”: “But he’d spent a whole year in the same classroom as these people and barely learned a thing about them. That one stung.”

Details I caught.

  • Orion knows Zulfiya read Kage’s temperature “to the exact degree” and his recovery “to the exact minute.”
  • Ailani: “Woah, woah, woah. Kage collapsed?” She didn’t know. “That idiot…” Orion: “This isn’t about you right now.”
  • “We’re not spying… We’re just…watching for the nuances.”
  • Huoyan: “If you tell me to take notes one more time, I will burn this lobby down.” “Technically, I’ve never told you to ‘take notes.’”
  • “Sup, Zebra! Eh, nah. Sorry, I’m not the greatest with nicknames.”
  • “King, I didn’t think this would end up this way. I do apologize.”
  • The forfeit. “Kage Ozul is my King… publicly showing my support.”
  • The word “support” makes his skin crawl, and his hand goes to the whistle. That’s Abby’s “number one supporter.”
  • “YOU’RE NOT GOING TO LIVE YOUR LIFE TO SUPPORT MINE!… SAY IT’S CUZ YOU KNOW I’M STRONGER!” This is the most revealing outburst in the book. He’d rather be called stronger than let anyone sacrifice for him again.
  • Anubis stops Huoyan: “PR nightmare.”
  • Zulfiya “focused on his eyes. They weren’t angry. They were distressed.” She calms him by saying what he needs.
  • “Never say you are doing anything to exclusively support me ever again.”
  • Orion: “second time Kage had an outburst in the past hour.”
  • Ailani: “Maybe he’s still just hurting from Abby.”
  • Orion: “Kage is civilized enough to not grieve by lashing out like this… He’s more emotionally intelligent than he looks.” Orion defends Kage’s character in the act of investigating him.
  • “Something no one can come up with an excuse for. Not even him.” “So, you want us to wait for him to get worse?” “It’s the only plan I have that’s foolproof.” Ailani’s skepticism is right, and now she’s suspicious of Orion’s relief too.
  • Cheyenne: “Partner, I gotta go out there and fight the weird kid now.” Lance: “Of course you can win, babe!”
  • Rebento: “Do you two have to shove how I’m single in my face every single day?”
  • “Kage doesn’t know anything about anyone!” It’s played as a joke, and the narration now says it stung.
  • “I saw him with a baseball once… flying out of the building. He didn’t have a baseball bat.” Orion’s wrong guess about Elias (strength) leads to bad advice, and Cheyenne gets flattened. Orion puts his head in his hands.
  • Kage wonders if Elias “actually wanted to be a hero or just be Lucifer’s babysitter.” He’s more right than he knows.
  • Cheyenne dashes at “one.” “Technically, the rules only said they could start as soon as Gene said so, and Gene had definitely said ‘one.’” Cheyenne is a rules lawyer. So is Kage. They’d get along.
  • “Her name is Cheyenne Bandalore! Her enhancement is—.” The power card gets cut off mid-sentence when Elias freezes her. The narration itself gets frozen. That’s the best meta-joke in the book, and it keeps Cheyenne’s power a mystery. Don’t explain it until it matters.
  • “Why in the world would you ever dash towards me like that?… I truly feel bad for you.”
  • “You’re making my clock tick down rather fast. You are strong. That’s very admirable.” Elias compliments her right before he hurts her. The clock line is a clue: her struggle is burning his time, which means she was fighting back hard.
  • Elias’s card: Chronal Inertia. Touch stops an object “dead in spacetime,” forces are banked and released all at once, and each hold has a three-minute clock that only burns when the world pushes back. His glasses are Rhatella-made and track each clock.
  • Lance runs down to help Cheyenne.
  • Gene: “The semi-finals of this half will take place after the next half!” The bracket logic is spelled out.
  • Elias and Kage lock eyes. “Elias pushed his glasses up with one finger. Neither of them looked away, and the crowds and conversations around them faded out.” The chapter ends on the name of a power instead of a person: “Kage’s first real battle of the tournament was going to be against Chronal Inertia.”

What it’s doing. It’s two fights that are really about watching. Orion watches Kage, Elias watches everyone, and Kage starts to understand he’s being watched.

What works. The forfeit scene is the emotional high of the first round. The interrupted power card is inspired. Elias’s power is one of the best-designed in the book: clear, limited, and creative.

What I’d push on. Both my notes are answered. If I’m picking at something, it’s the Lance and Cheyenne reveal (“babe”), which is played entirely for Rebento’s reaction. Cheyenne is about to be flattened. One beat of Lance being scared for her before the match would make his run to the arena hit harder.

Score 9.5/10 (up from 9). The interrupted card is still the best meta-joke in the book, and Zulfiya’s hair is now the quietest new mystery.

3.5 Just Like You Both

1,187 words. The lobby.

What happens. Inazuma complains that every fight has been “nothing but talking.” Ailani sends him away. Kage defends Inazuma (“devoid of a filter”). Ailani says Inazuma reminds her of Kage. Orion agrees, then asks Kage flatly if he’s racist too, and brings up Kage breaking Ailani’s heart. Ailani slaps Orion. He apologizes, and he resents having to. Kage tells Orion to let the comments “slide off.” Orion is called to fight Inazuma, offers a handshake, and gets slapped away.

What changed in this draft. The line about Orion apologizing was rewritten (it now quotes the slur he’s apologizing past). On the reread, Orion’s half of the scene was stronger than I gave it credit for. His apology carries his other guilt: “Maybe Inazuma is just getting to me. Cheyenne getting hurt on my advice isn’t helping either.” His accusation has a better delivery: “Orion’s voice never rose, and somehow that made it worse.” And after Kage’s “let them slide off,” the new ending to that beat: “Orion didn’t answer. His hand balled into a fist at his side before he made it let go.” That’s the grip motif, given to Orion for the first time. Kage’s hands can’t hold on. Orion’s have to make themselves let go.

Kage’s defense also has a reason that’s harder to dismiss than I said last time: “he was nice to me when nobody else was,” and “he texted me all of last year. Even when I never texted back.” The orphan defends the one person who kept writing. It doesn’t excuse him, but now you understand him.

Details I caught.

  • “This whole tournament has been nothing but talking!” That’s a meta-joke, and a risky one, because the reader might agree.
  • “You two really are heroes to me.”
  • “He texted me all of last year. Even when I never texted back.” Inazuma was the only person reaching out, and loyalty can blind you.
  • “He’s just devoid of a filter.”
  • “Yeah, that’s not an excuse, Kage.”
  • “He kind of reminds me of you.” “He’s like you if you had Orion’s enhancement!” That’s the chapter title, and it’s a sharp observation.
  • Orion: “Are you racist as well, Kage?” It’s said “plainly,” and it isn’t really a question.
  • “You broke Ailani’s heart on two or more occasions, and you enjoy fighting me because you like to ‘prove you’re better.’”
  • Ailani: “CRETINO?!”
  • “He was the one Inazuma had called a ‘big black dolt,’ but now he was the one apologizing? Whatever calm he’d scraped together started slipping again.” This is the book noticing something real and unfair.
  • Kage: “Let them slide off you, okay?” It’s terrible advice from the boy whose power is making things stick. Whether or not the irony is deliberate, it works.
  • Kage’s warning: “You think Inazuma is like me. If that’s the case, then you know Inazuma would do anything to win.” He uses Ailani’s insult as tactical advice. That’s very Kage.
  • “May the faster man win,” with a hand out. Inazuma slaps it away: “A speedster should never make things go slower.”
  • “He’d overheard Orion and Kage’s argument over him… and that—that made Inazuma grin.”

What it’s doing. It sets up Orion’s fight as an emotional fight, not a speed fight.

What works. The book handles the racism plot honestly. Orion doesn’t get a clean resolution. He gets asked to apologize while the person who hurt him gets defended. That’s a true-to-life injustice, and the book lets Orion feel it.

What I’d push on. Still the same note, though the fist is a good start. Kage’s defense of Inazuma needs a consequence, and it gets only a partial one. This is the place where Kage could learn something about loyalty and harm, and right now he doesn’t. If that’s saved for later, good. If not, it’s a loose end that will bother readers. Orion should get a moment where Kage actually hears him. As of S4 Ch7 it hasn’t come.

Score 8.5/10 (up from 8). It’s short and uncomfortable, deliberately, and Orion’s fist is the best new beat.

3.6 Faster Than Fast

2,497 words. Arena: Orion vs. Inazuma.

What happens. Kage refuses to watch. He’s working on something with glue and asks Ailani to keep Orion from “doing something dumb.” Inazuma hits Orion with lightning, then with words: “Heroes don’t run from danger.” Orion freezes, remembering running from Kage in the dome. Orion fights angrily and gets shocked and beaten. Ailani shouts encouragement. Inazuma makes a “gun to your head… criminal” jab. Orion catches his kick, punches him in the groin, reveals his hero name is Hermes, and kicks him out. Kage shouts Orion’s own old lines back at him. The lobby objects to the low blow, and Anubis tells everyone that real fights have no rules.

What changed in this draft. My note was that Orion should notice his own feelings about the low blow. Now he does, in the right register for him: “He said it as calmly as he said everything, but he couldn’t tell if he meant a word of it.” That’s perfect. Orion’s control is a surface, and the book now shows the reader the gap under it.

Three more things I caught on the reread:

  • During the freeze, Inazuma’s taunt from the start of the fight echoes in his head: “He turned to the stands in an attempt to lock eyes with Kage, but Kage wasn’t there. ‘He must not like you.’ Only Ailani stood there looking back at him.” The insult gets a second hit, and it lands at the worst moment.
  • Kage’s glue project gets a clue: “He just stretched the glue between his two fingers, frowned at it, and shot a quick glance across the lobby at Lucifer before squishing it back into a ball.” So whatever he’s building, he’s building it for Lucifer. He doesn’t fight Lucifer until S3 Ch15, but Lucifer’s fire is the obvious problem for a boy whose glue vaporizes in heat (as Abaddon proved in S2 Ch12).
  • Lance’s late “Yes, sir” now has a target: “His eyes weren’t on Anubis, though. They were on Kage, the one who’d started all this talk about villains not caring about honor.” That sets up the hotel talk in S3 Ch13 directly.

And Ailani’s Spanish got corrected: “EVERYONE CALLENSE!” is the plural command, which is what you’d yell at a room. The last draft had the singular “cállate.” (Strictly, it’s “cállense,” with an accent, but in all-caps dialogue that’s a style choice.)

Details I caught.

  • Kage: “Inazuma is a character that causes stupidity out of others. He’s a rage-baiter.”
  • Ailani: “I want to know when you became such a high and mighty leader.”
  • “I’m not the only one who went through something traumatizing that night. As perfect as he seems to be, I’m sure he’s still hurting. Help him for me, Ailani. Please.” “Goosebumps grazed his skin” while he says it. Kage notices Orion’s pain, and the biomass flickers.
  • Ailani checks his forehead “for a fever,” which is really a probe: “‘Something off about him is bound to show up somewhere’ was her thought process.”
  • “Damn, you haven’t touched me this much since we were together.”
  • “While you play with your arts and crafts.” That’s the S2 Ch3 title, coming back.
  • “You’ve been talking to Kage more than me for the past six months.”
  • Inazuma’s card: Lightning Fast. Friction static is stored and released as lightning. He has to discharge or he burns out.
  • “You didn’t train for that speed! You just came out that fast.” “Heroes don’t run from danger.” Inazuma is guessing, but the jab lands exactly on Orion’s secret.
  • Orion’s legs “vibrate in a way he’d never really felt before. The feeling of wanting to run, but standing your ground anyway.”
  • He looks for Kage in the stands, and Kage isn’t there. “Only Ailani stood there looking back at him.” That’s devastating.
  • “I was born with the name Sokudo, and you were born with the chance to try and reach what I was predestined to do!”
  • “How’s this for a snowflake, huh?!”
  • “You really are just a big black dolt.”
  • “When you end up a criminal, you’ll get used to that.” The narration says plainly that this is racist, with “enough deniability that the crowd wouldn’t be able to call it.”
  • “His only beacon of hope during these past few days of despair.” That’s Ailani.
  • Orion’s “almost Kage-esque smirk.” “Your perception of me is off… And, your timing is predictable.” He turns his own catchphrase into an insult.
  • The low blow: “You should’ve kept yourself protected.”
  • “I’m pro hero…Hermes.” The name Kage gave him.
  • Kage: “DUDE, THAT HERO NAME IS TERRIBLE! PICK ANYTHING ELSE!” Orion: “WHAT?! I LIKE IT!” Kage: “I’M NOT YELLING THAT IN A CRISIS!” These are Orion’s S1 Ch6 lines, word for word, with the roles reversed. This is the best callback in the book.
  • Lucifer: “Yo, if you try low-blowing me I’ll beat your fucking ass.”
  • Kage: “You think villains care about honor?” He’s speaking from experience the room doesn’t know he has.
  • Elias: “So, you’re comparing Inazuma to a villain?”
  • Lance: “the antonym of clean.”
  • Ailani: “EVERYONE CALLENSE!”
  • Anubis: “If I were the one who needed rescuing, I wouldn’t care if you needed to shoot the villain.” “Win by any means necessary.” Lance’s second “Yes, sir” comes late, and he’s looking at Kage.

What it’s doing. It’s Orion’s fight against his own guilt, and the moment the tournament’s ethics shift.

What works. The callback ending is perfect. The freeze, when Orion hears “heroes don’t run” and looks for Kage, is the best piece of interiority Orion gets in the tournament. And Anubis’s speech makes the institution’s values explicit right before Lance challenges them.

What I’d push on. The note is answered. The only thing left is Kage’s defense of the low blow (“You think villains care about honor?”). It’s a great line because the reader knows why he knows. But nobody in the room reacts to how sure he sounds. Elias asks the right question (“So, you’re comparing Inazuma to a villain?”), and Kage deflects. Let Elias file it, the way Orion files things. He’s the one who reads liars.

Score 9.5/10 (up from 9). Ends on the best callback in the book, and now Orion has an inside.

3.7 Gone In An Instant

2,436 words. Arena: Rebento vs. Alexander; Ailani vs. Youseff.

What happens. Lance worries about Anubis’s speech. Alexander Lund freezes in front of the crowd, turns when his mother cheers for him, and Rebento blows him out of the ring with a kissed gumball. Anubis complains. Huoyan says, “Pacifists make great rescue heroes.” Kage watches Alex’s mother. Lance blames Kage’s “combat philosophy” for Rebento’s cheap win and challenges him to fight with honor. Kage apologizes, and Lance won’t accept it until he sees it. Then Youseff Nasser, Anubis’s brother, pretends to trip out of bounds at the bell and laughs at Anubis.

What changed in this draft. Last time I wrote that Lance “doesn’t know he’s saying it to an orphan who was just staring at someone’s parent.” The book now lets Kage feel it: “Kage’s eye twitched at Lance’s parent comment, but he tried to let it slide off him. The same thing he’d told Orion to do before.” That’s a great callback, and an unkind one. The advice he gave Orion two chapters ago (“Let them slide off you”) doesn’t work for him either. The boy whose power is making things stick can’t make anything slide.

Details I caught.

  • Rebento pops a gumball. “Fai,” “Che,” “Lancey.”
  • “You couldn’t drag me away from Fai with a tow truck.”
  • “A cowgirl massage.”
  • Rebento’s theory: “Kage using Orion as a shield to talk to Ailani… They just want the same thing.” That’s accidentally insightful about the love triangle.
  • Cheyenne: “We got together because I was friends with Rebento and he introduced us.” So Rebento introduced Cheyenne and Lance, and now he’s lonely around them. That’s sad and funny.
  • Alexander Lund: flower earrings, green hair, anxious. He gets no power card. He’s “bested” before we learn anything. That fits his whole experience.
  • His mother: “You can do it, sweetie!!! I’m so proud of you for trying!” Proud of him for trying. She’s the only parent in the book who is unconditionally proud.
  • Rebento kisses the gumball.
  • Rebento’s card: Sweet-Toothed Surprise. Glucose becomes kinetic charge. Rhatella built the gumball machines. He can’t charge living things: “he tried to charge Lance once during an argument.” That’s a great aside.
  • Huoyan: “I teach all heroes, not just ones who punch well.” “Pacifists make great rescue heroes.”
  • Kage: “Oh, his mom cost him.” “His hands drifted towards the glass as his eyes stayed fixed on that front row.” It’s the orphan looking at a mother. Ailani notices. (This is the third time in this section his hands go to the glass: Severine, now Alex’s mother, and later the crowd. The glass is the wall between him and the people he can’t reach.)
  • Lance: “You made it so we can’t even risk turning to our parents for a moment.” Lance doesn’t know he’s saying it to an orphan who was just staring at someone’s parent. Kage’s eye twitches.
  • “We’re not classmates here, Lance. Just opponents trying to get to the next round.” Lance: “You only just started talking to most of the people here this year!”
  • “Fight them how you’d want to be fought.”
  • “What if I end up against you?” “Then I’d want you to fight me the way I’m asking you to fight everyone else.”
  • Lance just walks away. “Kage braced for an arrogant comeback, but he was left with nothing.” Disarmed.
  • “I’m sorry! You’re…you’re not just an opponent to me!”
  • “We’re cool when I see you winning fights with honor. I don’t think you’re a bad person, but I can’t associate with you right now.”
  • Rebento, being scolded, “blew a bubble right in the middle of it. Kage could’ve sworn Lance’s voice went up an octave.” That’s the funniest small line in the chapter.
  • “Did you just apologize?” “Maybe I should check for a fever again.”
  • Gene broke the order because “if I can’t have favoritism towards my daughter, then we can’t have favoritism towards your brother.”
  • Kage thinks Volkan is Youseff. “He was the slow kid who Huoyan made run!” That pays off S1 Ch7.
  • Orion: “You are so off topic that it amazes me you’re tactical.”
  • Youseff: small, brown-skinned, about 5’3”, twiddling his thumbs, picking his nose. The crowd chants “Nasser!” Kage: “He’s living what I want and he’s just chilling?!”
  • Anubis gives two thumbs up. Youseff doesn’t wave back.
  • “OH NO! I’ve tripped and fallen out of bounds!”
  • Huoyan smirks and shrugs.
  • “I hope the Elite Program is fun for you. Because it sounds like torture to me.”
  • He lets Ailani’s crystal hit him and laughs at Anubis behind the soundproof glass.

What it’s doing. It fills out the bracket and gives Kage his moral challenger.

What works. Alex’s mother is a small masterpiece. In one line she shows everything Kage doesn’t have, and the book lets Kage stare at her without comment. Lance’s challenge is the right kind of conflict, a disagreement between two good people.

What I’d push on. Last time I worried that Ailani never got to fight. S4 Ch7 answers that: her match with Lance is the best-choreographed fight in the book, and it’s worth the wait. In hindsight, Youseff’s fake trip was the right call, because it saved her first real fight for the moment the reader most wanted it.

Score 9/10 (up from 8.5). The best of the anti-fight chapters, and Kage’s twitch gives it a sting.

3.8 Dinner Needs A Show

1,461 words. The hero lounge.

What happens. Anubis panics about the boring first round, refuses to cancel because of refunds, and dismisses Huoyan’s search for Abby as “not important right now.” He summons Lucifer, who plans to beat Reina without using his power. Anubis begs him to put on a show. Lucifer agrees, on one condition: if he beats Kage, or if Kage loses to Elias, Anubis stops grooming Kage. Anubis agrees, and adds that if Kage beats both, Lucifer is never his successor. Lucifer adds that Anubis can’t tell Kage or coach him. Kelly returns from escorting Severine. Lance vs. Volkan is called.

What changed in this draft. Lucifer’s lines were rewritten (“Cruz” instead of “Reina,” and a new argument). Beyond that, four things are either new or things I missed last time, and one of them is a major clue.

  1. Lucifer’s tail. When he asks “What’s in it for me?”: “Behind him, his tail started to sway. It moved slowly and heavily, like it belonged to something much bigger than him.” The tail has been the honest part of Lucifer all book. Now it’s Abaddon’s tail, wagging. That’s chilling, and it means the thing inside him likes this deal.
  2. Lucifer uses Anubis’s rule against him. “You said, ‘Win by any means necessary.’ It’s best if I win by not using my enhancement.” Two chapters after Anubis gives that speech, the top student turns it into a reason to deliver a boring fight. Anubis’s philosophy and Anubis’s business model are in direct conflict, and Lucifer is the first to notice.
  3. Gene heard everything. “She heard every word, though. Her smile stayed bright, but her whiskers gave one sharp twitch, and she didn’t look at Anubis once.” This is the first sign that Gene isn’t just a cheerful announcer. She built the Elite Program, and she just watched its biggest sponsor trade a student’s future for ratings. Watch her.
  4. Kelly has an enhancement, and he used it. “I had to use my enhancement on her cuz she wanted to start a full-on fight with me.” We still don’t know what it is. And: “He’d passed Lucifer in the hallway, and the kid had been grinning like he’d already won the whole tournament. Kelly glanced from Anubis’s smirk to Huoyan’s frown and decided he’d ask later.” Kelly reads rooms too.

Anubis also has a flicker of feeling before accepting: “anger stirring in his own chest,” and he answers “through gritted teeth.” That’s a partial answer to my note from last time (see below).

Details I caught.

  • “He spits in my face with a ridiculous display!”
  • “We have to give refunds to everyone!”
  • Huoyan’s predictions: Lucifer will forfeit against his girlfriend, and Elias will beat Kage with one touch. He’s wrong on both.
  • “Fans are in the stands eating overpriced food.” The chapter title is literal: a dinner that needs a show.
  • Huoyan: “I’m asking staff and personnel if there’s been any word of Neosame.” Anubis: “That’s not important right now!” That’s the most damning line Anubis has.
  • Lucifer expects “a nice offer.”
  • “I plan on fighting Cruz without using my enhancement.” “If I use my enhancement on Cruz, my chances of beating her plummet.” This is a setup for Reina’s heat absorption.
  • Huoyan smiles at the tactic.
  • “What’s in it for me?” said with “a grim excitement.”
  • “You stop grooming Kage for the top. Let him fall where he deserves to fall. Have him leave the school as the nobody he came in as.”
  • Anubis: “I wouldn’t have too much of an issue accepting your terms.” It isn’t framed as a reluctant concession. He’s open to it.
  • “If Kage beats Elias in a beautiful showcase to millions of people… You can’t drag that kind of momentum down.”
  • “If Kage manages to beat both of you, then I’m never considering you as a successor again. You’ll officially be Huoyan’s problem.” That’s an insult to two people at once.
  • “You’re not allowed to tell Kage about our talk… I want to see him struggle hard in front of all these people.”
  • “Sticky Situation doesn’t need my help.”
  • Huoyan: “Khaled…” He’s interrupted, and his phone buzzes “with another reply about Abby.” While Anubis sells a student’s future, Huoyan is still looking for the dead one.
  • Kelly: “That Neant girl fought me all the way back to her dorm.”
  • “Hope we saved the best for last.”

What it’s doing. It reveals how much Anubis’s support for Kage is worth: about one good fight.

What works. It’s short, cold, and clarifying. The deal gives Section 3 a hidden clock that only the reader and the adults know about.

What I’d push on. The anger and gritted teeth help, but the very next beat undoes them: “He smiled back at Lucifer. He was a fan of this negotiation.” If he’s angry, he isn’t a fan. If he’s a fan, he isn’t angry. Pick one, or make the switch visible (he’s angry at being cornered, and then he sees the angle and starts enjoying it). That switch would be the most frightening thing Anubis does in the book.

Score 9/10 (up from 8.5). The betrayal Kage doesn’t know about yet, now with a wagging tail and a witness.

3.9 A Lighter Crown

1,525 words. The lobby; Lance vs. Volkan begins.

What happens. Rebento and Cheyenne reassure Kage that they still like him. Cheyenne quotes his King speech back to him and asks about the whistle. Orion steps in to stop the questions and tells Kage, “People can’t know about Abby yet.” Rebento gets in Orion’s face. Cheyenne tells Kage to take the King thing slow, and to “enjoy being a prince.” The match starts: Lance draws a beam rapier, and Volkan hides in his hoodie.

What changed in this draft. Lance’s power card was quietly rewritten, and it now matches S4 Ch7 exactly. The blade “saws through almost anything rigid” (it used to “cut”), and his limit is “a point where he decides that he needs to stop” (it used to “choose”). Both words matter. “Saws” sets up the cast-saw mechanic that Ailani exploits in S4 Ch7. “Decides” makes the limit sound like something he controls, which is what Lance wants everyone to believe, and S4 Ch7 shows it isn’t quite true.

Also: Volkan “just wanted to last long enough for them to remember his name.” That line pays off in the next chapter word for word.

Details I caught.

  • “Since we met you properly, you’ve been pretty cool. Even if you got Lance to yell at me.”
  • Cheyenne: “When I met him he was a bit of an asshole… then I saw the way he felt about himself.”
  • Ailani: “DID HUOYAN BOW?!”
  • Cheyenne quotes him: “‘People will call me King Kage one day. That’s not arrogance, by the way. It’s a spoiler.’… it felt like someone who really knew what they wanted to be.”
  • “It felt like that speech was the last time he’d ever told the full truth, and he was unsure if he even believed that truth anymore.” That’s one of the most important sentences about Kage in the book.
  • He “clutched Abby’s whistle, then caught himself and tried to tuck it back under his collar, but Cheyenne clocked it quickly.” He’s started hiding the whistle. A week ago he wore it like a medal.
  • “Is that whistle from them? That…person you said you wanted to show you were a real hero?” Cheyenne connects the whistle to the store victim. She’s wrong about whose it is, but the whistle now does belong to a “soul up above.”
  • Zulfiya’s eye opens. She wants to know what the whistle is.
  • “Orion’s laughter cut off the instant he heard Cheyenne’s question.” Kage looks up and is “looking at Orion’s back. He’d stepped right in front of Kage.” Orion: “Hey, could you relax with all the probing?” He’s protecting Kage, and also protecting his own secret.
  • “People can’t know about Abby yet. After the tournament, Kage.”
  • Cheyenne: “You’re the type of person that Lance can’t help but gravitate towards, I reckon.”
  • Rebento: “I’ll explode you here if you talk like that to her again.” Orion: “You’re such a bluff.” “Quickster!”
  • Ailani “wasn’t a fan of how Orion handled the situation” and stays behind to keep an eye on Orion: “The last time Orion got into it with somebody today, it ended with a low blow in front of the whole stadium.”
  • Kage: “I don’t think I’ve ever seen Orion protect me like that.”
  • Kage steals Cheyenne’s hat, the one “she never took off,” and puts it on. She laughs and takes it back. That’s intimacy through a prop.
  • “You can take the time to enjoy being a prince, alright?” “Prince Kage for now.”
  • Volkan isn’t used to crowds, or to using his power in front of people.
  • “A show was asked of me, and a show I shall give!”
  • Lance’s card: Avalon’s Legacy. Beam blades: “The blade saws through almost anything rigid!” But flesh “moves with the blade instead of parting for it” and gets a searing strike instead of a cut. Rhatella made his hilt. “There seems to be a limit to Lance, a point where he decides that he needs to stop even when his hilt and hands are fine, but people aren’t quite sure why!” Paid off in S4 Ch7: the limit is “King Arthur.”

What it’s doing. It’s a pressure-release chapter that gives Kage a softer title.

What works. “A Lighter Crown” is a lovely title for the chapter where the King is offered a smaller crown and accepts it. Cheyenne gets real depth: she noticed the tears in S1 Ch11, and she’s the first person to tell Kage to slow down without condescending to him.

What I’d push on. Still the same note. Orion shutting down the questions is the right move for his character, but it’s the second time in a short span the group circles the whistle and Abby without anyone asking the obvious follow-up: where is Abby? Somebody in the student lobby should notice she’s missing out loud. Huoyan is the only one searching, and he’s upstairs.

Score 8.5/10 Quiet and warm, with one sentence that matters a lot.

3.10 Honoring A Snail’s Pace

1,996 words. Arena: Lance vs. Volkan.

What happens. Lance’s blade clanks off Volkan’s iron shell. Volkan, a “Volcano Snail,” rolls into him. Lance switches to a greatsword and hits with the flat, sending Volkan rolling toward the edge, until Lance catches him barehanded. He offers Volkan a free win if he lands one hit. Volkan refuses (“I want to earn it! I’m not just the slow kid!”). Lance explains his sword could kill Volkan, Volkan gives up, and Lance knights him in front of the crowd, which chants “Knight Volkan.” Volkan rolls out, happy. Kelly calls the remaining fighters Anubis’s “pawns.”

What changed in this draft. The sword rules got tighter, and they now set up S4 Ch7.

  • When Volkan’s roll knocks him back, “Lance drove his sword into the ground to stop himself from flying out. The blade slowly sawed a long groove through the arena floor until his momentum finally died.” Slowly. The blade is a saw, not a guillotine.
  • Lance’s explanation is now specific: “Most of your body is rigid, and rigid is exactly what my blade saws through. Why do you think I switched to a greatsword? It was for its mass, not its sharpness.” Then Volkan does the math himself: “The snail’s eyes slid to the long groove Lance’s sword had sawed into the arena floor. That could’ve been him.… If Lance fought him for real, Volkan wouldn’t lose. He’d die.” Last time I accepted “My sword could slice through you and kill you” as consistent. This version is better, because the groove shows the danger instead of stating it.
  • Kelly’s view of Huoyan’s phone now comes from Kelly: “Kelly could tell Huoyan was desperate enough by now that even finding her body, God forbid, would be a relief. But there was just…nothing to find.” And then the tenderest small beat between two adult men in the book: “Huoyan reached for it, then let his hand drop.… Kelly set the phone face down on his own knee. For once, Huoyan didn’t reach for it.”
  • Volkan’s despair inside the shell: “His one chance to make anyone remember his name was stopped before it had even started. And then something did stop. Not his chance. Him.” That’s a lovely sentence-level turn.
  • The payoff: “All he’d wanted was to last long enough for them to remember his name. Now the whole stadium couldn’t stop shouting it.” He “mouthed the words ‘thank you’ before curling up and rolling out on his own terms.”
  • Gene “still hadn’t looked at Anubis once.” The thread from S3 Ch8 continues.
  • Kelly’s pawn line now ends on Huoyan: “Over at the window, Huoyan gave a stiff, dissatisfied nod.” Huoyan agrees with Kelly. Silently.

Details I caught.

  • Volkan’s card: like a scaly-foot gastropod, a real deep-sea snail with an iron shell. He’s heat resistant (from hydrothermal vents) and has gills. “He may be slow to move, but he tries to think fast!” His name works on two levels: Volkan is Turkish for volcano, and salyangoz is Turkish for snail. That’s well done.
  • “My sword isn’t supposed to clank off skin. What the hell is under that hood?”
  • Kelly loves watching the tournament fall apart.
  • Huoyan: “Good job not forcing your son to compete. This whole thing is a shit show.” Kelly: “Woah, did you just curse?” (“Your son” is new, and it’s a small clue: Huoyan knows Kelly’s son didn’t come because Kelly didn’t make him. That makes the son a choice, not an accident. See Theories.)
  • Huoyan’s phone: “There was no sign of where she was. No trace of her in the city.” There’s no body.
  • Kelly takes the phone: “Try and relax.” Kelly is kind here, quietly.
  • Huoyan: “I could never get through to that kid.”
  • “Inside his shell, Volkan felt tears coming.”
  • Lance stops a roughly 500-pound rolling shell with one hand: “Lance was braced against the shell with one hand, grinning down at him.” “Lance’s legs were shaking, and his heels sat right on the edge of the ring, an inch from the grass.” He nearly ring-outs himself to save his opponent. “Me?”… “Yeah, I’m cool!”
  • “Volkan, we’re classmates! Just call me Lance!”
  • Kage: “If that’s what he calls honor, then I’m screwed. I’m not saving him from a loss if we end up fighting.”
  • Huoyan: “I didn’t teach him that… But, he isn’t doing it in a bad manner, I suppose.”
  • “I want to earn it! I’m not just the slow kid!”
  • Lance’s knighting sword is held “just above Volkan’s left shoulder, careful not to let it touch the iron.” Even in ceremony, he respects the rule that could kill.
  • “I’m just a snail…”
  • “Sir Salyangozu.” … “To me, you are much more than a snail.… A knight!”
  • “Knight Volkan, Knight Volkan!”
  • “The new knight felt like he was glowing on the field.”
  • Kage: “I’m not a knight; I was crowned prince by you.” Cheyenne: “If Lance can knight people, wouldn’t that make him a king?” “Even one follower each.”
  • Anubis: “The matches before this were all just palate cleansers!”
  • Kelly: “Yeah, and now every kid left is a pawn putting on a show for you, huh, Anubis?” “Pawn” comes back from S1 Ch13, from a hero this time.

What it’s doing. It’s the honor chapter. Lance shows what he means, and the crowd loves it.

What works. The knighting is the most purely joyful moment in the tournament, and it’s earned because Volkan was a punchline (late, forgotten, mistaken for someone else) for two sections. The Arthurian layer (Avalon’s Legacy, Lance as Lancelot, knighthood) is consistent and charming.

What I’d push on. Lance’s honor is admirable, and it’s also performative. He does it “for a show.” The book seems aware of this (Anubis loves it for the wrong reasons). After S4 Ch7 this note matters more, not less. We now know Lance has a berserker state called King Arthur that makes him “fight-starved.” A man who carries that inside him and spends this fight refusing to commit to a single real cut is holding something back with both hands. One beat here, a flicker of the yellow eyes when he has Volkan at the edge, would make S4 Ch7 land as a payoff instead of a surprise.

Score 9.5/10 (up from 9). The tournament’s warmest moment, and now the sword rules are on the floor where everyone can see them.

3.11 Studying Is Important

3,928 words. Arena: Kage vs. Elias.

What happens. Gene swaps the order so Kage fights Elias before Lucifer fights Reina, because she hated Anubis’s deal. Huoyan and Kelly physically stop Anubis from going down to coach Kage. Kage’s real plan is to stay calm so the biomass doesn’t surface. He runs at Elias on “one” to feel the power. Elias freezes him and gives the crowd a long speech about how Kage doesn’t deserve to be there, then banks a flurry of blows. Kage sticks to the wall when released, steals and breaks Elias’s glasses, overloads him with tape, glues himself to Elias’s arm, and finally, while frozen, sprays a tidal wave of glue because excretion is an involuntary process. He survives the final launch with a snapping strand of tape and lands in bounds: “I remembered to eat breakfast!” Elias yields.

What changed in this draft. You turned my main criticism of this chapter into a plot point, which is the best kind of revision.

The speech is long for a reason now. Last time I said Elias’s speech was too long and asked you to cut it by a third. Instead, you made the length part of the fight: “Elias noticed. He adjusted his glasses and double-checked the readout. He’d planned this speech to end right as the hold did, but Kage hadn’t struggled once, and a hold nobody fights barely burns its clock. He’d been talking for over three minutes, and there was still time left on it. He wasn’t about to let the crowd watch him improvise, so Elias decided to put a button on it.” That’s brilliant. Kage’s calm, which he’s using to hide the biomass, is the exact thing that stretches his humiliation. The longer he holds still, the longer Elias has to keep talking. The reader’s impatience with the speech is now Elias’s problem too. Keep the speech the length it is.

The clock is visible. The old “1:15 of 3:00” readout is replaced by Kage’s view: “he was close enough to see the glasses. Little numbers ticked across the lenses, way too slowly, and he had no idea how much longer he’d have to sit through this public dismemberment.” “Dismemberment” is a deliberate word for a boy who was dismembered a week ago.

Gene answers Huoyan. Last time I flagged that Gene didn’t answer “you’re gambling with Kage’s future now.” She still technically doesn’t (“Gene didn’t answer his question. She just made a statement”), but the statement is the answer: “Anubis is the one who bet the kid’s future, not me. I just made him roll the dice before he gets paid. Isn’t it great that now he gets to sweat through the whole match with nothing in his pocket?” And then the key detail: if Elias wins fast, “It’d be an easy veto.” So Gene has a veto over who enters the Elite Program. At the end of the fight: “Her whiskers gave one sharp twitch. So much for an easy veto.” That’s the second whisker twitch (the first was in S3 Ch8, and in between, in S3 Ch10, she “still hadn’t looked at Anubis once”). Gene is now a real player.

The echo is exact now. Elias tells Cheyenne in S3 Ch4: “You don’t know my enhancement? That’s unfortunate, but it explains your naive charge.” Kage tells Elias here: “You don’t know my power? That’s unfortunate, but it explains your naive charge.” Last draft, the two lines were slightly different (“naive charge” versus “naive run in”). Now they match. That’s continuity work paying off.

Other beats worth noting (some new, some I undersold last time):

  • Kage’s plan is now stated as a priority list: “His real plan had nothing to do with beating Elias. Winning would be nice, sure, but staying hidden came first.”
  • “Kage watched every hit land and felt none of them. Not yet.”
  • After he breaks the glasses: “Elias’s hand twitched toward his face before he caught it and lowered it again.” Then later, at the worst moment: “Elias’s hand rose to adjust his glasses only to find nothing.” Two reaches, the second one unchecked. Good build.
  • “No glasses meant slower math, so Kage was gonna give him a whole lot of math.”
  • “Kage laughed as he stood back, mostly so nobody could see his hands shaking.”
  • “Lucifer wasn’t angry at Elias. He was angry that his deal could be breaking in real time.” And his tail “lashed harder with every second of it.”
  • “He slowly stood up and braced for more boos. They didn’t come. The same crowd that had been booing him a few minutes ago was on its feet, cheering.” The crowd’s fickleness is the tournament’s theme in two sentences.
  • “Kage turned back to Elias, this time with a finger gun. ‘So, you quit?’” That’s Anubis’s gesture from S1 Ch2. Kage doesn’t know he’s copying it.
  • The ending: “Instead, he checked in with his own body. His heartbeat was slowing down. Nothing black was leaking out of him. As far as he understood, nobody knew a thing.”

Details I caught.

  • Gene: “Did you forget this is MY tournament?”
  • Huoyan: “It was hard to pay attention with how much you were quivering.”
  • Gene: “I absolutely hated the deal he made with Lucifer… I’m not going to gamble away a kid’s future… a captain always goes down with their ship.” Huoyan: “You’re gambling with Kage’s future now. What makes this different?” Gene’s answer is a non-answer that answers (see above).
  • “Sticking it to Anubis is fun…”
  • Anubis “stared at Gene for a bit. He knew something was up… Another thing filed for later.” Orion files things. Now Anubis does too.
  • Huoyan: “I may not agree with Lucifer, but I defend my students.”
  • “WHAT’S THAT OVER THERE?!” Anubis is held back and goes limp. It’s the most undignified the number-one hero gets in the book.
  • Ailani: “You’re shaking, Kage…” He pushes her off.
  • “The biomass flared up whenever he got hurt or his heart started racing. So, if he wanted to get through this fight without exposing it, he had to go all in on tranquility. Total calm.” He has to stay calm to hide the monster.
  • “Make sure Lance watches this one!”
  • Lucifer: “If the order could change this easily, who knew what else Anubis would try to wriggle out of?” He lies to Elias about why the order bothers him. Elias “could tell.”
  • Kage runs on “one,” copying Cheyenne on purpose, to learn.
  • Elias’s speech: “I’m not an egotistical person… I’m not doing this to embarrass Mr. Ozul here. Well, the second half is partially a lie.” “This man does not deserve to be here!… This man went to a hero school for food instead of the goodness in his heart.” That’s true, according to the Prologue. Elias is cruel and accurate.
  • He lists thirteen classmates he studied. He leaves out Youseff. Huoyan: “Heh, he didn’t say your brother’s name.” Did Elias consider Youseff not worth studying, or impossible to read?
  • Alex’s mother is excited somebody studied her son.
  • “Most people look at a car crash as they drive by, but what do they do after? They drive off.”
  • “I hope your fifteen minutes of fame were fun.”
  • “I’m sticky, Elias!”
  • The glasses have Rhatella’s face on them. He hesitates, then breaks them anyway. This becomes Lance’s whole complaint.
  • Huoyan smiles. Kelly catches it.
  • “I almost didn’t…stick…that landing.”
  • Elias: “I don’t need those to see. They just help me calculate things faster.”
  • Elias tracks which tape strips he’s released. He’s still sharp without the glasses.
  • Kage glues his hand to Elias’s arm at the moment of contact. “If you strike me and I get launched, you’re coming with me.”
  • Lucifer: “He better.”
  • Elias stops freezing and just dodges, which cuts his mental load in half. That’s smart.
  • “This is the third time I’ve been held down by you, Elias. I get your power now.”
  • “My power… It’s basically just excretion. Y’know, super similar to sweating. That’s a natural internal process.”
  • “You don’t know my power? That’s unfortunate, but it explains your naive charge.” That’s Elias’s line to Cheyenne, word for word, turned back on him.
  • “Elias’s hand rose to adjust his glasses only to find nothing.” That’s a perfect beat.
  • “You quit?” “No, I don’t.” “You can’t save yourself anymore, Ozul.”
  • A thin strand of tape to the dome: “He had so little left that the strip was barely a thread, and mid-swing it snapped.” He lands “on the very edge of the white concrete, still in bounds.”
  • “I remembered to eat breakfast!” Orion smiles. Ailani hugs Orion.
  • “I yield, Kage Ozul.”
  • Lucifer: “DAMMIT! THAT FUCKING ANT IS UNBELIEVABLE!”
  • “The biomass survived unexposed, and that was his top priority.” Winning is second.

What it’s doing. It’s the best-designed fight in the book, and the chapter where Kage is fighting two opponents at once: Elias and his own body.

What works. Everything about the fight. Kage runs on “one” not because he’s reckless, but because it’s the fastest way to learn the rules. The excretion solution comes straight from S1 Ch2’s power card. The breakfast line pays off a joke from 70,000 words earlier. And the hidden constraint (he can’t struggle, or the biomass shows) makes Elias’s humiliation speech twice as painful.

What I’d push on. My old note is withdrawn: the speech’s length is now part of the mechanics, so cutting it would break the fight. If there’s anything left, it’s that Kage’s first hesitation over Rhatella’s face on the glasses goes by in one clause (“There was a moment of hesitation”). That stamp matters again in S3 Ch12, when Lance brings it up. One more word of what Kage thinks when he sees it would make Lance’s accusation sting more.

Score 10/10 Still the best fight in the book, and better than it was.

3.12 The Whole Truth

2,022 words. After the fight; the bus; the hotel.

What happens. Gene announces the rest is tomorrow, with two students to a hotel room. Ailani refuses Orion’s plan to spy on Kage overnight and rooms with Orion instead, to watch Orion. Kage offers Elias friendship, and Elias declines in a precise breakdown of why he can’t trust Kage: Kage’s behavior while frozen shows he was hiding something. Kage claims Lance as his roommate. On the bus, Elias pulls Lucifer’s horn (“What did you do?”), apologizes to Lucifer and Reina for losing, and Reina realizes both boys are keeping something from her. At the hotel, Kage eats the entire minibar.

What changed in this draft. Elias’s dissection got one new passage, and it’s the most dangerous paragraph in the book for Kage’s secret. The old version ended its reasoning with “That conflict is internal, not external.” The new version goes further: “But, that conflict would live in your head. What I felt under my hold was in your body. Something in you was pushing against my enhancement while you sat perfectly still.” Elias didn’t just infer that Kage was hiding something. He felt it, through the hold. His power freezes a body in spacetime, and something inside Kage’s frozen body kept pushing. That’s the biomass, detected by touch, by the one classmate smart enough to keep thinking about it. Elias is now the person most likely to figure out the truth before Kage tells anyone.

Kage’s reaction is new too, and it’s the right one: “The crowd was still roaring somewhere behind him, but it sounded muddled and far away. Goosebumps prickled up his arms. If he argued, his heart could start racing, and he couldn’t risk another outburst, not from his mouth and definitely not from the biomass.”

Two small fixes. Gene now explains why Lucifer vs. Reina got pushed to tomorrow (“That fight ran us right up to closing time”), which closes a logistics gap. And Ailani’s room choice now has a stated cover and an unstated reason: “Youseff really pissed me off earlier,” Ailani said. “She didn’t mention the other reason.”

Details I caught.

  • “It might be a viable strategy to spend the night with a future opponent.”
  • Ailani: “Absolutely not… I’m spending the night with you.” Orion turns red. Her real reason is to keep an eye on him.
  • Elias: “You are tactically more in tune with yourself than I gave you credit for.” “Is that your way of saying you like me?”
  • Kage: “The Kage you were criticizing doesn’t exist anymore.” “The guy I’m trying to room with tonight keeps telling me to be more respectful, so…this is me being respectful.” He’s already planned to room with Lance. “I’d really like us to end up friends one day.”
  • Elias’s dissection: “I cannot be friends with someone I cannot trust.… Why not struggle out of my holds?… it seemed like a part of you wanted to struggle, and you had to actively fight against that urge… Something in you was pushing against my enhancement while you sat perfectly still.… either I am unable to trust you because you’re illogical, or I’m unable to trust you because you’re hiding something. The worst part is, I believe the answer is both.” That’s exactly right. (Kage asked for friendship, so “friends” is a better word for Elias to refuse than the old “partners.”)
  • “Agreed. That was the most logical thing you did all battle.” Breaking the glasses was logical. The scoff is “a bit less dismissive.”
  • “I CALL DIBS ON SPENDING THE NIGHT WITH LANCE!”
  • Rebento: “Your new boyfriend, Lancey!”
  • Cheyenne: “I’ll have you six feet under in less than two shakes of a lamb’s tail.” Kage: “I have no idea what that means!”
  • Elias “grabbed onto one of Lucifer’s horns and pulled his head up. ‘What did you do?’” He knows Lucifer well enough to read guilt.
  • Elias apologizes for losing: “I know we all wanted to go as a group.”
  • Reina: “Whether we get in the Elite Program or not, we’ll still have each other!”
  • “Reina had to look at two boys who were both treating her like she didn’t deserve the whole truth.” That’s the chapter title, and it’s the first time Reina’s point of view gets real weight.
  • Room assignments: Alexander and Elias (a silent night, Alex there because his mom made him stay), Reina and Lucifer, Zulfiya and Volkan (distant), Cheyenne and Rebento, Ailani and Orion, Lance and Kage.
  • “After the Elias fight, her worry for Kage diminished a bit, and her worry for Orion only climbed after the day they had. She knew Inazuma had gotten in his head in a way that she hadn’t seen before.”
  • The minibar: “Every soda, candy bar, chocolate milk, all of it.” He unplugs the fridge and moves it next to his bed. Lipids are fuel, and he grew up poor. Both explain the hoarding.
  • “An orange slice wedged in his mouth like a grin.” That’s a great image.
  • “I’m so glad you asked, Sir Lance.”

What it’s doing. It’s a cool-down chapter that sets up the night’s most important conversation.

What works. Elias’s breakdown of why he won’t trust Kage is one of the smartest passages in the book. It shows that Elias is the most dangerous person to Kage’s secret, more than Orion or Anubis. The room assignments are a neat device for showing the class’s social map at a glance.

What I’d push on. Still the same small thing. The room-assignment paragraph summarizes four relationships quickly. Zulfiya and Volkan “didn’t speak much” is a missed opportunity, especially now that Zulfiya is hiding her hair from Kage (S3 Ch4). Give her one line to Volkan that shows she’s thinking about it.

Score 9/10 (up from 8.5). Elias’s breakdown now includes the one thing Kage can’t explain away.

3.13 A Kings’ Quarrel

2,046 words. Hotel room, Saturday night. (Unchanged in this draft.)

What happens. Kage demands to know if his Elias fight was “honorable.” Lance says no, because he broke Elias’s glasses. Kage defends himself by saying the glasses were Rhatella’s and he’s one of her only friends. Lance compares him to Anubis: he only cared how it looked. Kage asks what’s wrong with being like Anubis. Lance asks why he wants to be number one, and then tells his own story: his father was a villain in Quebec who taught him to steal, until Lance, watching a frog approach some ants, realized he’d always hated it. They end up laughing. Kage promises no more gear-breaking and his best effort. Lance promises to stop deciding who deserves what.

Details I caught.

  • “Indubitable!” Lance: “That’s not even correct.”
  • “You destroyed your opponent’s gear without a second thought, Ozul.” “Oh, now I’m Ozul?” “I had a second thought, but it had nothing to do with Elias.” The second thought was Rhatella.
  • “You took advantage of him by taking away the thing that makes him better than you.”
  • “He’s not some random villain.” Every time Lance says “villain,” it lands differently for the reader.
  • “What disadvantage did you have?” “The question hit Kage like Lance was using one of his swords.” He can’t tell the truth (the biomass), so he tells a half-truth: “I was the only one who fought a semi-final that hadn’t used their power all day.”
  • Kage takes Lance’s hilt from his bag to show him Rhatella’s stamp. That’s not honorable either, and he apologizes for it at the end.
  • Lance has never really spoken to Rhatella: “She just handed me the hilt.”
  • Kage on Rhatella: “The only thing she loves doing is creating gadgets for people because that’s the only way she feels close to them. I’m probably one of two or three people she can call her friends.” Kage understands Rhatella better than the book has shown him spending time with her. It works because it’s also true of Kage.
  • “Okay, Anubis.” Lance’s two-word takedown.
  • “What’s wrong with being like Anubis? I want to be number one.”
  • “He’s not the guy who saves the most people.”
  • “I want to be number one because it’s the only way to make up for the things I’ve done.”
  • “Same as you. I gotta make up for the wrongs I’ve done.”
  • Lance’s backstory: “My dad was a villain.” A “high earning villain in Canada… Quebec” (Faisceau is French; that fits). “He taught me how to steal, how to do heists, best ways to escape, breaking and entering without a trace.” “It was the only time he spent with me.”
  • Kage “was curious what that feeling felt like.” What it feels like to have a father teach you something. He holds it in. That’s the orphan again.
  • The ants and the frog. “I walked over and turned the frog another way, towards the pond… I had saved those ants without even thinking about it.” That’s the best origin story in the book: small, specific, and moral.
  • “I was romanticizing every moment with my dad… a part of myself felt like dying.”
  • “I try to hold people to the standard I didn’t hold to myself.”
  • “Someone like you, who I see a lot of good in, would be better off trying to win against the odds instead of turning them in your favor.”
  • “You don’t sound egotistical while doing it. You just can’t see yourself ending up any other way.”
  • “You don’t want to be the next Anubis, do you? I figured someone like you’d want to be better than Anubis.” Kage laughs: “You’re right, Lance.”
  • “I am really bad at saving people. Everyone I’ve tried to save, it never ended well for them.” He squeezes the whistle. “When someone’s in trouble, and they arrive, you immediately know things are going to be alright.” That’s Abby’s sentence, in his mouth.
  • “Your smile is pretty comforting. It wouldn’t work if your smile was creepy.” That’s foreshadowing for his bloody grin the next day.
  • “It’s getting harder and harder to turn off the fighting brain as of late.” The biomass again.
  • “Your face is pretty punchable.”
