  • “Don’t give me your credit card, rich boy.” So Lance has money. Villain money?
  • The promises: no breaking gear; “my fighting will be the same quality as the Elias fight”; and from Lance, “I’ll try and stop deciding who deserves it.”
  • “Sorry about grabbing your hilt. That wasn’t very honorable either.” “No, it wasn’t, Kage.”

What it’s doing. It’s the book’s moral conversation, a debate between two good kids with opposite methods and the same wound.

What works. This is one of the best dialogue scenes in the book. It’s funny, then honest, then funny again. Lance’s backstory lands because it’s specific (Quebec, heists, ants). And the shape of their friendship (Lance the villain’s son choosing heroism, Kage the hero choosing villains in secret) is perfectly mirrored without anyone saying it.

What I’d push on. Kage’s half-truth (he “hadn’t used their power all day”) is a nice dodge. But give the reader one line of Kage almost telling Lance the truth. Lance just confessed his father was a villain. If ever there were a person Kage could confess to, it’s Lance. Showing him come close and back off would make the secret heavier.

Score 9.5/10 The heart of Section 3.

3.14 Honor Is For Suckers

1,566 words. Arena, Sunday morning: Reina vs. Lucifer. (Unchanged in this draft.)

What happens. Kage falls asleep on Lance’s shoulder on the bus. Elias asks Lucifer to beat Reina quickly so he can study Kage, and Lucifer says he’s going to have fun instead. Kage sits next to Elias to watch. Lucifer opens by flooding Reina with fire, which she absorbs and turns into muscle. Reina’s Rhatella-made heat pack lets her shrink to dodge and regrow to grab. Lucifer overfeeds her heat to slow her, then smashes her support item. He hesitates, then kicks her out of the ring and looks to Anubis for approval instead of to her. Gene, still messing with Anubis’s plans, sends Lucifer straight into his match against Kage.

Details I caught.

  • Fans were promised entry at six a.m., so the students were brought in early.
  • Kage sleeps on Lance’s shoulder. Cheyenne and Rebento take photos. Lance doesn’t push him away.
  • Gene: “We brake for nothing and no one!” The pun is probably intentional and it’s funny either way.
  • Reina kisses Lucifer’s cheek.
  • Elias: “You could crack him open for me.” Lucifer: “Sorry, Min. I’m going out there to have some fun.” Elias is “more perplexed with that answer than anything.” He doesn’t know about the deal.
  • Kage, “circulating some glue in his hand,” sits by Elias: “Scooch over?” Elias reaches for glasses that aren’t there.
  • Huoyan: “Here it comes. You’re getting exactly what you wanted, Khaled.”
  • Reina’s card: Heat Charge. She absorbs heat and turns it into muscle, shrinks as she punches, and gets dizzy when empty. “She’s the only one in the world that can truly say they can handle Lucifer’s heat!” That pays off Lucifer’s “She can take my heat” from S2 Ch5. She’s the one person whose power cancels his.
  • “Awww, babe you shouldn’t have!” in a cute voice from a hulking body. That’s a fun contrast.
  • She juggles him like a footbag and finishes with an overhead soccer kick.
  • Rhatella’s heat pack (Cruz, C, comes before O).
  • “THE FUCK WAS THAT?!”
  • Lucifer’s plan: overfeed her so she’s big and slow, then smash the gear. “Kage turned to Lance… and he saw Lance’s hand covering his face in indignation.” Lucifer breaks gear the morning after Lance lectured Kage about it.
  • “Sorry, Cruz! I gotta move on!”
  • Reina: “Heh, Lu! That was really…good…” “Lucifer wasn’t even paying attention to her. His eyes were on the teacher lobby with Anubis giving a thumbs up.”
  • Kage: the coming fight “made his skin crawl in a way that was oddly alluring.” The biomass again.
  • Kage laughs at Elias. “Fight hungry? Possibly.” Elias is frustrated he can’t read him.
  • Gene “knew that she had to keep throwing wrenches in Anubis’s”fool-proof” plan.” This reads differently now that S3 Ch8 and Ch11 have shown her whiskers twitch. She isn’t being random. She’s sabotaging him on purpose.
  • Lucifer looks at Kage instead of Reina: “Elias recognized the look, recognized the way it matched his own with Kage’s yesterday.”
  • “WAIT! WHAT?!” They say it in unison.

What it’s doing. It shows what Lucifer is willing to sacrifice for Anubis’s approval, which is Reina.

What works. Reina is great here. Her power is clever, her personality is a delight, and the moment where Lucifer ignores her after the win is one of the coldest beats in the book. The title is ironic (honor is for suckers), because the lesson of this chapter is that dropping honor costs you people.

What I’d push on. The title is a little on the nose. Consider something that points at Reina, since she’s the one who pays. For example, “Good Luck, Babe,” from her line in the next chapter (“good luck babe! Love you!”).

Score 8.5/10 Reina deserved better, and the chapter knows it.

3.15 Paid In Exposure

2,386 words. Arena: Kage vs. Lucifer, part one. (Unchanged in this draft.)

What happens. Kage nearly skips to the arena. His friends gather to wish him luck, and he hands Abby’s whistle to Orion for safekeeping. On the field, Lucifer is furious that he’s had no rest. Elias predicts Lucifer will lose. Kage and Lucifer clash in midair. Lucifer headbutts him and burns his face. The biomass regrows it in the smoke, and the teachers notice something feral in his eyes. Kage rips off his shirt and taunts him, shows off fire-proof caulk, then challenges him to fight without powers. They trade punches without dodging until Lucifer knocks out Kage’s tooth. Kage spits the tooth into his eye, knees him, mounts him, and headbutts him bloody, laughing. He whispers: “Give…me…Abaddon.” And then: “Or, I’ll knock you out and bring him here myself.”

Details I caught.

  • “This is an advantage being handed to me on a silver platter. I’m taking it! Right, Lance?” Lance gives a thumbs up. Lance’s ethics are flexible when it’s Lucifer.
  • The well-wishers: Ailani, Zulfiya, Cheyenne, Lance, Volkan, Rebento. “Volkan was the one that made him feel the most emotional.” Someone he barely knew acted like his friend.
  • He gives away the whistle. “Keep that safe for me, okay? I don’t want it getting damaged.” He’s protecting it, and he’s also taking off his anchor right before the fight where he loses himself.
  • “It’s Prince Kage, Zulfiya!” “Prince doesn’t sound too bad!”
  • Lucifer: “That rat bitch upstairs is playing games!” That’s about Gene.
  • Reina: “Good luck babe! Love you!” She still says it after he ignored her.
  • “Merch numbers were up whenever Lucifer was fighting.”
  • Elias: “Ozul’s brand of chaotic is actually chaotic in a way many other ‘drunken’ fighters aren’t… He’s illogically intelligent.”
  • A collar-and-elbow tie-up. That’s a wrestling hold, and it’s a good detail.
  • Anubis hands Huoyan a beer: “You know I barely drink anymore. I’m around children all day.” Huoyan used to drink more. Kelly confiscates it: “A shit brand you chose, anyway.”
  • Kelly: “That Lucifer kid is a monster in the making!” That’s dramatic irony.
  • Lucifer cuts off Kage’s first syllable with a headbutt. Kage never gets to speak first in this fight.
  • “THEY’RE GONNA HAVE TO STITCH THAT FACE OF YOURS BACK TOGETHER!” Lucifer burns his face. “Overkill even for Lucifer.” In S3 Ch16, Abaddon reveals Lucifer assumed the nurses could fix it.
  • The biomass: “Black tendrils spewed from Kage’s mouth and nostrils… Burned tissue slagged off in slabs of skin.” It’s body horror hidden by smoke.
  • “Something in Kage’s eyes seemed off. The emotion looked almost…feral.”
  • “Kage’s hand twitched on the way up to his hair.” He reaches for the whistle. It isn’t there. “A soft laugh.” Without his anchor, he goes.
  • He rips his shirt off.
  • “What’s the problem?… You afraid?” Lucifer is backing away.
  • Huoyan: “He got burned when I shot a fireball at him.” That’s a callback to S2 Ch2.
  • Anubis: “A HERO WHO HAS SECRETS IN HIS POCKET IS THE BEST KIND!”
  • “DO IT AGAIN!”
  • Fire-stop caulk. It’s a real material, and that’s what he was making while he ignored Orion’s fight. In this draft, S3 Ch6 has him glance “across the lobby at Lucifer” while he works on it, so the payoff is now set up. And “Kage threw the glue he had in his hands away” on his way to the arena: he finished it.
  • The explanation “dragged… akin to some mad scientist talking about their greatest creation.” The narration notices his voice changing.
  • “No powers for a bit! Just hand to hand combat!” He throws away the advantage he just showed. That isn’t strategy. It’s ego.
  • Ailani presses her hand to the glass, the same way Kage did for Severine: “I think he’s…trying to prove he’s better. Not the better fighter, better in every category.” “He’s not…acting himself. He’s worse.”
  • “Like two rams running into each other to assert dominance.”
  • The tooth. It gets knocked out, spat into Lucifer’s eye, and in S4 Ch4 it turns out not to grow back.
  • He headbutts again and again. “Mothers began to avert their children’s eyes.” Alex pushes his mother’s hand away so he can keep watching. That sets up what happens next.
  • “I promised Lance I’d beat you at your best. This isn’t your best.” He uses Lance’s honor code as a weapon to summon a demon.
  • “Lance… didn’t feel like the Kage he had spoken to last night.” Cheyenne hides under her hat. Zulfiya covers her mouth.

What it’s doing. It’s the exposure of Kage’s monster, in front of millions.

What works. The escalation is excellent. Every step (losing the whistle, the laugh, the shirt, the caulk, dropping powers, the headbutts) is Kage getting worse on purpose, and the narration lets the friends say it out loud. The twist of using Lance’s honor code to justify summoning Abaddon is dark and smart.

What I’d push on. The fire-stop caulk rant is described as dragging, which is the point, but the reader only gets its first half before the narration summarizes. Let the reader hear a little more of it, so they feel the change in his voice for themselves.

Score 9.5/10 Kage’s worst self, on live TV.

3.16 Giving Into Yourself

3,662 words. Lucifer’s mindscape; arena: Kage vs. Abaddon. (Effectively unchanged in this draft.)

What happens. Inside Lucifer’s mind, in his childhood kitchen, Abaddon sits in a child’s chair. He shows Lucifer the night Kage kidnapped him and argues that they’re one being, that Lucifer is “a secondary player in Kage Ozul’s story,” and that letting him out is the only way to beat Kage. Back in the arena, the crowd cheers the brawl. Lucifer looks at Elias, mouths “I’m sorry,” and sets himself on fire. Abaddon emerges in front of the world. Reina didn’t know. Elias runs to the bathroom. Anubis knew, and celebrates the ratings. Kage breaks his own wrists over and over to push Abaddon to the edge, runs out of energy, and gets his arm snapped as the world watches it heal. Kage claims he summoned Abaddon to protect the crowd, while unable to meet Abaddon’s eyes. He chokes Abaddon with tape while being slammed into the floor again and again. Seeing Alex’s frightened face, he sticks out his tongue, and Alex laughs and starts a chant. Both fighters black out at the top of the dome. The biomass heals Kage completely. Gene rules that Lucifer went out first. Orion raises the whistle alone.

Details I caught.

  • The childhood home. Abaddon at the kitchen table “in a child’s chair.” That’s a brilliant image. The monster sits in the place the child used to be.
  • “Can we relax on the yelling..?” Abaddon switches to “I” in here, and mostly keeps using “I” in the arena afterward (apart from his entrance line, “ABADDON IS IN THE FLESH ONCE MORE!”). See Continuity: I think this could be a deliberate change.
  • “That’s because I’ve been holding you prisoner here.”
  • “You may have been put to sleep a few days ago.”
  • “Being out again after years was a dream indeed!” “After years.” Abaddon has been out before. See Theories about Elias.
  • “That red one is impervious to damage… Immune, child! He repairs himself in unimaginable ways… His enhancement seemed like he threw sap everywhere.”
  • “He shouldn’t have a healing enhancement.” Lucifer’s reaction is the reader’s reaction.
  • “That level of regrowth requires energy. The more he breaks, the more fatigued he will be.” That’s the rule the fight then follows.
  • “I am tired of living my life watching yours through this domestic kitchen window!”
  • “Holy shit. That means Min was right about the claymore. Kage was the one who was there that night.”
  • “If I let you out, the second you’re done with Kage you’re going to try and kill people!”
  • “You knew that blast to the face could be healed by one of the nurses’ enhancements at your school. I thought your killing instinct was finally arriving. How sad. You’re still weak.” So Lucifer burned Kage’s face because he assumed someone could fix it.
  • “You’re the weak virus scraping for nutrients in its body.”
  • “I am not a sickness or a disease! We are one!… You wear those devil horns, but you won’t accept your true self?”
  • “Why do you think you had so much energy as of late? It’s because I was freed!” That explains Lucifer’s Saturday energy.
  • “A bug that needs to be squashed before he cocoons and grows wings!” The insect insults finally get a metaphor that cuts the other way. Bugs become butterflies, like the one Ailani made.
  • “You’re a secondary player in Kage Ozul’s story. Hell, you might even end up his back up.” That’s meta, and it hurts, because it’s what Lucifer fears most.
  • “That’s what you promised Anubis, right?”
  • Abaddon kicks him out of the chair and through the front door.
  • Blood on both foreheads. “KEEP GOING! THIS IS AWESOME!” “Wrestlers who finally got color on camera.” “Color” is wrestling slang for blood. It’s an apt detail.
  • “For a moment, Lucifer and Kage smiled at each other.” They stop when they realize it.
  • “You having fun, Luci?” “Only Min gets to call me that.”
  • “I’m sorry,” mouthed to Elias.
  • He breathes fire on himself, and the smoke hides the transformation.
  • Reina: “WHEN COULD LU DO THAT, MIN?!” She didn’t know.
  • Elias “smacked it away… JUST WATCH HIM!” He runs to the bathroom. It’s the most emotional he’s ever been.
  • Anubis: “Yeah, I did. I didn’t think he could control it though.”
  • “THAT IS HOW YOU PUT ON A SHOW!” Huoyan is “dejected.”
  • “AND I’M STILL HERE! You ready for round two, demon?”
  • The wrist-breaking. “That quarter of an inch meant the world.” He breaks his wrist, heals, and breaks it again, over and over, pushing a ten-foot demon toward the edge a quarter inch at a time. It’s the book’s clearest image of how Kage approaches everything.
  • His temperature rises from hardening the concrete. That’s the S1 Ch2 cost, still enforced.
  • “YOU FOOLISH PEOPLE CHEERED THIS MAN?”
  • “Like a child would snap a pencil.”
  • Anubis: “HE CAN HEAL HIMSELF?! THIS BOY IS REMARKABLE! THAT’S YOUR STUDENT!”
  • Orion and Ailani: “whatever Kage was doing now had to have been what he did that night.”
  • Rebento: “What…exactly did we enable by letting him be here?… One week and he’s capable of being in the finals?” Rebento is the first to say what an outsider would think.
  • “You’ll try to attack them if I lose…won’t you?” “Your selective intelligence confounds me. So smart, and so stupid.”
  • “I’m the only one standing between this audience and possible death… fail to save people a third time… You’re gonna have to kill me!” It’s said “while unable to meet Abaddon’s eyes.” The narration flags it as a lie he’s telling himself.
  • “The truth is so terrible that you need to make all of this look like your grand plan?! OH, I LIKE YOU!”
  • The tape garrote. “You’ll pass out in a minute or so without air.”
  • Kage doesn’t heal, because healing would drain the last of his energy. He fights the biomass to keep fighting.
  • Huoyan: “He’s going to die down there.” Anubis grips his shoulder harder: “If he wanted to quit, he would have. He’s choosing to continue despite his body saying no. Isn’t that what being a hero is all about?”
  • Alex. “He smiled and stuck his tongue out at Alex.” Alex laughs, “and understood that was the point.” That’s the S1 Ch9 hero-name philosophy, carried out at the worst possible moment. It’s the best moment in the book.
  • “DON’T GIVE UP, KAGE! YOU CAN BEAT HIM!” “YEAH, KAGE! BEAT THAT DEVIL!” Then the whole crowd.
  • They black out at the top of the dome. “The biomass took over with nothing left holding them back.” Both are healed. Lucifer reverts.
  • “As stated in the rules, the ones I just made up.” Gene’s honesty is very funny.
  • Orion: “a smile… that he desperately didn’t want on his face.” He takes out the whistle, closes his fist around it, and raises it. “Orion’s fist was the only hand raised up high for Kage in that lobby.”

What it’s doing. It’s the climax of Section 3. Both boys give in to what’s inside them, and one of them still finds a way to be a hero inside that.

What works. Almost everything. The mindscape gives Lucifer real interiority. The fight is horrifying and inventive. The tongue moment is the thematic peak of the whole book. The last image, Orion with the whistle, is the most beautiful ending of any chapter.

What I’d push on. Gene’s ruling (“a split second before”) is fine as a joke, but Kage’s victory now depends on a referee’s discretion, and that’s something Rebento or Lucifer could fairly point out later. That’s a feature if you use it. And the mindscape argument runs long. Abaddon makes his case three or four times. Tightening it would make the kick through the door hit harder.

Score 10/10 The climax the first half of the book was building toward.

Section 3 overall

Score 9.2/10 (up from 8.9). Section 3 starts slow, deliberately, with a stretch of anticlimactic matches, and ends with two of the best chapters in the book. This draft’s revisions are concentrated in the first twelve chapters, and almost every one of them adds a watcher: Ailani squinting at Kage, Ailani squinting at Orion, Gene’s whiskers, Huoyan’s phone, Elias feeling something push back inside his hold, Zulfiya keeping her hair away. The tournament is now as much about who’s watching whom as about who wins. The middle is carried by the conversations between fights more than the fights themselves, and those conversations are where Lance, Cheyenne, Reina, Elias, and Orion become people. The Elias fight and the Abaddon fight are the book’s two best action sequences.

Chapter by Chapter: Section 4, “Ailani Shoma” (in progress)

Chapters 4.0 through 4.6 are unchanged in this draft, word for word. I reread them anyway, and I’ve added notes where the new Chapter 7 changes how they read.

4.0 Chapter NULL: The World’s Been Watching

1,124 words. Four rooms around the world, during the Kage–Lucifer fight.

What happens. Severine sips tea and decides their rematch is “an inevitability.” Rhatella sobs over her broken inventions while re-shipping the boys, wishes she were the one they fought over, and turns away at exactly the moment Kage’s arm heals on camera. In a bar, a drunk loses $300 on “demon boy,” and the man who won it calls Kage his student; his ice won’t melt. In a crappy apartment in Russia, Nerezza rewinds Kage’s screams, until Thanatos kicks in the door with a new assignment: kill another official pushing the “Security Accords.” She shows him the broadcast. He sees Kage and screams, “DAMMIT, FROST!”

Details I caught.

  • “NULL” is a great name for a chapter outside the count. It’s an interlude, a zero, and a programmer’s joke all at once. It also suits Section 4’s subject: a world watching.
  • A teapot “whistled.” That’s a quiet echo of Abby’s whistle.
  • Severine: “I suppose that would make him significantly superior.” That pays off S1 Ch8 exactly. “Ozul still won’t be able to hold a candle to my perfection.”
  • Rhatella: “Kage and Lucifer both break my stuff and now they’re breaking each other.”
  • “Could I have been wrong about my Orion x Kage assessment?”
  • “Reina throws a wrench in that idea, but he did break her—oh who am I kidding—MY wonderful machine.” She sees her gear as part of herself.
  • “THRUPLE!”
  • “Maybe that’s the next page in this zany manga!” That’s a wink at the book’s own format.
  • “All I know is I wish I was the one they were all fighting over…” Then: “Lord knows he wouldn’t take my heart!” Rhatella has a crush on Kage, or at least a hope she’s sure won’t come true.
  • She turns away exactly when the arm heals. It’s a comic near-miss, and it also keeps the one person with Kage’s pre-biomass blood sample in the dark.
  • The bar. “He’s just a…student of mine. You could say I’m something of his mentor.” The whiskey ice sphere stays whole: “It is a bit too warm in here, though.” That’s Frostbite. “He promised me he’d win. I’m holding him to it.”
  • “Commentators in English, Spanish, and Russian.” That’s a nice global detail, and it explains how Nerezza is watching in Russia.
  • Nerezza: “Children screaming in pain is next level television.” Put that next to Anubis’s “THAT IS HOW YOU PUT ON A SHOW!” from one chapter earlier. The book’s sharpest pairing.
  • “You know you can knock, Thanatos?!” “You’re getting soft. I would’ve been stabbed by now from doing something like that a year ago.”
  • “You never watch T.V. unless it’s the news.”
  • The Security Accords. “Another government official is in office and is already trying to push the Security Accords again.” “They’re fixated on getting these out so cameras are all over the hero city.” So the murdered official in S2 Ch6 was killed over surveillance, and the villains are fighting cameras. Meanwhile Anubis wants cameras in the dorms. The politics of the book are coming into focus.
  • “It’s annoying to look for you in Russia.”
  • “You have to earn that. You can sleep on the couch.”
  • “Your boy is putting on a show for the world.”
  • “DAMMIT, FROST! I’M GONNA KILL HIM!”
  • “You can kill him later. We need to have a serious talk first.”

What it’s doing. It widens the frame. Every faction is watching Kage now, and none of them want the same thing.

What works. It’s a great structural move. A chapter outside the count that shows the reader four audiences at once. Frostbite’s ice is a perfect reveal-by-detail. And the Severine opening ties off a seed with one sentence.

What I’d push on. Rhatella’s monologue is funny, but it’s the third time we’ve seen her ship the boys. The sad turn at the end (“wish I was the one”) is new and good. Get to it a little faster.

Score 9/10 The world, watching. A strong section opener.

4.1 Honey Toast

1,913 words. Flashback: first year.

What happens. First-year Kage, cat ears still in his bag from the maid café, tries his “Ozul Charm” on a girl at the field. Ailani knows who he is. He invents the ship name “Kailani,” an acronym (“H.O.T.”), and offers a bet: if he wins a fight, he gets her number; if he loses, she gets anything she wants. She knocks him to his knees with one crystal. She chooses honey toast at a bakery, takes his hand, and he lets go to stare at a cake. He can only afford the toast and a cup of water. She tells him she wants to be dependable for her family and become a top choreographer and dancer. She shares half the toast. He inhales it. She leaves her number on a napkin with a heart.

Details I caught.

  • “When he was in class, sleep. When he was at his dorm, sleep. All the rest of the time was put into work.” That’s first-year Kage. The “lazy” boy was working.
  • Cat ears. “Those guys giving me those fat tips.” It’s a funny world detail.
  • “I know who you are, Kage. You can run along and go take your nap.” His reputation got there first.
  • “Kailani” really does roll off the tongue.
  • “Those ladies in general studies.” Ailani has friends outside the hero course.
  • “That mirror definitely blushes every morning.”
  • “H.O.T. Honest, outgoing, and totally my type.” “You’re outside, so at one point you were ‘going out’. Therefore, you’re outgoing.” That’s a great dumb-smart joke and very Kage.
  • “Wouldn’t that last one be: ‘totally everyone’s type’ then?” She’s quick.
  • Jazz hands with tape confetti from his nails. It’s the playful version of his power.
  • “He wasn’t really sure what a fighting pose looks like.” First-year Kage has no idea how to fight.
  • Ailani’s card: Crystal Release. A chromatic sigil; range of 25 meters; large constructs hold for 10 to 15 seconds; overuse locks her hands from the fingertips to the wrists; she can forge weapons “as massive as a war hammer.” It’s a clean power with a clean cost. In S4 Ch7 both the cost and the war hammer decide her semi-final, exactly as the card promised.
  • “The freshman class’s Ailani Shoma!” The card is written in its own time period.
  • “You really are the whole package.” He flirts while unable to breathe.
  • “I don’t want to hurt you.” He was saying this on the day they met. In S2 Ch3, it’s “I don’t want to fight you.”
  • “She took hold of Kage’s hand.”
  • “It felt to him as if his hand was meant to be enveloping hers.”
  • “You let go of my hand to ogle at a cake? You know, you may not get the chance to hold it again.” This is the grip motif at its gentlest, and it’s a quiet prophecy of the breakup.
  • “I won’t let go a second time! I swear!” He does.
  • “One order of honey toast and… a water cup, please.” He couldn’t afford two things. That’s a quiet, sad detail about money.
  • Ailani’s why: “I want to be a hero to be dependable. I have a family at home and they deserve to feel safe.” “I want to be a top choreographer and dancer!” That pays off Anubis’s “other dreams” and the dance clues.
  • Kage’s why, back then: “Simple goals. I want money, and I want a woman.” That’s the baseline. Look how far he’s come, and in which directions.
  • She shares half the toast. He “vacuums” it. She wipes honey off his face: “I’m never gonna cook for you if this is how you eat.” It’s the honey and bread from S1 Ch3, finally on the page.
  • “Maybe I am [a tease].”
  • The napkin: name, number, a little heart.
  • “Don’t leave me hanging!”

What it’s doing. It shows the relationship at its start, so the reader understands what’s been lost and what Ailani can still see in him.

What works. It’s charming. Kage’s flirting is genuinely funny, and Ailani is clearly more than a match for him. The water cup is the best small detail in the section. This is the kind of chapter that makes a reader root for a couple.

What I’d push on. There’s one missed chance. This flashback is the perfect place to plant something about why they broke up, a crack in the honey. As written, it’s all sweetness, and the reader has to take S1 Ch3’s summary on faith. One small sour note (he checks out another girl on the way out, or misses the call he promised) would make it truer.

Score 9/10 The book’s best romance chapter.

4.2 The People Want More

1,319 words. Bathroom; student lobby; hero lounge.

What happens. Elias composes himself in the bathroom and comes back to find Kage and Lucifer gone. In the hero lounge, Anubis celebrates record merch sales. Kelly wakes both boys with smelling salts. They immediately try to fight again and ask, together, who won. Anubis tells them Kage did. Lucifer demands to know how Kage regenerates. Kage lies easily (“Tar Healing”) and explains knowing about Abaddon by citing Anubis’s “backstory” comment and the Bible. Kelly kicks him a minifridge. Both boys are wearing hero-course merch because their clothes were destroyed. Gene asks the crowd whether to keep going, and it roars.

Details I caught.

  • Elias “splashed his face… tried to calm his breathing by rubbing his temples.” Then he went “angrily stomping back to the lobby.”
  • “When Lucifer gets down here, he’s got a lot of explaining to do.”
  • “Merch sales are through the roof right now!”
  • Gene dumped a bucket of water on them, which did nothing.
  • Huoyan: “Why not just let them rest?” Anubis: “A breakthrough in his enhancement like regeneration only comes from extreme determination!” He’s wrong. It came from a villain.
  • Kelly’s “Irish remedy.” A smelling-salt vial. Iasacht is an Irish word, and this is a nice touch.
  • “THE FIGHT ENDED TEN MINUTES AGO!”
  • “OKAY! THEN WHO THE HELL WON?!” They say it together. These two are more alike than either would admit.
  • “You did!” Lucifer puts his head in his hands.
  • “Losing is a path to growth.” That’s aimed at Lucifer, since the deal is now void.
  • Kage: “I wouldn’t have people cheering me if I didn’t have a goliath to overcome.” He’s gracious in victory, a little.
  • Lucifer: “I exposed myself in front of millions of people and you couldn’t just lose?!” “You’re supposed to be the lazy, quiet kid.”
  • “The lying was coming to him more naturally than ever before.” That’s the narration naming the biomass’s effect directly.
  • “Tar Healing.” “The adhesive came out black.” It’s a smart lie, because it fits the visible facts.
  • “I wanted destruction, or as the bible would put it, I wanted Abaddon.” It’s a second smart lie, and Huoyan calls it “very articulate.”
  • Kage slips: “when we…fought…in the…school…yard…” He nearly confesses an old offense while covering a new one.
  • “Don’t snap at me like I’m your fucking dog.” “Sorry, I don’t mean to curse in front of kids.”
  • Lucifer looks at Anubis, asking with a look whether this is really the future. He “swallowed every curse word.”
  • Both boys are dressed in hero-course merch. The two biggest stars of the tournament are walking advertisements. That’s a perfect visual joke for the book’s theme.
  • “It’s not what I think. It’s what they think.”

What it’s doing. It’s the aftermath, and it shows Kage’s lying becoming fluent.

What works. The merch detail and the unison “WHO WON?!” are excellent. Kage’s lies are clever enough that the reader half-believes them, which is the point.

What I’d push on. Huoyan accepts the Abaddon explanation too quickly. He’s the most suspicious adult in the book, and he’s watched Kage’s body do impossible things. He should stay unconvinced, even silently. One look at Kage’s mouth, where the tooth is missing, would do it.

Score 8.5/10 The liar finds his voice.

4.3 Can You Outrun Explosions?

1,653 words. The student lobby.

What happens. Lucifer slams Kage into a wall and makes clear he knows Kage kidnapped him. Elias punches Lucifer, deliberately without freezing him, for summoning the demon, then hugs Kage. Lucifer confesses his deal with Anubis. Elias reveals he has seen Abaddon before and never wanted to feel that again. Reina learns both boys kept it from her, and goes to sit with Kage’s friends. Rebento accuses Kage of lying about his powers and calls a vote to get him out. Lance says he trusts Kage. Nobody votes against him, and everyone, including Elias and Lucifer, votes for him to stay. Rebento turns on Orion’s “secrets,” then heads out for his match.

Details I caught.

  • “I know you were in my dorm and knocked me out. What I want to know is why, Kage? Is lying a part of your training too?” Lucifer is sharper than he lets on.
  • Elias hits Lucifer without banking the force: “He wanted Lucifer to feel every hit.” That’s a great way to use a power to show character.
  • “You ignorant, selfish, inconsiderate devil!”
  • “This…moron tried to apologize to me before going through with it like that would make me feel better!”
  • Elias hugs Kage. “You’re lucky you’re alive… If you lost, I don’t think we’d be seeing you ever again.” The person who refused to trust Kage the night before is the first to hug him.
  • “I made a bet with Anubis that if I beat him he would stop scouting him.” “And, Anubis took that deal?”
  • Elias: “I don’t try and get a leg up through outside means. I handle things on my own.” That echoes Orion’s “I like doing things on my own” from S1 Ch4. Two characters who handle things alone.
  • “You lost to him! That’s not exactly handling it, Min!”
  • “I was worried I wouldn’t be able to keep watching you! I was worried that the demon might get out and I wouldn’t be there to stop it!” Elias’s whole role, in one line. He’s Lucifer’s jailer and his friend.
  • “You know I never wanted to feel that feeling again.” He’s been here before.
  • “Uhm, can I say something?” “NO!” “NO!”
  • Reina: “So, both of you knew about this and kept it from me?!… Okay, cool. I’m gonna go sit over there with Kage.” She grabs Kage by the ear on the way.
  • Rebento: “You’re not off the leash just cuz Lucifer’s got secrets too.” “His power is glue, dude!” “He’s now a wildcard.”
  • Lance hesitates, then: “I trust Kage.” … “I trust Kage deeply.” “He promised me three things… He did all of these things.”
  • “Like, when fighting sometimes you end up as someone else.” “I assume that happens to you as well.” That’s Lance’s seeded “limit,” almost explained, and he’s projecting onto Kage something Lance is afraid of in himself. S4 Ch7 confirms it: “It was what he’d meant when he told everyone that sometimes, when fighting, you end up as someone else.” The someone else is called King Arthur. Lance defended Kage in this vote because he recognized the thing in Kage’s eyes. He has one too.
  • “I’m the class rep!”
  • “He showed Alex down there that everything was going to be alright.” That’s Abby’s phrase again, in Lance’s mouth this time.
  • The vote. “Who’s in favor of getting this liar out of here?!” Only Rebento’s hand. “Who’s in favor of Kage staying?” Lance, Zulfiya, Orion, Ailani, Volkan, Cheyenne, Reina, and then Elias and Lucifer. “We hate him and don’t trust him, but we’re not gonna kick him out with some stupid popularity contest.”
  • “If that even is his real name.”
  • Rebento to Orion: “Big Vance got some big secrets along with Kage and Lucifer, huh?” He’s right and doesn’t know it.
  • Kage stops Ailani from yelling. She’s “tired of being reactionary,” even toward Kage.
  • Kage’s only thought: “Anubis took that deal..?” “His entire future was decided by a split second of breath.” And: “He was lying to a room of people who just agreed he was trustworthy.”
  • “Can you outrun explosions?”

What it’s doing. It’s the class’s verdict on Kage, and Kage’s verdict on Anubis.

What works. The vote is a terrific scene. It’s a moral test for the whole class, and the most surprising votes (Elias and Lucifer) are the most principled. Elias’s confession about the demon gives his entire character a new floor. And Kage’s guilt at the end is exactly right: the trust is the punishment.

What I’d push on. Rebento’s suspicion is correct, so he shouldn’t come off as a pure villain in this scene. He’s the only one asking the right question. Giving him one sympathetic beat (he’s scared, not just angry) would make the vote more interesting and set him up as a future ally or threat.

Score 9.5/10 The class decides, and Kage feels it.

4.4 Sweeter Than Sugar

1,341 words. The lobby; Orion vs. Rebento begins.

What happens. Orion asks why Kage didn’t defend him. Kage says, “We’re not friends,” and Orion gives him back the whistle: “You look like you need it.” Ailani asks if Kage and Abby were ever together. Lance asks about the healing. Kage dodges both and makes a wager with Lance: if Kage wins the tournament, Lance bows; if he loses, Kage wears Cheyenne’s hat on the podium. When he smiles, everyone sees his missing tooth, and Zulfiya explains that healing can’t regrow detached body parts. Cheyenne and Ailani ask if Kage and Lance kissed. The match begins, and Rebento’s knuckle-cracking hides a cherry gumball. He blasts Orion and names himself “Sugar Bomb.”

Details I caught.

  • Orion: “I defended you and you couldn’t do the same for me?” Kage, “whined out before laughing”: “Why would I do that? We’re not friends.” Orion: “You’re right. We’re not.”
  • The whistle returns: “I think I probably should’ve given that back to you sooner. You look like you need it.” He’s caring for Kage and hurt by him at once.
  • Ailani: “Were you and Abby ever…together?” “No! We were just friends!” “If that’s how you act around your ‘not friends’, you admitting to being friends with her shows a lot of closeness.” She’s right, and she’s a little jealous.
  • Lance: “Can you do it on others?” That’s a good question. If Kage can heal others, he could have saved Abby. He couldn’t, and he can’t.
  • “I’m gonna stand on that first place podium. I swear on everything.”
  • The wager. “If you end up on that top podium, I promise to bow before you.” “You have to wear Cheyenne’s hat and stand like her on the podium.” Kage already wore that hat in S3 Ch9.
  • “Your mouth got a hole bigger than a sandpit! Oh, bless your heart.”
  • Zulfiya’s rule: “Detached body parts can’t simply be ‘grown back’ through healing.… If you were to get decapitated…” “Arms, legs, teeth.” “You’d end up an amputee… a stump.” That’s an important limit on the biomass, and it makes Kage’s wrist-breaking strategy retroactively terrifying. He “grew apprehensive.”
  • She calls him “Prince” now.
  • Lance: “Hey, you got a look now! That’s good! I got nervous that it might’ve messed up your grin.”
  • Cheyenne and Ailani, together: “Did you guys kiss in that hotel room?” Kage, not blushing but angry this time: “ARE YOU GUYS GONNA MAKE THAT JOKE EVERY TIME ME AND HIM ARE HAVING A MOMENT?!” That’s the book poking at its own running joke. Rhatella’s shipping, Rebento’s “boyfriend,” and now this.
  • “Oil and water.” It’s apt, and Kage’s power is literally about mixing.
  • “You’re sweeter than sugar! That’s why I wanna drink you up.”
  • Reina’s sulking.
  • Orion: “Does intimidation like that actually work on people?”
  • Rebento: “Inazuma… at least he was respectful enough to try and beat you with his enhancement.” Orion: “Sounds like you’re just used to dirty.”
  • The cherry gumball, hidden in a cracking fist.
  • “You’re looking at future pro hero: Sugar Bomb!” That’s another hero name for the book’s growing list.

What it’s doing. It moves the relationships around before the next fight. Orion’s hurt, Ailani’s jealousy, and Kage and Lance’s closeness.

What works. The whistle moment is beautiful and painful. The tooth is an excellent physical consequence. It’s permanent, it’s visible, and it’s a rule the biomass can’t break.

What I’d push on. Kage’s “We’re not friends” here is cruel in a new way. Before, it was a mutual joke. Here, he’s hiding behind it after failing to defend someone who defended him. Give the reader one beat of Kage noticing he’s done that, even if he doesn’t fix it.

Score 8.5/10 A tooth and a whistle.

4.5 Running Off Those Calories

1,105 words. Arena: Orion vs. Rebento.

What happens. Rebento floods half the arena with charged gumballs. Orion throws loc rings from his dreads to set off a chain reaction, leaps through the colored smoke, and drags Rebento’s face along the floor until it bleeds. Rebento blows himself back into bounds with a jawbreaker, scatters sprinkle-bombs, then surrounds Orion with floating hard candies by telekinesis. Kage shouts “GET LOW!” Orion slides under the blast, appears behind Rebento, and wins. Orion credits Kage’s improvisation and tells Rebento he fights a lot like Kage.

Details I caught.

  • The gumball machines are on his belt. “Rainbow polka-dots.” “What good is running as a power if your feet can’t touch the ground?” That’s the right question for a speedster.
  • Orion’s loc rings as projectiles. That pays off his introduction in S1 Ch1.
  • A lime gumball makes a green explosion. The sugar flavors are a nice touch.
  • He comes “out of a yellow smoke cloud.”
  • Cheyenne: “Isn’t Orion going too hard? That move could deform Rebento!” Ailani: “That speed is like a light jog for Orion. If he wanted to tear Rebento’s face off, he wouldn’t be moving that slow.” Ailani knows Orion’s control intimately. They’ve trained together.
  • The jawbreaker against the dome is clever.
  • “I’m trying to win squeaky clean for you.” Orion takes Lance’s honor code as a constraint, or a taunt.
  • “The smell of burning caramel.” That’s a great sensory detail.
  • A sprinkle shotgun.
  • Rainbow swirl eyes are Rebento’s telekinesis tell. That’s a nice addition to the list: Kage’s hair, Ailani’s rotating sigil, and now Rebento’s eyes.
  • Kage: “ORION! HE’S SURROUNDED YOU WITH BOMBS! YOU GOTTA GET LOW!” He knows because it’s his own trick, the glue-orb barrage from S1 Ch15.
  • Orion’s baseball slide.
  • “Can you at least not do the face again?” “You got it.” That’s a funny, polite brutality.
  • “Me, Ailani, and Orion are the big three around here!”
  • “Oh, Orion knows why.”
  • “He improvised a plan for me to execute. Not bad.” That’s the combo from S1 Ch6, and it works best when they’re not fighting each other.
  • “For someone who really doesn’t like Kage, you sure do fight a lot like him. I’m shocked you guys aren’t friends.” “Not friends” again, from the person who keeps saying it about himself.

What it’s doing. It’s a clean, fun fight that proves the Kage–Orion partnership works when they’re on the same side.

What works. It’s colorful and quick. Every Rebento trick is a different candy. The “GET LOW” payoff is great, because Kage’s knowledge comes from his own history with Orion.

What I’d push on. The face-dragging is brutal for Orion, who prides himself on control. Ailani explains it as precise, and that’s fine. But it might be worth one line of Orion’s own thoughts about why he chose that. He’s been off since Inazuma. Is this him still angry?

Score 8.5/10 A sugar rush.

4.6 Curtain Call

1,591 words. The student lobby.

What happens. Ailani heads out early, wanting to be first onto the field for her match against Lance. She and Lance stare each other down, then both demand to know who Kage wants to win. Kage points at Orion: he wants to fight Orion in the grand final, maybe for the last time, because of the ban. Rebento comes back angry that Kage coached Orion, and Kage is cruel to him (“You’re getting lapped as is”). Rebento leaves to sit with Lucifer and Elias. Kage tells Lance that Anubis’s love is conditional and that he has to win to stay Anubis’s successor, and then realizes he’s said “successor” out loud. Everyone surrounds him. Ailani is called to the arena: “This is my time, and I’ve been dying to perform.”

Details I caught.

  • Ailani wants to be “the first one out there for my match.” She’s mirroring Kage running through the stadium doors.

  • The staredown.

  • “Who do you want to win, Kage?” in unison.

  • “You’re on the thinnest of thin ice.”

  • Lance: “I didn’t see you defending Kage when Rebento was going crazy.” That’s a low blow, and accurate. Kage stopped her.

  • Kage fidgets with the whistle and points his thumb at Orion. “YOU’RE HOPELESS!”

  • “I want to fight Orion because this might be the last time I ever get to fight him!” That’s the ban from S2 Ch2.

  • “It’s only been a little over a week since we last fought.” That’s correct.

  • “The ban was agreed upon between him and Anubis… Anubis has final say on it too.” “If that crowd is calling for me and Orion to go at it, who are we to deprive them?” Kage is now reasoning like Anubis: the crowd decides.

  • Lance: “Thespian at heart, and a tactical brain. You are one weird guy, Prince.”

  • Ailani: “Do not tell me that the whole ‘prince thing’ is catching on.”

  • “He’s like a walking contradiction.” Orion: “Kage makes no sense.”

  • Rebento: “your fucking lying friend told me you coached him.” Orion: “We’re not friends.” (again)

  • “That took me a year to get down.” Kage pulled off the same orb-swarm trick the very first time his telekinesis worked, in the middle of a fight, by accident.

  • Kage: “You’re the only one not buying, remember?” “You’re a choice for the Elite Program, I guess. It’s always good to have extra options.” “You wouldn’t even be a footnote right now. You’re getting lapped as is.” That’s the cruelest Kage gets in the book. It’s the biomass, or the win going to his head, or both.

  • “Lance, let me punch him.” “No. You’re the one being disrespectful first… but Kage, you should tone it down a little.”

  • “You’ve known me this whole time, and you just met Kage yesterday!” Rebento’s hurt is real.

  • “I’m going to sit with Lucifer and Elias. At least they have some sense!” That’s a new alliance: the three people who don’t trust Kage.

  • “I’m not trying to befriend everybody.” That’s a step back from “I should probably get used to working with others.”

  • “His love is conditional.” “I don’t deserve that love.”

  • Ailani: “You’ve done great… You don’t think that’s enough?”

  • “Should a number one hero ever be number two?”

  • “He was willing to gamble me away before. Why would he stop now?”

  • “I have to make his thoughts a reality if I want to keep any shot at being his successor.” Then he covers his mouth.

  • “Every person around him made him feel smaller.”

  • Lucifer and Elias give him dirty looks, but “neither of them was as surprised as everyone else.” They knew Anubis had picked him.

  • Lance: “Last night you said you wanted to be better than him.”

  • “Ailani tied her hair up.” It’s the classic gesture before a performance.

  • “Don’t you move a muscle. After I win, you’ve got a lot of answering to do.”

  • “This is my time, and I’ve been dying to perform.”

  • Lance: “That would mean you’re rooting for me, right?” and Ailani: “Wow, you’re going to root for him over me?” Both of them want to be the one Kage picks. S4 Ch7 opens with them agreeing to “show off so he picks one of us instead.” Their semi-final is a fight for Kage’s attention.

What it’s doing. It sets up Section 4’s real story: Ailani taking the stage, and Kage’s secrets starting to come out by accident.

What works. It’s a great cliffhanger chapter. The successor slip is the first crack in Kage’s secrecy that he causes himself. Ailani’s exit line is a perfect section-in-progress ending: it’s about performance (the dancer), timing (her turn), and the fact that she’s tired of being a side character in Kage’s drama.

What I’d push on. Kage’s cruelty to Rebento is the right escalation, but the book needs to make sure the reader sees it as a symptom, not just a mood. A one-line physical tell (goosebumps, his hand going to the whistle and not finding it calming) would tie it clearly to the biomass, the way the book did in S3 Ch6.

Score 9/10 A strong place to pause, and S4 Ch7 picks it up without a seam.

4.7 Saw Right Through You

1,747 words. Arena: Ailani vs. Lance. (New in this draft, and the current final chapter.)

What happens. In the arena before the bell, Lance fidgets with his hilt. Ailani asks him whether Kage acted strange during their hotel talk. Lance says Kage is strange, “but I wouldn’t say any stranger than most of us.” Ailani is amazed Kage made a friend overnight, “One he actually calls a friend!” They agree they’re Kage’s best friends, that he still picked Orion, his “not friend,” and that they should “show off so he picks one of us instead.” Lance apologizes for his jab about Rebento. Ailani: “You can make it up to me by losing.” Gene skips the countdown (“Screw the count! Just go!”).

The fight is almost wordless. Lance’s blades saw through Ailani’s crystals, and he splits her wall from the top down. She notices his blade can’t cut, only “hit, then saw,” and swarms him with small crystals so he can’t keep up. He switches to a claymore and bats them like baseballs, then throws a wakizashi as a feint and kicks her from behind. His eyes start glowing yellow. Her fingertips start to crystallize. She blocks his blade with her own crystal hand before it can saw, then punches him with hardened fists. He uses the hilt as a club. She catches it and pockets it. He pulls out a spare. His eyes go fully yellow: the limit he’d been avoiding is a state he calls King Arthur, a blind, fight-starved rage that sharpens his other senses and dulls his planning. Before it takes him completely, he reveals he can form blades without holding the hilt. The hilt in Ailani’s pocket grows an odachi that launches it into the air, he dismisses the blade before she can grab it, and he catches it and slams her with a falchion. She saves herself from a ring-out by forming a crystal floor above the grass and floating back. With one construct left and her hands nearly solid crystal, she swings a giant crystal hammer, not at Lance but at the floor in front of him. His foot slips into the crater, under the arena floor. That’s out of bounds, by the rule Geneki lost to. Ailani wins. Reina is the first to get it. Lance snaps out of King Arthur and laughs. Kage cheers, then sees that Orion looks unhappy. The second-half final is Orion against Ailani.

Details I caught.

  • The title has at least four meanings, and all of them work. Lance’s blade saws, and the chapter goes out of its way to call it “more like a cast saw.” Ailani saw right through the mechanic, the feint, and finally the floor. Lance goes blind, so in the end he can’t see anything at all. And Section 4 is named for the person who can see through Kage. Abby’s power card in S1 Ch1 said “she can see through anyone!” Abby is gone. Ailani is the one doing the seeing now, and the chapter ends with her one win away from facing him.
  • Lance “kept turning his hilt in his hand.” The hilt is the thing he fidgets with, the way Kage fidgets with the whistle.
  • “Just glad I made it this far. I’m counting my blessings.” Then: “Ailani had been counting beats her whole life.” The chapter is built on counting.
  • Ailani is still investigating: “Can you tell me how yours and Kage’s talk went last night? Did he act strange in any way?” She’s using Kage’s new friend as a source. Lance doesn’t notice he’s being questioned.
  • “One he actually calls a friend! I don’t know if you realize how high of an honor that is.” Kage has never called Orion a friend. He called Lance one within a day. That stings for Orion, and Ailani knows it.
  • “Duh! We’re his best friends. And yet, he still chose Orion. His ‘not friend.’” Ailani understands the Kage–Orion relationship better than either boy does.
  • “We should show off so he picks one of us instead.” This is new for the book. All tournament, Kage has wanted to be watched. Now two fighters are performing for his attention. Kage has become the audience.
  • Lance: “Sorry about the Rebento thing earlier. That was a low blow, and you know I’m against those.” He’s using the language of Orion’s literal low blow to apologize for a verbal one.
  • “It was. You can make it up to me by losing.” She’s joking. He does.
  • “Screw the count! Just go!” Cheyenne went early on “one.” Kage went early on “one.” Gene, who loves rules she makes up, removes the count entirely. That’s a nice background callback.
  • The first exchange: a cruciform sword “slammed against the crystal before sawing through it.” The saw rule is on the page in the first paragraph of the fight.
  • She raises a crystal wall. “Lance jumped, planted his blade on the top of it, and sawed down as he dropped, splitting the wall in half.” That’s a great image, and it uses gravity to do the sawing for him.
  • A stiletto in a “reverse grip to strike faster.” Lance knows his weapons.
  • “For every dodge Ailani managed, she took two hits.”
  • “The crowd was falling in love with the two competitors already, but Ailani and Lance didn’t bask. There weren’t even any words exchanged.” After a tournament that Inazuma complained was “nothing but talking,” this is the first fight with no talking at all. It feels different on the page, and that’s the point.
  • The read. “Lance couldn’t slice through them. His swings only hit, and he had to hold the blade against a crystal to saw through it. That meant he couldn’t instantly cut her crystals, since his blade was more like a cast saw. So, she kept up the numbers. Hit, then saw. Two counts to her one, and Ailani had been counting beats her whole life.” This is the best sentence in the chapter. The dancer wins the exchange because she hears the tempo of his weapon. Her dream (choreography) and her power finally work together. And “cast saw” is an exact, real comparison. A cast saw oscillates instead of spinning, so it cuts rigid plaster but only vibrates skin. That’s precisely Lance’s power card (“flesh doesn’t get cut! It moves with the blade”).
  • He switches to a gladius, then a claymore for reach, and “batted them like baseballs so they’d shatter.” Baseball comes up again (Orion’s wrong guess about Elias in S3 Ch4).
  • The feint. She drops her wall and sees a wakizashi in front of her, “and realized there was no Lance holding it.” He threw the sword and went around. Then he picks up the hilt and makes a cutlass.
  • “Lance’s eyes glowed yellow as he struck again and again. Ailani noticed. Was that a tell, like the one Lance had pointed out in Rebento’s eyes?” Every enhancement in this book has a tell (Kage’s hair, Ailani’s rotating sigil, Rebento’s rainbow eyes). Lance is the one who taught the room that Rebento’s eyes are a tell, in S4 Ch5. Now his own eyes give him away.
  • Her cost arrives on schedule. “Her fingertips were beginning to crystallize.” That’s the S4 Ch1 card: “Starting at the fingertips, the crystals slowly creep up toward the wrists.”
  • Her best defensive idea. “His blade needed time to saw through anything, so she put her half-crystallized hand in front of it. It stopped as it hit her hand, and before it could begin sawing, she kicked Lance away and got a few punches on his face with her hardened hands.” She turns her own cost into armor. That’s the kind of rule-based fighting the book does best.
  • “Lance’s hilt had no sword formed out of it, even though his hands looked fine.” That’s the S3 Ch9 card almost word for word: “a point where he decides that he needs to stop even when his hilt and hands are fine.” The reader who remembers the card knows something is coming.
  • “Words remained a luxury neither of them wanted to spend. Everyone in both lobbies seemed to feel the same.”
  • The hilt as a police baton. She catches it, crystal to the stomach (“Lance felt like he’d coughed up a lung”), uppercut, and she “slid the hilt upside down into her pocket to force Lance to fight without it.”
  • The spare hilt, “one Rhatella had made him,” forms a cinquedea. Rhatella makes spares. That’s in character for a girl who makes gear because “that’s the only way she feels close to them.” It also means she cared enough about Lance’s gear to make two, and he never really talked to her (S3 Ch13).
  • “Her hands were getting stiffer. The crystallization was crawling to her palms now.”
  • King Arthur. “Lance’s eyes were now completely yellow. The limit he’d been avoiding was something he called King Arthur. It was what he’d meant when he told everyone that sometimes, when fighting, you end up as someone else. When it took hold, he became so fight-starved that he was literally blinded by his desire for combat. He lost his sight, but his other senses sharpened. The problem was, his way of fighting got significantly less refined. He grew more anxious to strike first instead of planning.” See “What it’s doing” below. This is the biggest reveal in the chapter.
  • The hidden trick. “Everyone figured his blades could only form in his hand, but he didn’t need to be holding the hilts to form them at all.” The card said “in his hand.” It never said “only.” The hilt in her pocket grows an “oversized ceremonial odachi,” and “the sheer size of it sent the hilt skyrocketing out of her pocket.” She reaches for it, he dismisses the blade “at Lance’s command,” throws the small hilt at her outstretched arm, catches the big one, and makes a falchion. It’s a combination move with three hilts’ worth of logic in one sentence. It’s also a little hard to follow (see below).
  • The save. She’s flung toward the out-of-bounds “and only caught herself by forming a crystal floor just above the grass and hitting it hard. Her sigil turned ninety degrees as she floated the floor back to the arena.” The rotating sigil is her telekinesis tell from S2 Ch3. And the trick is Severine’s from S3 Ch2: standing on your own construct, technically above the grass, technically not out. Severine lost because she dropped the barrier. Ailani doesn’t drop hers.
  • “She was perceptive, though. When she looked at Lance’s face, she could see that he wanted to end the fight now. He’d fully fallen into King Arthur’s grasp.” A blind fighter who wants it over is predictable. A basket-hilted broadsword for the finish.
  • The hammer. “Ailani spent her last construct on a massive crystal hammer.” That’s the “war hammer” from her S4 Ch1 card. “Lance heard it coming and stepped back, sure he’d dodged it.” He’s blind, so he’s fighting by sound. “Ailani wasn’t aiming for Lance, though. She smashed the arena floor right in front of him instead, and it broke apart all the way to his feet. Lance’s foot slipped down into the crater.”
  • Gene: “As established by my wonderfully splendid daughter Geneki’s failure, if you’re touching under the arena floor, that’s out of bounds!” Two ring-out rules from Round 1, Severine’s and Geneki’s, and Ailani uses both in one fight.
  • “The crowd cheered tentatively, unsure how exactly she won.” The same confusion as Reina’s win over Geneki in S3 Ch2.
  • “Woah, that’s crazy smart!” Reina yelled. Reina is the one who gets it first, because she’s the one who won that way. It’s a lovely small payoff for a character the book has been sidelining since the bus.
  • Lance “snapped back to reality. His eyes went back to normal.” “Oh, man! I lost! That was awesome, Ailani! Sorry if I got a little carried away there at the end.” “A little carried away” is how Lance describes going blind with bloodlust. It’s the same minimizing move as Kage’s “I kinda blacked out the other night” (S2 Ch14). Both boys downplay the thing inside them.
  • Ailani: “When Geneki went out that way, I clocked it as a path to win. Gen’s my friend, after all, so I watched that replay way more times than she’d like.” Her win comes from loyalty and homework. Elias’s chapter was titled “Studying Is Important,” and Elias studied thirteen classmates. Ailani studied one replay of one friend’s worst moment, and it was enough.
  • “I’m just surprised no one else was paying attention to that.” That’s a quiet jab at the whole room, Kage included. Rebento said Kage “doesn’t know anything about anyone.”
  • Her hands are “still locked stiff at her sides.” She goes into her next match with damaged hands. Remember that.
  • “Kage cheered as it all sank in, but his joy slowed when he turned to Orion, who seemed unhappy with the result. Kage understood how Orion felt. It’s easier to fight people you don’t know that well, but Orion didn’t have a choice in the matter.” Kage has refused to fight Ailani since S2 Ch3 (“I don’t want to fight you”). Now Orion has to, and Kage sees it. Kage still doesn’t know about Orion’s crush, which makes this sentence truer than he knows.
  • The last line: “The second-half final was going to be Orion Vance versus Ailani Shoma.” The two people who have spent the whole tournament secretly investigating Kage now have to fight each other for the right to face him.

What it’s doing. Three things.

  1. It gives Ailani her showcase, and it’s worth the wait. Her first real fight in the present-day story comes 51 chapters after her introduction, and every move she makes comes from something the book planted: the card’s crystallization cost, the war hammer, the rotating sigil, the dance, Geneki’s loss, Severine’s loss. She doesn’t win on power. She wins on attention. That’s the right identity for the girl the section is named after, the person who notices things.

  2. It reveals that Lance has a monster too. This is the big one. The book now has three boys who carry something that takes over when they fight:

    What’s inside What it does What sets it off How they talk about it
    Lucifer Abaddon A ten-foot demon with its own voice Full sleep “Prisoner,” “virus”
    Kage Thanatos’s biomass Heals him and amplifies “what’s already there” Injury, a racing heart “Tar Healing” (a lie), “I kinda blacked out”
    Lance King Arthur Blinds him and makes him “fight-starved” Pushing past his limit “A little carried away”

    And look at the names. The boy who preaches honor, whose name is Lance and whose power is Avalon’s Legacy, carries a berserker inside him named after the king of Camelot. In S3 Ch10, Cheyenne joked, “If Lance can knight people, wouldn’t that make him a king?” Now we know there’s a king in him, and it can’t see. Kage wants to be King Kage. Lucifer wants to be number one. Lance has a king already, and he spends his whole life trying not to let it out. That reframes everything Lance said in the hotel room. “I try to hold people to the standard I didn’t hold to myself” isn’t only about his father. It’s about the thing that makes him fight dirty when he stops deciding to stop.

  3. It sets the second-half final as the most emotionally loaded match left: Orion, who likes Ailani and lied to her about Kage’s death, against Ailani, who’s been watching both of them. Whoever wins faces Kage.

What works. This is the best-choreographed fight in the book. The Elias fight is still the best-designed (the excretion rule is a better puzzle), but this one has the best flow. Every exchange causes the next one: the wall makes him jump, the swarm makes him switch to a claymore, the claymore’s swing creates shards she blocks with a wall, the wall blinds her, the blindness lets him feint. You could storyboard it. The choice to cut nearly all dialogue is exactly right for a chapter about two people who are both trying to stop talking and perform. And the ending works on two levels: it’s a clever rules win, and it’s a win only someone who cared about a friend’s embarrassing loss could have found.

What I’d push on.

  • The King Arthur paragraph is an info dump in the middle of the best rhythm in the book. The fight is in Ailani’s head, wordless and fast, and then a long paragraph stops to explain what King Arthur is, what it does, its drawback, and a hidden trick, from a narrator who suddenly knows Lance’s private name for it. Two fixes, and you could use both. First, keep the reader in Ailani’s head: she sees his eyes go fully yellow, his strikes get wilder, and his head turns toward sounds instead of movement. She figures out that he’s blind. That’s more exciting than being told. Second, let the name “King Arthur” come out after the fight, from Lance himself or from Cheyenne in the lobby. “I call it King Arthur” in his own embarrassed voice, right after “Sorry if I got a little carried away,” would be a terrific line.
  • Cheyenne needs one beat. Her boyfriend just went blind with bloodlust on live TV, and if anyone in the lobby knows what King Arthur is, it’s her. One cut to her face when his eyes go fully yellow (her hat coming down over her eyes, the way she hid during the Lucifer fight) would tell the reader this has happened before, and that it scares the people who love him.
  • The odachi sequence is a lot to track in one sentence. Hilt in pocket, blade forms, hilt launches, she reaches, blade vanishes, small hilt thrown at her arm, big hilt caught, falchion. It’s a great trick, but it reads in one breath. Break it into three or four short sentences, and give the reader the beat where Ailani’s fingers almost close on the hilt.
  • The weapon parade. Cruciform sword, stiletto, gladius, claymore, wakizashi, cutlass, cinquedea, odachi, falchion, basket-hilted broadsword: ten blade types in one fight. It’s fun, it’s knowledgeable, and some of the switches have a reason on the page (the claymore “for more reach,” the cinquedea because the spare hilt is “smaller”). The ones that don’t (falchion, broadsword) start to read like a list. Either give each one a reason in a few words, or let a couple of them go unnamed.
  • A small POV slip. “Lance felt like he’d coughed up a lung” is in Lance’s head, in a chapter told from Ailani’s. It’s tiny, but it’s next to the King Arthur paragraph, so the chapter’s camera wobbles twice in a row.
  • The adults are absent. That’s mostly a good choice, because it keeps the fight pure. But Anubis loves spectacle, Gene has a veto, and Huoyan teaches both of them. One line from the hero lounge, especially Anubis reacting to King Arthur (“THAT is a showstopper!”), would remind the reader that the people deciding these kids’ futures are watching a boy go blind and calling it content.

Score 9/10 Ailani’s showcase was worth the wait, and King Arthur changes how I read Lance. It loses a point only for explaining the reveal instead of letting Ailani discover it.

Section 4 so far

Score 9.1/10 (up from 8.9). With Chapter 7, Section 4 has a great interlude, the book’s best romance chapter, the class vote, a cliffhanger, and now its namesake’s first real fight, which turns out to be the best-choreographed fight in the book. The section’s job is to put Ailani at the center, and Chapter 7 finally does it. She wins by seeing what nobody else saw. The next chapter puts her against Orion, and the winner faces Kage. Everything the section has set up (Ailani’s squint, Orion’s secret, Kage’s “lot of answering to do”) is now pointed at the same two matches.

Character Dossiers: The Four Leads

Kage Ozul (“Sticky Situation,” “King,” “Prince”)

At a glance. Second-year hero student from Japan. Orphan, raised in foster homes. Arctic blue eyes (hidden on villain errands by yellow contacts, which happen to be Abby’s eye color), scarlet hair that drooped “lazy” until he cut it into a spiky, cartoonish, action-figure shape in S2 Ch10. Eyeliner since then. About seven inches shorter than Orion. A gap in his smile since S3 Ch15. Wears Abby’s gold whistle on a string around his neck. Kage is Japanese for “shadow,” which is a sharp name for a boy who spent his first year being invisible, and who later escapes a shadow-user by flying toward the moon.

Power: Adhesive Manipulation. He consumes the lipids (fats) in his body and excretes adhesives through his nails, both fingernails and toenails. He’s shown making double-sided tape, white glue, industrial tape, rubberized bouncy glue, cement and concrete, compressed explosive glue “mines,” lipid-dense extra-strength tape, fire-stop caulk, tape confetti, and a glue key cast.

Costs. No food means no fuel, which is why he eats cheese, drank oil once, and empties minibars. Hardening draws cold from his own body, and overuse causes fever: 102°F, then 105°F, then 110°F, which should be fatal. Telekinesis nearly killed him the first time, and since then it seems to work only when he’s certain of himself.

What the book quietly says about his power. It’s more than glue. The evidence: he “refused to explain” it. Rhatella calls him a “human insulator.” His hair stands on end when he uses telekinesis. His body “continued to shake and vibrate” after the first collapse. Huoyan says, “Something’s off,” and “I haven’t told Anubis everything.” Anubis’s notes say “stronger than originally thought.” Kage himself says, “maybe my DNA is made to handle that.” And he can do telekinesis even though his power behaves like a body-based “mutation,” which Huoyan implies usually can’t.

The biomass. Since S2 Ch7, part of Thanatos’s body is inside him. It regenerates broken bone, burned skin, and torn muscle in seconds, but it can’t regrow a part that’s been removed, like his tooth. It flares with injury and a high heart rate. It costs huge amounts of energy. Thanatos’s warning, in this draft, is that it will “amplify what’s already there. The things you try to suppress and hide will be difficult to hold back now.” (The last draft said “your traits, especially the negative ones.” The new wording matters: it doesn’t make him worse. It makes him more.) Elias has now felt it from the outside: “Something in you was pushing against my enhancement while you sat perfectly still.”

Arc so far.

  1. The ghost (Prologue). Lazy by choice. “Staying small and safe.”
  2. The penitent (Section 1). Trains until he bleeds, picks fights, loses, and gets back up. Wins once, against Orion.
  3. The recruit (S1 Ch12–14). Shakes a villain’s hand because a villain was the first adult to say what he wanted to hear.
  4. The broken (Section 2). Chooses Thanatos over the whistle. Watches Abby die. Is taken apart and rebuilt.
  5. The ghost again (S2 Ch10–12). Blows a silent whistle for days. Gets a new face. Kidnaps the top student on a villain’s errand.
  6. The performer (Sections 3–4). Returns as a crowd favorite and wins three matches. Lies more easily each time. Gets crueler. And in the middle of it, sticks his tongue out at a scared kid.
  7. The audience (S4 Ch7). For the first time, other people perform for him. Ailani and Lance fight partly so “he picks one of us instead.” He’s in the grand final, waiting to see who comes for him.

What makes him work. Kage is funny, which is rarer and harder than people think. His jokes (“I almost didn’t…stick…that landing,” “H.O.T.,” “Sticky Man”) are bad on purpose, in character, and they show a boy who uses humor to manage pain. He’s clever in a way the fights prove rather than state. And he’s genuinely good underneath: the hero-name speech, carrying Orion to the nurse, twisting in midair to protect Orion’s legs, putting the claymore back. The book is making the reader love him right as it starts making him worse. That’s the right order.

His core contradiction. He wants to save people and he wants to be watched. The first instinct came from the store. The second came from being an orphan nobody ever cheered for. Anubis feeds the second and calls it heroism. Thanatos feeds the second and calls it evolution. Only Lance, and in her own way Abby, speak to the first.

Key lines.

  • “Every time I save someone, I want to tell them my name and make them giggle.”
  • “People will call me King Kage one day. That’s not arrogance, by the way. It’s a spoiler.”
  • “Somebody paid for my invisibility with their life.”
  • “You can’t break…what’s already broken!”
  • “I am really bad at saving people. Everyone I’ve tried to save, it never ended well for them.”
  • “Should a number one hero ever be number two?”

Relationships.

  • Orion: rival, “not friends,” the person he understands best in a fight. Kage named Orion’s hero name. Neither knows the other’s secret from the dome.
  • Abby: the friend he didn’t know he had until she died for him. Her whistle is his anchor. When it’s off his neck, he loses control.
  • Ailani: the person who sharpens him. He loves her. He keeps hurting her. She’s been squinting at him since S3 Ch1, and she’s now one win away from facing him.
  • Lance: his moral counterweight, and his newest real friend, the one “he actually calls a friend.” After S4 Ch7, also his mirror: Lance has something inside him too (King Arthur), and he’s learned to stop before it takes over. He may be the person who can teach Kage to do the same.
  • Thanatos: the man who saved him, owns him, and lied to him.
  • Frostbite: the man he bows to. His hand curls around nothing when Frostbite is near.
  • Anubis: the man whose approval he wants and whose love he now knows is conditional.
  • Huoyan: the teacher who actually worries about him, and whom he doesn’t fully see yet.
  • Lucifer: his mirror. Two boys with monsters inside them, both chasing Anubis.

What I’d watch. Two risks. First, the biomass could become an excuse. If every bad thing Kage does is blamed on the mass, he stops being responsible for anything. The book handles this well so far: the cruelty is his own, amplified. This draft made that explicit by rewriting Thanatos’s warning to “amplify what’s already there.” Keep it that way. Second, the “number one” hunger needs a cost that isn’t physical. He’s lost a tooth. He should eventually lose something he can’t regenerate in a relationship.

Prediction. The book is heading toward a moment where Kage has to choose between being watched and saving someone, publicly, with the biomass pushing him toward the wrong answer. I think the whistle will be in his hand when it happens. Nearer term: whoever wins Orion vs. Ailani, the grand final will be a fight against someone who knows something about him that the crowd doesn’t.

Rating 10/10 as a protagonist. He’s the reason the book works.

Orion Vance (“Hermes,” “Mercury,” “Speed-Limiter’s” rival)

At a glance. Tall, dark-skinned, dreadlocks, covered in gold (rings, loc rings, lip piercings, earrings, necklaces, bracelets). Amber-gold eyes. Seven inches taller than Kage, one inch taller than Lucifer. Enjoys slow walks as a “guilty pleasure.” Says “I am Greek, Kage, not Roman,” which could be a heritage hint or just a joke about the gods. His hero name, which he chose to keep, is Hermes, and Kage gave it to him.

Power: Celestial Velocity. Gold and violet comet light. Officially “up to two hundred miles per hour,” broken in S2 Ch6 when he hit 250 (the last draft said 300). Superhuman reflexes, wall-running, and “perception and timing.” Techniques: Aether Step (a burst), Divine Relay (stored kinetic energy dumped into a kick), and Zephyr Mode (full output, painful to re-flare). With a passenger, about 100 mph. Costs: overheating, joint strain, and “a single misstep could tear him apart.” His power also stretches time for him emotionally. When he tells Ailani that Kage is dead, “His enhancement stretched every millisecond of her silence into an eternity, and for once, he couldn’t outrun a single one.”

Arc so far. Orion starts as the calm, kind, competent one. He picks up the boy at the punching bag, befriends the lonely inventor, and says “perception and timing.” Then the book breaks him in three steps:

  1. The dome (S2 Ch7). Given the choice between his life and Kage’s, he chooses his own at the count of three. In this draft, the narration takes away his excuse: he can hear that Kage wants to help, “But a small, ugly part of Orion didn’t care, and it already knew what it wanted to say.” Then he runs: “He had made his choice at three. Now he was making it again.”
  2. The lie (S2 Ch8 onward). He tells Ailani that Kage died. He doesn’t tell anyone about Abby. He hides that Kage was working with Shifting Horror. And he admits to himself that “a part of him prayed for this.”
  3. The anger (S3 Ch5–6, S4 Ch5). Inazuma’s racism and “heroes don’t run from danger” crack him. He wins by a low blow, apologizes for it, resents apologizing, and drags Rebento’s face across the floor.

Through all of it, he keeps Kage’s whistle safe and raises it for him when no one else does.

What makes him work. He’s the book’s best example of a good person who did a bad thing and is now living inside it. Orion never becomes a villain. He becomes someone with a secret, which in this book is the more common kind of corruption. His “We’re not friends” is a defense mechanism, and the book shows it in his body: the blush near Ailani, the punching bag “like a boxer aiming for someone’s vitals,” the smile “he desperately didn’t want.”

Key lines.

  • “Speed isn’t just running straight. It’s perception and timing.”
  • “You improvise. I execute. That’s our combo.”
  • “That’s enough counting! I made my choice. Kage, not me.”
  • “Why’d you have to go and die on me?! You piece of shit!”
  • “I was taught never to speak ill of the dead.”
  • “Your perception of me is off… And, your timing is predictable.”
  • “I’m pro hero…Hermes.”

Relationships.

  • Kage: rival, “not friends,” the person who brings out his competitiveness. He chose to let Kage be broken. Now he’s protecting Kage’s secret partly to protect his own.
  • Ailani: his crush (the blush in S2 Ch6, S2 Ch13, S3 Ch12). He’s comforting her over a boy who isn’t dead, while falling for her. She’s now suspicious of him too (“For a guy with no plan, he sounded weirdly relieved about it”), and after S4 Ch7 he has to fight her.
  • Rhatella: his first real friend in the book. He hasn’t been shown with her since Section 1, which is a thread to pick back up.
  • Abby: guilt. “He didn’t take the time to get to know her.”
  • Inazuma: the person who found his wound and pressed on it.

What I’d watch. This draft gives Orion more interior lines in Section 3 (his unspoken confession in S3 Ch1, “He’d turned one baseball into a whole game plan,” “he couldn’t tell if he meant a word of it”). But he’s still never been alone on the page with what he did in the dome since Kage came back. His match against Ailani is the natural place for it. He has to fight the girl he lied to, about the boy he let die.

Prediction. Nerezza, Rebento, or Kage himself will expose the dome choice, and the confession will come at the worst possible time. With Ailani now his next opponent, the likeliest moment is during or right after that match. And I think Orion will forgive himself before Kage forgives him.

Rating 9.5/10 The book’s quiet second protagonist.

Abby Neosame

At a glance. Small, pale, blonde, with yellow eyes and a twin-layered halo. Wings she rarely shows (Kage never saw them until she died). Nearly silent steps. A dolphin-shaped backpack. Worked at a maid café. Finished her homework in the morning. Believes “God chose me to be the one who makes sure everybody’s okay.”

Power: Eyes of Divinity. She heals with yellow light and sees through people, including injuries, temperature, and things like “something is zapping your energy from inside!” The scanning can’t be turned off, so she sees everyone’s pain all the time. Limits: the patient has to be alive, it doesn’t work on herself, and serious wounds need speed. Ultimate technique: Divine Judgment, an explosion of her own body that strikes enemies and herself. Her eyes glow “a deep yellow” before it fires. (Lance’s eyes glow yellow before King Arthur takes him, and Kage wears yellow contacts for villain work. Yellow eyes in this book mean someone has gone past a limit.)

Arc. Abby has the shortest arc and the biggest impact. She approaches Lucifer in her second week of school to help him, and gives him the distance he asks for. She meets Kage at the punching bag. Watching him rise again and again, she decides to be his “number one supporter.” She gives him her dead brother’s whistle. She’s thrown out of Lucifer’s doorway and goes to help Kage instead. She dies on the street, using her last breath to tell Kage that everything is going to be alright.

Her wound. Her brother was “incredibly sick.” She was “too young for doctors to trust her.” She was told to wait, she waited, and he died. “She sat by his bed every night with her hands folded to pray, when she could have laid them on him instead.” “But she never tried, and that had been killing her ever since.” That’s why she won’t wait for Lucifer, and why she walks the city from sundown looking for two boys who didn’t ask for her: “She had waited for her brother. She wasn’t going to wait for a whistle.”

What makes her work. Abby is goodness without being naive. She reads Lucifer correctly, respects his boundary, and is sad without being pathetic. And her death is designed around her own rules: she can see every wound, she freezes at the sight of pain, and she can’t heal herself. Her power kills her in a way only her power could.

Key lines.

  • “It’s okay, Kage. Everything is going to be alright.”
  • “Nurses and doctors have patient confidentiality!”
  • “Is helping others not a desire?”
  • “I’m happy to not be your friend, Lucifer.”
  • “Kage…I will be your number one supporter…”
  • “I will always be your number one supporter, Kage.”

Relationships.

  • Kage: the person she chose to support, possibly with a crush (Huoyan suspects one; Ailani asks). He felt it too late.
